# Changelog

## Unreleased

- Removed (the junk-check cleanup, owner 2026-11-21: "mấy cái check này làm gì? sao lại check vô case đặc thù thế làm nặng lint? trò check cái gì hợp lý k dc à!!!!"): 41 hfs finding codes and 72 dead runtime codes that guarded one-off cases, the rules-cited and check-premise gates and ANATOMY_RULE_NO_ENFORCER. CONTRIB-7 and CONTRIB-10 are rewritten: a check is added only for a broad recurring class a machine can decide, and feedback closes as a root fix or declined (`starci ticket close <id> --kind feedback --target-kind root-fix|declined`). Old codes stop without an alias; the mappings are in the release notes.
- Added (`starci app codemod`, `codemod/grammar.mts`): the Grammar's codemods, one per Grammar release that needs one and named after its version (owner 2026-11-21: "starci app codemod v0540 đi pattern thế 1 codemod đi kèm 1 bản grammars"). `starci app codemod --list` prints each codemod of the installed Grammar with its version and summary. `starci app codemod v0540` runs one codemod: it is dry until `--execute` and exits 1 when it leaves a `TODO(type-role)` case. `starci app codemod --from <installed> [--to <target>]` runs every codemod in between, in version order. The codemods load from the app's installed `@starci/grammar/codemods` (0.54.0 and later) and use the app's TypeScript compiler. A target above the installed Grammar, an unknown id and a Grammar with no codemods are refused, each with what to do.
- Changed (canon pins): the Grammar family is 0.54.0 (`~0.54.0`).
- Changed (owner 2026-11-21, one fact per code): the eight README codes HFS_README_BADGE_NOT_LIVE, HFS_README_DESCRIPTION_INVALID, HFS_README_DEVELOPMENT_INCOMPLETE, HFS_README_PRIVATE_URL, HFS_README_SECTION_MISSING, HFS_README_SECTION_ORDER, HFS_README_TITLE_INVALID and HFS_README_WORK_POINTER_MISSING are one code, HFS_README_INVALID. Detection is unchanged and every finding keeps its message (it names the title, section, command, URL or badge at fault). There is no code alias: a record or note that cites an old code reads the new one.
- Changed (R375): the six seed codes HFS_SEEDS_SHAPE, HFS_SEED_MANIFEST, HFS_SEED_DDL, HFS_SEED_DATA_SCHEMA, HFS_SEED_TEST_DATA and HFS_SEED_SECRET_REF are one code, HFS_SEEDS_SHAPE; the message names the file, the line and which seed law it breaks. Detection is unchanged. HFS_SEED_PII (database-config, R259) is a different rule and keeps its code. No code alias exists: HFS_SEED_MANIFEST, HFS_SEED_DDL, HFS_SEED_DATA_SCHEMA, HFS_SEED_TEST_DATA and HFS_SEED_SECRET_REF now read HFS_SEEDS_SHAPE.
- Changed (data family, R400, R426, R427, R434): HFS_DATA_DEPENDENCY is HFS_DATA_IN_CODE; HFS_DATA_UNSEALED, HFS_DATA_STANDARD_SEALED, HFS_DATA_MANIFEST_DRIFT, HFS_DATA_FILE_SIZE and HFS_DATA_CAP are HFS_DATA_COPY_INVALID; HFS_DATA_ADAPTER_UNGUARDED is HFS_DATA_ADAPTER_DRIFT; DATA_STORE_UNDECLARED, DATA_STORE_KIND, DATA_STORE_ADAPTER and DATA_DERIVED_SOURCE are DATA_STORE_INVALID; DATA_PURPOSE, DATA_DRIVER_KIND, DATA_SEED_UNPINNED, DATA_FORCE_MISPLACED, DATA_BACKUP_LOADED and DATA_EXTENSION_UNDECLARED are DATA_SET_INVALID. DATA_COPY_NAME stays: the CLI refuses a seed-standard name with the same code. Detection is unchanged and each message still names the file, key and value at fault.
- Removed (R435, owner approved): HFS_DATA_SECOND_LOADER and its finder `rules/data-loaders.mts`, a list of regexes for ways around the one seed path. The convention stays in contexts/project/stacks.md PROJECT-STACKS-13 as guidance; the loader of the seed step (R435: STACK_SEED_STANDARD_UNREADABLE, STACK_SEED_STANDARD_SCHEMA, STACK_SEED_STORE_COMMAND, STACK_SEED_DOOR_REFUSED) is unchanged and is now the rule's first code.
- Changed (R437): HFS_BRAND_MISSING, HFS_BRAND_PROMPT_STALE, HFS_BRAND_PROMPT_INCOMPLETE and HFS_BRAND_BASE_UNKNOWN are one code, HFS_BRAND_PROMPT_INVALID. All four were `localOnly` (a warning in `--env local`, an error in `--env prod`) and still are; HFS_BRAND_INVALID (the sheet's own shape, always an error) stays apart because its severity differs. The newer FE_BRAND_*, FE_GLOBALS_* and FE_STATE_PAGE_* codes are untouched.
- Changed (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNWARMED`, `cache/freshness.mts`): the finding says a whole run wrote cache records its commit does not hold; it was the only error in 172 of 286 red runs and never a defect. It is now `info` (exit code unaffected) in every run, and an error only in the run of the managed pre-push hook, which exports `STARCI_PUSH_GATE=1` (the one place a cold cache costs CI time). `starci app push` and the lander commit the records before that hook runs. `_FORGED`, `_STALE`, `_MISSING`, `_UNPORTABLE`, `_FILE_TOO_LARGE` are unchanged. Apps: refresh the managed `.husky/pre-push` (`starci sync`) so the hook sets the variable; until then a plain `git push` of an uncommitted cache is not refused (CI recomputes it).
- Changed (`starci` CLI): `lane land`/`lane gate` no longer discard the finding, `app push` no longer filters it; `app push` still loops (a record can move once its own commit changes HEAD).

## 6.17.5 - 2026-11-21

- Fixed (ci.yml): a switched-off gate never skips the release deploy; each deploy job runs on its own status check and names the results it needs, and the verdict summary says whether a deploy runs or why not.

- Added (images.yml, `sync/stack-images.mts`): when the release environment is declared `custody: ci-key`, the `image-sealed` job binds no Environment and reads the repository secret `SOPS_AGE_KEY` (the CI key), behind the release-only guard it already had; ci.yml's images call and each `deploy-<env>` call of a ci-key environment pass `secrets: inherit`, the only way a called workflow whose job binds no Environment reads a repository secret (without it the automatic deploy of a ci-key environment read an empty key). R251 accepts the unbound sealed job only in images.yml, with the release-tag guard and the tag-is-version check before any secret, for a `--env` declared ci-key, and accepts `secrets: inherit` only on a ci.yml caller of stack-deploy.yml whose environment is ci-key. Apps on environment custody render byte-identically.
- Changed (docs only, `app secret server-key --restore github`): legacy. It serves only an old GitHub Environment backup, which nothing writes any more; the help, README and contexts/project/stacks.md say so. No behaviour change.

- Added (`environments.<env>.deploy.custody: environment | ci-key`, default environment; `application-stacks.schema.yaml`, `stack-deploy-config.mts`, `sync/stack-images.mts`): for a private repository on a plan without Environments (GitHub Free). With one environment in ci-key custody, stack-deploy.yml's dispatch input `environment` is a `choice` of the declared environments, the deploy and rollback jobs bind `environment: ${{ (inputs.environment != '<ci-key env>' ...) && inputs.environment || '' }}` (no Environment for a ci-key name, so they read the repository secret `SOPS_AGE_KEY`, the app's own CI key), and the automatic-deploy pause reads the repository variable `STARCI_DEPLOY_PAUSED_<ENV>`. The release-tag steps are unchanged. Any workflow on any ref can read a repository secret: the guard is the workflow allow-list, those steps and R251, the exposure the tag deploy already has with the CI key. `custody: ci-key` with `approval: reviewers` is refused (`deploy.custody`). With every environment in environment custody the render is byte-identical. Docs: contexts/project/stacks.md PROJECT-STACKS-7.
- Changed (R251, `rules/deploy-workflow.mts`): a job whose binding is that ci-key expression is accepted only in stack-deploy.yml, with the release-tag guard and the tag-is-version check before any secret is read, for names the declaration gives `custody: ci-key`; otherwise HFS_DEPLOY_WORKFLOW names why.
- Removed (`app secret server-key --backup github`, `stack/server-key.mts`): the server key is never copied into a repository. `starci app secret` refuses `--backup` with that reason (exit 2); its break-glass backup lives only in the harness repository's break-glass environment. `streamIdentityCommand` and the `ghCommand` dependency are gone. `--restore github` stays; its note about a repository-level `SOPS_AGE_KEY` no longer asks to delete it (it may be the CI key).


- Added (`deploy.images.<service>.runtimePublicEnv`, owner 2026-11-21: "1 ảnh chỉnh theo môi trường chứ?"): one web image per sha, configured per environment at runtime. An image that declares `runtimePublicEnv: true` is built without the NEXT_PUBLIC_* keys of its `buildArgs` (literal and sealed): `starci app stack build-args` and `stack build` pass only the other keys, and images.yml builds it in the public `image` job, with no GitHub Environment and no secret when no other key is left, pushed under `sha-<sha>` on every pushed run. The render (`stack render` / `stack deploy`) sets those keys as the service's runtime environment (sealed ones opened from their member; `deploy.environment` wins for a key both name; a changed value changes `configDigest`). The app reads them through `createPublicEnv` of `@starci/next-data` (`window.__ENV`). Without the declaration everything renders as before. Shared split: `buildTimeArgs` / `runtimePublicArgs` (`runtime/scripts/hfs/stack-deploy-config.mts`). Docs: contexts/project/stacks.md PROJECT-STACKS-22, cicd.md. Spec: `src/stack-runtime-public-env.spec.mts`.

- Added (`starci app stack`, PROJECT-STACKS-6): a native process may declare `serve: {build: {argv, cwd?}, argv, cwd?}`. `stack up` then builds it and runs the built artifact (next start, node dist/...), never a watcher. Its own `argv` (next dev, ts-node-dev) runs only with `--watch`, the opt-in for live coding. A process without `serve` runs as before.
- Added: `starci app stack restart --env <env> --process <name>[,<name>] [--watch] [--execute]`. It ends only the named processes, rebuilds and starts them, and waits for their health on the loopback and over each dev tunnel hostname that routes their port. The compose services, volumes, custody and tunnel stay up. An environment with no running process is STACK_LOCAL_NOT_UP.
- Changed (the app skeleton's `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml`): the api and web processes declare `serve` (`npm run build:be` then `node dist/apps/api/src/main.js`; `npm run build:fe` then `npm run start:web`), and the web gets `PORT`.

- Added (`application-stacks.schema.yaml` `$defs.environment.hostServices`): the host services an environment needs (`needs: {starci.embed: [local, sv-hk-1]}`, an optional landing `machine`), judged by the starci CLI against the owner host's config.yaml (contexts/harness/host.md "What an app needs"); the render ignores it. Spec: `src/hfs-stacks-host-services-schema.spec.mts`.
- Changed: the shared embeddings opt-in describes `qwen3-embedding:4b` at 2560 dimensions.

## 6.17.4 - 2026-11-21

- Fixed (`starci app check`, the build and audit parity families): the run asked the failure-code catalog for CI_PARITY_STEP_FAILED only, so a host without Docker, whose database step is the warning CI_PARITY_DB_SKIPPED, ended in HFS_MANIFEST_INVALID ("has no failure-code catalog entry loaded"). It now asks for every code the parity steps reported. Found by StarCi Next.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.17.4.

## 6.17.3 - 2026-11-21

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): `@starci/grammar` 0.52.2 (Expandable, the Skeleton box shapes), and the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.17.3.

## 6.17.2 - 2026-11-21

- Changed (the UI Audit gate, PROJECT-GALLERY-8; owner, 2026-11-21, the ruling that wins: "lần đầu ghi screen.yaml thì phải audit đến pass ghi bằng chứng. sau này sửa code trang nào hay k ưng trang nào chỉ đối chiếu với screen.yaml -> và có thể lan rộng ra để sửa hàng loạt nhưng lúc đó đủ luận điểm phản biện với thầy"): a touched screen is judged only until its audit first passes (2+ rounds, no lens open in the last, an auditor who is not the builder). That pass is its baseline: `audit.baseline` `{ at, rounds }`, written by `starci audit run` and kept by every later cycle. After it, UI_AUDIT_STALE (shots or code drifted), open lenses and later cycles are never findings. A screen with no audit is still UI_AUDIT_MISSING.
- Changed (HFS_APP_GALLERY_SCREEN_INVALID, R395): the audit block's shape is judged here, for every screen, with `baseline`. UI_AUDIT_INVALID is gone, and the round count is no longer part of the shape.
- Added (`starci app uiaudit cycle <area>/<screen> --reason <why>`, reached as `starci audit cycle`): closes the screen's audit cycle into `considered` (one chosen entry: the reason, the rounds and their dates, the last verdict of each lens, builder and auditor; the rounds stay in git history) and starts an empty audit, so the next `audit run` prepares round 1. The lawful way back after round 4: a first cycle that never passed stays the one to pass (UI_AUDIT_NEEDS_OWNER names the verb), and a baseline is kept.
- Fixed (`starci audit run` past a cycle's round 4): it records nothing. Before, it re-recorded the shot sha256, so a reshot image inherited round 4's verdicts.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.17.2.

## 6.17.1 - 2026-11-21

- Fixed (UI audit reach, `cache/uiaudit-family.mts`): the reach reads the head of a source or an i18n catalog it compares with the base whatever its size. The rule read budget (`readText`, 1 MiB) read a larger catalog (NIVO's vi.json, 1.15 MB) as absent, so every key counted as changed and one edited line marked every screen that reads the catalog (site/blog-article on every NIVO lane, whose audit is at round 4 and cannot be re-run). Spec: `src/ui-audit.spec.mts`.
- Fixed (`landingBase`, `lint/ratchet.mts`): a lane with no commit of its own yet (HEAD on origin/dev's tip) is judged from HEAD, its work tree alone; before, it fell through to the merge-base with origin/main and judged all of dev's history since main as the lane's own. Spec: `src/landing-base.spec.mts`.
- Added (`app secret server-key --backup github`, `stack/server-key.mts`): a GitHub Environment whose deployment refs are the tag policy `v*` is accepted when an active tag ruleset covering `refs/tags/v*` (or `~ALL` with no exclusion) forbids creating, updating and deleting those tags; the refusal names the missing ruleset. Reviewers and main-only stay accepted (stack-deploy.yml deploys only `v*` tags, so such an Environment cannot be limited to main).
- Added (R451, `lib/sops-recipients.mts`, owner 2026-11-21 "one key per project"): an app that deploys from CI may hold its own age key, public half declared under `apps.<project>.ci` of `ops/vps/recipients.yaml` (and the shipped `knowledge/hfs/sops-recipients.yaml`), private half only in that repository's Actions secret SOPS_AGE_KEY. The token `ci` in an environment's set resolves to the judged app's own key and to nothing for an app without one; a CI key two apps share, or one that is also the harness, a server or a backup key, is refused; another project's CI key is named as such. `app secret init` and the stacks gate resolve the same set.
- Added (`stack connect`, CI): the vps-ci member's optional VPS_PORT becomes the Port line of the ssh block, checked like the owner transport. ENV_SEALED_UNREAD knows the vps-ci transport keys (VPS_SSH_KEY, VPS_KNOWN_HOSTS, VPS_GATE), which `stack connect` reads and no app does.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/sops-recipients.yaml`): the sv-hk-1 backup recipient and the nivoos-lite CI recipient, public halves only.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.17.1.

## 6.17.0 - 2026-11-21

- Changed (UI audit reach, `cache/screen-reach.mts`): a pure re-export barrel is transparent. An import of it reaches only the modules exporting the names it takes (named, `export *` when a name is not listed, namespace and default imports take all of it), and the barrel itself is no reached file, so a change behind a barrel no longer marks every importer.
- Added (slot manifest): the optional slot field `renders: false`, data in `knowledge/hfs/slots.yaml`, for the slots whose modules cannot change what a screen renders: `fe.hooks.swr`, `fe.hooks.socketio`, `fe.modules.api`, `fe.transport.*`, `fe.modules.config`, `fe.modules.auth`, `fe.package.api*`. The UI audit reach walks through their files but counts no change to them. Identified by the slot of the file, never by path.
- Changed (UI audit reach, `cache/symbol-diff.mts`): a reached module that is no barrel marks a screen only when a name the screen takes from it changed since the base. The exported symbol's printed declaration (comments and formatting left out) with the top-level declarations and import bindings it uses is compared between base and head per symbol. A side-effect, default or namespace import, a module with `export *`, an unparsable file and a `.json` catalog count whole; a file the base did not have adds nothing. The walk keeps its MAX_REACH cap.
- Changed (UI audit reach, `cache/catalog-reach.mts`): an i18n catalog counts only for a screen whose reached sources read a key that changed, was added or was removed. The key usage is the i18n canon's own (`architecture/i18n-keys.mts` now exports `i18nUsageOf`, `patternsOfSource`, `tailMatches`, `leavesOf`): a source with a translator reads the full keys of its calls and, for a call whose key is a template or a variable, its whole namespace; a source without one is read by the loose key patterns. A catalog or source that does not parse counts.
- Changed (check counts): `counts.error` stays every error finding; `counts.blocking` is the ones in the verdict, `counts.reportOnly` the ones a pending cutover, the local registry or a report profile keeps out of it, and each `counts.byCode` entry splits `blocking` and `reported`. The human summary line reads "N blocking error finding(s), M more reported, not blocking (...)".
- Changed (`UI_AUDIT_STALE`): the message names the first changed file that reaches the screen and the count of the others.
- Added (PROJECT-GALLERY-6 item 9, `rules/gallery-design.mts`): `pending: { reason }` on a `design.json` page (and on a `board.yaml` section, `rules/gallery-board.mts`), for a screen that is approved but not buildable yet because it waits on data or an API and lives on a query state of a route the code does change. HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_CHANGE_UNAPPROVED and the UI Audit (`cache/uiaudit-family.mts`) skip it; the reason must be non-empty and only a `drafted` or `approved` page may carry it (else HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_MANIFEST). The new info finding HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_PENDING lists every pending screen with its reason in each run and nudges a pending screen that has a shot in the work tree to drop the mark; a board section with a `pending` reason is exempt from HFS_APP_GALLERY_BOARD_SECTION_UNMAPPED. Spec: `src/app-gallery-pending.spec.mts`.
- Fixed (a lane's check and the batch lander's gate judged different diffs): the gallery coupling (HFS_APP_GALLERY_EXPLAIN_STALE, HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_CHANGE_UNAPPROVED) and the UI Audit took the lint ratchet's base, `origin/<branch>` else `HEAD^`. In a lane that is pushed `origin/<lane>` equals `HEAD`, so the base fell to `HEAD^` (often a cache commit) and the lane's own `app check --env prod` judged nothing; the lander's gate (a detached merge of the lane into origin/dev) judged the lane's whole diff and raised the findings. The new `landingBase` (`lint/ratchet.mts`) is, off `main` and `dev`, the merge-base of HEAD with STARCI_BASE_REF, else origin/dev, else origin/main, and the run prints it (`gallery coupling: judged N file(s) changed since <sha>, <why>`). The lint ratchet keeps its own base.
- Fixed (`stack db-upgrade`, PROJECT-STACKS-19): a declared login role the SOURCE cluster does not hold yet (an app role a migration of the release creates) is no longer refused as STACK_DB_UPGRADE_PASSWORD_DRIFT. Its proof is skipped with the report line "login <role>: ... is not on the source cluster; migrate creates it and gives it its sealed password (not proven here)" and the record lists it in `absentLogins`. A role the source holds must still log in with its sealed password, or the upgrade refuses as before.
- Changed (`stack deploy --adopt`, adopt.mts): after the expand migrations the adopt step gives the declared app role (hfs.json `db.appRole`, `database.appPassword`) its sealed password, the step a first start already takes, so a role the release's migration just created is never left without one (STACK_FIRST_START_APP_ROLE when it fails). The dev path already does this in the migrate step of `stack up`.
- Fixed (`db adopt` compare across server majors): a database restored from an older server (PG16 into PG17) holds its CHECK constraints re-rendered by the newer major (casts, `= ANY (ARRAY[...])` forms), so a replay on another major differed in 13 CHECKs with the same allowed values. Adopt now reads the live `server_version_num`, checks the major of the scratch replay (the app's own db image, else the one named by `--image`) against it and, when they differ, replays on the pinned postgres image of the live major (the managed db image's base for 17, the previous majors `db-upgrade` pins, `PREVIOUS_MAJOR_IMAGES`), so both sides are rendered by the same server version. The output says which (`compare  definitions rendered by the same server major (PostgreSQL N) on both sides`). Only when no image of the live major is pinned, or the pinned plain image cannot replay the baseline (an extension only the app's db image holds), the compare falls back to a textual normalisation of the definitions (casts, `= ANY (ARRAY[...])` written `IN (...)`, parentheses, layout) and the output says so; a changed allowed value stays a difference either way.
- Added (`db migrate` guard, `DB_MIGRATE_NOT_ADOPTED`): migrate, standalone, inside `stack up` and inside a deploy's migrate steps, refuses (dry run and `--execute`) a database that holds tables in its app schemas (every schema but the platform's `auth`, `storage`, `extensions`, `supabase_migrations`) while its migration history is empty or missing: "this database already holds a schema; adopt it first (starci app db adopt)". Before, it replayed the bootstrap and the baseline over the existing tables. A fresh empty database bootstraps as before. A Swarm deploy meets the same refusal in its preview and migrate steps (`STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_PREVIEW_RED` / `STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_FAILED` carry it); it does not adopt by itself: `deploy --adopt` (stack/adopt.mts) moves a stack to new runtime names and never touches a schema, so the two cannot double-adopt, and the owner adopts the database with `starci app db adopt` before that deploy.
- Added (`db adopt --if-pending`): adopts when the database holds tables and no history, and otherwise (no tables, or a history that is not empty) answers "nothing to adopt" with exit 0. The refusals of the schema compare are unchanged.
- Added (`stack up --env <dev>`): when `database.baseline` is declared, the database service starts alone and healthy, its role and database are provisioned, the database is adopted (`db adopt --execute --if-pending`: the same compare, `legacyHistory` and harness-owned objects honoured; a difference stops `up` with `STACK_LOCAL_ADOPT` and the diff, before any other service starts and before migrate), then the other services start and migrate runs. `up` prints `adopt: adopted: ...` or `adopt: nothing to adopt`. An app with no baseline declared starts everything at once as before.
- Added (`stack up --env <dev> --only <service>`): starts that one service and waits for it; the database service also gets its role and database. Nothing is adopted or migrated and no process starts, for inspecting a database by hand.
- Fixed (R431 `DB_PRIMARY_KEY_SHAPE`, `DB_FOREIGN_KEY_NAME`, `database-keys.mts`): the key shape is judged over the whole ordered set of active migrations, not at each table's CREATE. A primary key added later (`ADD PRIMARY KEY`, `ADD CONSTRAINT ... PRIMARY KEY`, a column-level one on an added column), dropped (`DROP CONSTRAINT` of its name, `DROP COLUMN`), an `id` column added, retyped or dropped, a table dropped or renamed, and a foreign key dropped or its column dropped or renamed all count. A baseline in the shape a database has today (generated by `starci app db baseline`, the model of `db adopt`) and a later migration that reshapes it to `id uuid` plus a unique natural key are judged as one; a shape that stays wrong cites the CREATE and the last migration that changed the key. A statement the reader cannot follow (a rename of a column to or from `id`, a constraint rename) leaves the table judged as its CREATE declared it. `DB_FOREIGN_KEY_INDEX` still looks anywhere in the set. A baseline that adds its keys with `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT ... PRIMARY KEY` (a pg_dump) no longer reads as "no primary key".
- Fixed (R431, DO blocks): the static statements inside the `DO` blocks of a migration (a reshaping step guarded by `if not exists (...) then alter table ... end if`, so a re-run does nothing) count, in order, where the block sits; the key rules read them. An index written on a column's old name still covers a foreign key on the renamed column.
- Fixed (`DB_RLS_REQUIRED`): judged over the ordered set like R431. A table created without `enable row level security` (or, for a declared definer-owned table, `force row level security`) in its own migration passes when a LATER migration enables (and forces) it, or drops the table; a table that ends without it keeps its finding. A baseline in the shape a database has today carries no RLS (adopt would see policies the live database lacks) and a later migration adds it.
- Added (`db adopt` compare): the objects the harness owns are never compared (the seed receipts table `SEED_RECEIPTS` of `stack/seeds.mts` is the one name; the migration history lives in a schema the compare leaves out already), and the new optional field `database.adopt.legacyHistory: [<table>]` of `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml` names the legacy history tables of the tool that built the database (a TypeORM `migrations` table): each, with its sequence, default, constraints and indexes, is left as it is and listed in the output (`left as is, legacy history`). An undeclared extra object still refuses `DB_ADOPT_SCHEMA_DIFFERS`.
- Added (R451, PROJECT-STACKS-5, owner 2026-11-21 model B): one recipient policy for every app's sealed members, `runtime/knowledge/hfs/sops-recipients.yaml` (the shipped copy of the harness's `ops/vps/recipients.yaml`, public halves only; `runtime/scripts/lib/sops-recipients.mts`). Dev is the harness recipient alone; staging and production are the key of the VPS they deploy to plus that server's one backup recipient, never the harness key. `app check` reports HFS_SOPS_RECIPIENTS (a rule that does not name exactly its environment's set, each stranger named as the harness key, another server's key or an app-only identity), HFS_SOPS_RECIPIENT_UNDECLARED (no recorded server, an undeclared server, a backup not minted), HFS_SOPS_CATCH_ALL and HFS_SOPS_MEMBER_RECIPIENTS (a member's cleartext `sops.age` recipients), each with its fix (`rules/sops-recipients.mts`). The severity is data: `enforce` of the policy (global, and per server for the environments deploying to it) is false, so every R451 finding is a warning in every env, and no app fails `--env prod` on it, a fresh one before it has a server included; flipped to true, a finding is an error, report-only in `--env local`.
- Changed (`app secret init`, the scaffold): no per-app age identity is minted and nothing is written into the app's `.claude/secret.env`. Init writes one `.sops.yaml` rule per declared environment from the policy (dev the harness recipient; a deployed environment its server key plus backup, from public halves only, or no rule while either is unknown), keeps every existing rule (never rotates) and seals the declared placeholders of the environments a rule reaches. The scaffold writes the dev rule and seals only dev placeholders; a deployed environment's members wait for its server and backup keys. `initializeAppIdentity` is replaced by `initializeAppRules` and `policyCreationRules`.
- Added (the SOPS_AGE_KEY guard, `runtime/scripts/hfs/secret-identity-guard.mts`): before sops opens a dev member, the public recipient of every SOPS_AGE_KEY the lookup could take (the environment, the app's `.claude/secret.env`, its main checkout's, the harness secret.env) is compared with the member's recipients; `stack up` and every verb that opens a dev member refuse SECRET_IDENTITY_MISMATCH naming the file read, its recipient prefix, the prefix expected and any later place that matches, and `app check --env local` warns HFS_SOPS_IDENTITY_MISMATCH. Deployed environments are not guarded: a dev machine holding no identity for them is expected. `stack down` opens no member.
- Changed (STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_MISSING): an environment that cannot be sealed yet, because no `.sops.yaml` rule reaches it and its R451 recipient set is unresolved (a new app's production before its server and backup keys), reports that once as HFS_SOPS_RECIPIENT_UNDECLARED instead of one STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_MISSING per absent declared member (`stacks-gate.mts`). A fresh starter stays clean of stack findings.
- Added (stacks, PROJECT-STACKS-21): the optional `environments.<env>.services` of application-stacks.yaml, the opt-in to the server's shared services (schema `$defs.environment.services`, `stack/shared-services.mts`). `services.embeddings: {member, key?, consumers?, required?, network?, url?, urlEnv?}` joins each consumer (default: the services of deploy.images) to the external network `starci-embeddings`, sets `EMBEDDINGS_BASE_URL=http://embeddings-gate:8080/v1` and grants `EMBEDDINGS_API_KEY` through `<KEY>_FILE`. A consumer that also declares the URL variable is refused. `HFS_STACKS_SHAPE` checks the consumers against the rendered model (`rules/stacks-shared-services.mts`). An environment without the block renders byte for byte as before.
- Fixed (render, R294): a grant source declared `required: false` whose sealed member has no envelope yet no longer refuses the render with STACKS_DEPLOY_CONFIG_INVALID: it is left out (`pendingOf` of stack-deploy-config.mts), the render prints a `pending` line and `app check` reports the warning STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_PENDING. A source that omits `required` or sets it true still refuses. A shared-service opt-in not marked required is such a source, so the member and the opt-in can land before the key is sealed.
- Added (failure-codes.yaml, R294 in both catalogs): STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_PENDING.
- Changed (template): the skeleton's production environment carries a commented `services` example.
- Changed (routes keyed per app, `gallery-routes.mts`; contexts/project/gallery.md PROJECT-GALLERY-4): a route is `<app>:<route key>` in `routeKey`, `feRoutes`, the route exclusions, the gallery coupling (`HFS_APP_GALLERY_EXPLAIN_STALE`, `HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_CHANGE_UNAPPROVED`), the UI-audit reach and the board sections. A landing and a product app that both serve `/` no longer count a change of one as a change of the other's screens. A screen's app is its optional `app` of `screen.yaml` (added to the schema; also read from an `explain.md` front matter and a board section), else the app whose route tree serves the route, else the single front-end app; `gallery/routes.json` entries take an optional `app`. Two apps serving a route and no `app` is the new `HFS_APP_GALLERY_ROUTE_AMBIGUOUS` (local: a warning in `--env local`, an error in `--env prod`). A project with one front-end app judges as before.
- Fixed (`repositoryName`, `repo-identity.mts`): when `origin` is a local path, the repository's own `origin` is followed one hop and its last segment names the repository, so a repro clone of a working copy reports the product (`starci-next`) and not the folder it was cloned from (`starci-next-app`); an origin that is no repository, or has none, keeps the last segment of the path as before.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.17.0; @starci/eslint-canon-be 3.28.3, @starci/eslint-canon-fe 9.21.4, the Grammar family 0.52.1 (ranges `~3.28.3`, `~9.21.4`, `~0.52.1`) and @starci/gallery 0.1.15.

## 6.16.0 - 2026-11-20

- Added (R450, PROJECT-STACKS-20): an environment pending its cutover. `environments.<env>.cutover: { state: pending, reason, until: <YYYY-MM-DD>, keep?: [<member> | <member>/<KEY>] }` in .starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml (schema, `runtime/scripts/lib/stack-cutover.mts`) makes `app check` (both envs) report, for that environment alone, the findings of the codes failure-codes.yaml marks `cutoverTolerant` (`true`: STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_MISSING, STACKS_LITE_SELFHOSTED_DB, STACKS_LITE_REDIS_PERSISTENCE, STACK_STORAGE_MISSING, STACKS_IMAGE_ENV_DIVERGES; `kept`: ENV_SEALED_UNREAD of a member or key `keep` names), listed `CUTOVER PENDING (<env>), REPORT ONLY` and in a section of their own beside the gates off by declaration (`runtime/scripts/hfs/check-cutover.mts`); every other finding still blocks. An unsound block is HFS_CUTOVER_DECLARATION_INVALID and a past `until` HFS_CUTOVER_EXPIRED, both blocking and judged outside the cache on every run that selects hfs; neither tolerates anything.
- Added (`stack/cutover.mts`): while an environment carries a cutover block, `stack deploy` in every form (image, --ci-run, --urgent, --adopt, and so the managed stack-deploy.yml) and `stack prepare --execute` refuse it with STACK_CUTOVER_PENDING before any action module loads.
- Added (STACKS_SEALED_MEMBER_MISSING): a sealed member the declaration names that the environment has not sealed, or a declared key its member does not hold yet, is its own code instead of HFS_STACKS_SHAPE (`secrets[]`) or STACKS_DEPLOY_CONFIG_INVALID (`deploy.secrets` members); a present file that is no sops envelope stays HFS_STACKS_SHAPE. ENV_SEALED_UNREAD findings carry their `key`.
- Changed (templates): the skeleton's production environment carries the commented cutover example.
- Added (`stack db-upgrade --env E --image <db@sha256> [--old-image <ref@sha256>] [--execute]`, PROJECT-STACKS-19; stack/db-upgrade.mts, db-upgrade-core.mts, local-db-upgrade.mts): the sanctioned Postgres major upgrade, a dump and restore into a declared new volume in throwaway containers with no network. The pair is declared, never guessed: `environments.<env>.deploy.dbUpgrade: {volumes: {<new>: <old>}}` (schema `deployDbUpgrade`, exactly one pair, the new volume one of `deploy.volumes` and the one the stack source mounts as the data directory), or `deploy.renamedFrom.volumes` for a stack renamed at once; a dev environment names `local.dbUpgrade.hold`. The old major runs on a work copy (the old volume is mounted read-only only and measured equal before and after), its own pg_dumpall --roles-only keeps every password hash, the target image initialises the new volume with the sealed superuser password (stdin, tmpfs), restores roles then databases, and both clusters are inventoried (databases, extensions, row counts, sequences, object counts, roles and hashes, memberships); the sealed superuser and app passwords log in over TCP. A Swarm run is one recorded attempt (kind `db-upgrade`, record `upgrade`) under the deploy lock; the dev run journals in its state directory under a lock file and swaps the proven restore in after holding the old data directory. Refusals STACK_DB_UPGRADE_* (26).
- Changed (`stack deploy --adopt`, adopt.mts): with `deploy.dbUpgrade` it adopts the upgraded volume of the same stack: an accepted release, the database stopped, the newest `db-upgrade` of the journal done into the declared volume with the deploy's db image (STACK_ADOPT_UPGRADE_NO_RELEASE, _UNPROVEN, _IMAGE), then the hold deploy and adopt step; `adoptedFrom.kind: upgrade`. A failure redeploys the previous release on the old volume; `stack rollback` to it needs `--data-acknowledged` (STACK_ROLLBACK_UPGRADED_DATA). A plain deploy while `dbUpgrade` is declared and the database still mounts the old volume is refused (STACK_DEPLOY_DB_UPGRADE_PENDING). An environment without `dbUpgrade` deploys exactly as before.
- Added (pins): `postgres-16` (16.16) and `postgres-16-alpine` (16.16-alpine) by digest in contexts/stack/pins.yaml, mirrored by PREVIOUS_MAJOR_IMAGES: the old-major server of an upgrade when the image the database ran is gone.
- Added (failure-codes.yaml): the STACK_DB_UPGRADE_*, STACK_ADOPT_UPGRADE_*, STACK_DEPLOY_DB_UPGRADE_PENDING and STACK_ROLLBACK_UPGRADED_DATA refusals.
- Fixed (`HFS_BACKING_UNUSED` still raised for a backing served by an integration module): the app-root check judged only the root's own files, so it never saw `be/src`. It now gets the whole tracked tree and resolves each import of every production back-end source with the compiler's module resolution over the be tsconfig (`paths` aliases such as `@modules/*`, relative paths, `index.ts` barrels and re-exports), counting a source that lands in `src/modules/integrations/<service>/`. No path-string match.
- Fixed (`HFS_UNTRACKED_ROOT_ENTRY fe/apps/web`, `ARCH_INTERNAL_IMPORT_UNRESOLVED`): the managed auth client (slot `fe.modules.auth.client`, template group `fe/auth`) no longer names the folder `web`. Its path is `apps/__app__/src/modules/auth/{client.ts,email-doors.ts,index.ts}` and sync renders it into the fe app that serves sign-in: the app marked `"signIn": true` in hfs.json `sides.fe.apps[]`, else the only kind-next app. Several kind-next apps with none marked are refused `HFS_SYNC_SIGN_IN_APP_AMBIGUOUS`. An app named `web` renders where it did.
- Added (hfs.json): the optional `signIn: true` of an fe app of kind `next` (at most one app of the side).
- Added (`starci app db adopt [--db-url-env <NAME>] [--image <ref>] [--execute]`, `db/adopt.mts`): adopts a database another tool built (TypeORM) as the SQL-migrated one. It refuses unless the history is empty and the live schema (platform schemas left out) equals the replay of `db/bootstrap.sql` plus the baseline on a scratch database, listing every difference (`DB_ADOPT_SCHEMA_DIFFERS`); then, in one transaction, it runs the bootstrap, creates the history table, applies the baseline's privileges and records the baseline applied. The baseline never runs. Dry run by default; `--execute` is armed off the loopback.
- Added (`db/client-image.mts`): a host without `psql` and `pg_dump` runs them from the pinned postgres image through `docker run --rm` (the connection passed in, the password only in the docker process's environment, `-f` and `--file` paths mounted), for `db migrate`, `plan`, `dump`, `post-cutover` and `baseline` alike, staging and production included. The host binary stays the first choice; `STARCI_DB_CONTAINER` is unchanged. `DB_BASE_IMAGE` moved to `db/image.mts` (still exported by `sync`).
- Added (passport kit): the optional `AUTH_COOKIE_NAME` names the refresh cookie (`env.optionalCookieName`: an RFC 6265 token of at most 64 characters, else the boot fails `KeyInvalid`); the default stays `refresh_token`. The auth package already reads and clears the cookie through the configured name. Apps that share a parent cookie domain must set distinct names (KEYS.md, README, stacks.md). R248 reads `optionalCookieName` as an optional key.
- Fixed (`HFS_MANAGED_FILE_DRIFT` on package.json): sync no longer renders the root script `migrate` (`cli migrate run`, the TypeORM migrate path of the ops cli); the one edition migrates with SQL through the `db:*` scripts, so an app that removed `migrate` keeps it removed.
- Fixed (`HFS_BACKING_UNUSED` against the stacks rule that demands `qdrant` in `sides.be.backing`): a declared backing service is also consumed by a back-end integration module named after it (`src/modules/integrations/<service>/`, imported by another file), the client an app writes over the service's own protocol. An app with a real npm client is judged as before.
- Added (R259 `DB_MIGRATION_DESTRUCTIVE`): a table dropped by a contract migration and created anew, under the same name, by a later migration marked `-- replaces: <table>` is a replacement, not a destroyed object. The rule verifies the marked migration creates the table and sorts after the drop (else a finding on it), judges code references against the new definition and still refuses a read of a column the old table had and the new one lacks, and reports the lost rows of the old table as an info line. `db.destructiveRulings` is unchanged: it accepts an unreferenced object outside a contract migration and cannot cover a referenced table.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.16.0; @starci/eslint-canon-be 3.28.2, @starci/eslint-canon-fe 9.21.3, the Grammar family 0.52.0 (ranges `~3.28.2`, `~9.21.3`, `~0.52.0`) and @starci/gallery 0.1.14.

## 6.15.0 - 2026-11-20

- Changed (templates, R212): the last lite folders take the plain names. be/skeleton-lite and fe/skeleton-lite are merged into be/skeleton and fe/skeleton (the lite file wins where both existed; the full-only be files, fe/skeleton/apps/app, apps/landing and packages/__project__-* that no scaffold rendered are gone), and be/patterns/{jobs,queues}/platform-lite into platform (the TypeORM persistence.* templates and their entries in jobs.yaml and queues.yaml are gone). The scaffold output of a new app is byte for byte the same. The template snapshot 5.8.3 took the same merge, with its manifest paths.
- Changed (names): rules/lite-proof-lane.mts, lite-secrets.mts and stacks-lite-backing.mts are rules/proof-lane.mts, secret-custody.mts and stacks-backing.mts (rule ids proof-lane, secret-custody, stacks-backing); scaffold/add-*-lite.mts, lite-exports.mts and queues-cli-lite.mts are add-cli, add-database, add-webhook-inbox, add-queue, package-exports and queues-cli. The pattern key `liteAdapter` is `adapter`; the unused `liteFiles` key is gone (a topic has one files tree). scaffold-gate.mts: `refuseUntemplatedAdapter` and `AdapterRefusal` replace the lite-named pair.
- Kept (finding codes): HFS_STARCIWORK_LITE_FORBIDDEN, HFS_LITE_SECRET_CUSTODY, LITE_DB_MIGRATION, STACKS_LITE_SELFHOSTED_DB, STACKS_LITE_REDIS_PERSISTENCE and BE_LITE_PROOF_LANE_MISSING keep their names because apps cite them. The check has no per-code exception or list an alias could extend, so a second name would be a duplicate catalogue entry with no reader; a later train renames them when the apps have moved.
- Removed: `starci app add inbox` (the verb and its usage line; it only refused), the TypeORM-authority branch of architecture/sql-owner.mts (ruleParams.be.schemaAuthority is `sql` in the manifest and an app cannot set it), the full-only slot-less skeleton files listed above, the `@lite` and `@full` tags of the spec proof labels.
- Changed (slots.yaml): the `goesTo` and `why` text that described two editions says it once.
- Added (R449, `runtime/scripts/hfs/rules/master-plan.mts`, PROJECT-MASTER-PLAN-1): the Master Plan of an app, `.starciwork/master-plan.yaml` (schema `starci/master-plan@1`): nine fixed layers (business, tech, patterns, ui, data, testing, security, observability, domains), each with `decisions[]`, `reason`, `rules[]` (`{ id, kind: always|never, text, enforcedBy? }`, `enforcedBy` an hfs rule id, a check preset or `review`) and an optional `roadmap[]`. The app check reports `HFS_MASTER_PLAN_MISSING` (a warning in every env) while the app tracks none, and `HFS_MASTER_PLAN_INVALID` (an error) for a malformed file, a repeated rule id or an `enforcedBy` naming an hfs rule the catalog does not hold. Slot `app.starciwork` admits the file (manifest 3.42.0), the `.starciwork/.gitignore` template re-admits it, the Work boundary (`isProductPath`, `isLiteProductPath`) holds it, and the app skeleton carries the nine layers with nothing decided.
- Fixed (UI Audit gate, PROJECT-GALLERY-8 item 3): a screen is touched when a file its route's `page.tsx` reaches through value imports changed, not only its page, its feature folder or its screen.yaml, so a change to a shared composite, a hook, a module, a workspace package or an imported catalog reaches every screen that renders it (`cache/screen-reach.mts`: the app's own TypeScript under the front-end app's tsconfig, type-only imports left out, at most 6000 files per walk, edges memoised by path and mtime). The gate's line names a route whose walk hit the cap.
- Added (R395, PROJECT-GALLERY-9): the design board of an app, `gallery/board/board.yaml` (slot app.gallery-board, `rules/gallery-board.mts`). An app that keeps the record is judged on HFS_APP_GALLERY_BOARD_INVALID (the record's shape, a section without its module `gallery/board/<area>/<screen>.tsx`), HFS_APP_GALLERY_BOARD_ROUTE_MISSING (a coded `page.tsx` route with no section, unless excluded in gallery/routes.json) and HFS_APP_GALLERY_BOARD_SECTION_UNMAPPED (a section whose route is not coded and not marked `planned`, or marked `planned` while coded); the last two are localOnly. An app without the record is outside the rule.
- Added (stacks, PROJECT-STACKS-18): the optional `environments.<env>.deploy.renamedFrom: {stack, releasesBase?, volumes: {<new>: <old>}}` of application-stacks.yaml (schema `deployRenamedFrom`, normalised by stack-deploy-config.mts: never the environment's own stack, every new volume one of `deploy.volumes`, every old one named once). An environment without it normalises, renders, deploys and journals exactly as before.
- Added (`stack deploy --adopt`, stack/adopt.mts): the first release of a renamed stack takes over the copied data instead of a first start. A preflight that changes nothing (STACK_ADOPT_UNDECLARED, _ALREADY_ACCEPTED, _VOLUME_MISSING, _VOLUME_EMPTY, _SOURCE_RUNNING, _SOURCE_UNREADABLE, _SOURCE_NO_RELEASE, _SOURCE_NOT_STANDBY), `adoptedFrom` in release.json and `adopted` in the attempt journal, the hold deploy with the database alone, the proof of its migration history (STACK_ADOPT_NO_HISTORY, STACK_ADOPT_NO_DATABASE), the plain migrate preview, backup and expand path inside it, then the real deploy. A failed adopted deploy, and `stack rollback --data-acknowledged` of the adopted release (STACK_ROLLBACK_ADOPTED_DATA without it), put the new stack in standby and resume the old stack: its last release is the rollback target, never its stored bytes. `--adopt` never runs with `--urgent`.
- Added (`stack volume copy --env E [--from V --to V] [--verify] [--execute]` and `stack volume verify`, stack/volume.mts): a cold copy on the manager in a throwaway container of the pinned `deploy.backup.image` (no network, source read-only), files, bytes, content and metadata digests of both sides, refused on a difference (STACK_VOLUME_MISMATCH, STACK_VOLUME_SOURCE_CHANGED with `--verify`), while anything that runs mounts either volume (STACK_VOLUME_IN_USE), into an undeclared or non-empty volume (STACK_VOLUME_UNDECLARED, STACK_VOLUME_TARGET_NOT_EMPTY). Without --from/--to the pairs are `renamedFrom.volumes`. An executed copy is a recorded attempt (kind `volume-copy`) under the deploy lock.
- Added (`stack standby` and `stack resume --env E [--renamed-from] [--timeout s] [--execute]`, stack/standby.mts): writers then stores to 0 (a global service held by a placement constraint), then `traefik.enable=false`; the services are recorded first in the attempt journal (kinds `standby` and `resume`, outcome `done`) under the deploy lock; resume starts stores, waits for healthy, starts writers, waits for healthy, restores the routes. `--renamed-from` acts on the old stack's releases base, lock and journal. STACK_STANDBY_ALREADY, STACK_STANDBY_NO_SERVICES, STACK_STANDBY_DOCKER, STACK_STANDBY_RENAMED_UNDECLARED, STACK_RESUME_NO_STANDBY.
- Changed (`stack prepare` and its dry run): an environment with `renamedFrom` is checked on both sides (old and new volumes, the old stack's services and replicas, the old releases base and its last accepted release).
- Added (failure-codes.yaml): the STACK_ADOPT_*, STACK_VOLUME_*, STACK_STANDBY_*, STACK_RESUME_NO_STANDBY and STACK_ROLLBACK_ADOPTED_DATA refusals.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.15.0; @starci/eslint-canon-be 3.28.1, @starci/eslint-canon-fe 9.21.2, the Grammar family 0.51.1 (ranges `~3.28.1`, `~9.21.2`, `~0.51.1`) and @starci/gallery 0.1.13. The slot `app.gallery-board` is since 6.15.0.

## 6.14.1 - 2026-11-20

- Fixed (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNWARMED` never converging): two checkouts of one commit now write byte-identical committed records whatever each built or fetched (check-cache.md "Untracked program inputs", "Machine-local records").
- Fixed (build artifacts in program keys and programs): a file a tsconfig's include globs reach that git ignores and that lies under no `__generated__` folder is a build artifact (`runtime/scripts/hfs/program-artifacts.mts`), such as Next's `next-env.d.ts` with its imports of `.next/types/**`, a `dist/` or a `.tsbuildinfo`. It is no untracked input of a side key (`cache/side-key.mts` `untrackedProgramInputs`) or a types program key. It is no root of the programs `readTypeScriptProject` builds (the architecture machine). The types family runs `tsc` with them hidden through `cache/tsc-launch.mts`, which is TypeScript's own `executeCommandLine` over a directory listing without them, so every checkout type-checks the program a clean checkout has. Generated code (`npm run codegen`) stays an input.
- Fixed (keys on remote state): verdicts keyed on a moving remote ref or on untracked build output are machine-local. That covers the db rules (the merge-base with `origin/main`), the database migration step of the build family, the nivoos package checks (`origin/dev` and the built tools) and a project check that names `refs`. They are kept and reused in the untracked tool cache (`local-records/<folder>.jsonl`, under the digest of the family's fingerprint parts, `CheckSession.localRecords` / `replaceLocal`) and are never written into `.starcistacks/cache/`, so they are outside the set the push gate compares. A committed record an older harness wrote there is dropped on the next run.
- Added (`src/check-cache-determinism.spec.mts`): the guard. A checkout of the cache-warm commit that holds a framework build's artifacts (one of them ill-typed) and has `origin/dev` and `origin/main` at another commit writes nothing into the committed cache.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.14.1.

## 6.14.0 - 2026-11-20

- Changed (R212, owner ruling 2026-11-20): an app has ONE edition, what the live apps run (all of them declared `edition: "lite"`): the full code structure, slots, patterns and back-end unit specs; no Work features catalog; chat agents code directly; its own Docker Swarm stack with the backing services it declares; the integration and e2e test world on request only. The lite overlays of knowledge/hfs/slots.yaml (`lite:`, `litePresence`, `liteManagedBy`, `ruleParams.be.lite`) are folded into the base data and the `editions` keys, `editionWhy`, `codeEditions` and the evidence `edition(s)` fields leave the slot manifest and the rule catalog (their schemas refuse them now). A both-sides slot whose presence differs by side names it in `sidePresence` (`repo.app-image`: `{fe: optional}`).
- Deprecated: hfs.json `edition`. Any value is accepted and ignored; the app check reports the warning HFS_EDITION_DEPRECATED (never blocking) on the declaration. A later train drops the field and the warning becomes a declaration error.
- Removed (full-only rules no app runs): R36 BE_SQL_OUTSIDE_PERSISTENCE, R112 BE_INTEGRATION_SPEC_MISSING, R167 BE_ASYNC_SPEC_MISSING, R173 BE_SAGA_E2E_MISSING, R277 HFS_SECRET_OUTSIDE_ENC (an off-home envelope stays HFS_LITE_SECRET_CUSTODY), the full enforcer of R155 BE_PATTERN_SPEC_MISSING (R284 keeps the code), the slot be.integrations.keycloak, HFS_EDITION_INVALID and HFS_EDITION_FORBIDDEN_PRESENT (the test world, the browser journey and their scripts and dependencies are allowed, on request).
- Removed (edition-only constraints no app depends on): the Swarm refusal of stock backing images (MinIO, Postgres, Kafka, Keycloak, RabbitMQ: swarm-stack.mts), the edition admission of backing services (Qdrant is a backing service an app may declare), the `editions` filter of the findings (`judgedFindings`). `app.browser` and `app.browser-config` are opt-in again (`browser: true`).
- Removed: the `--edition` flag of `starci app scaffold`, `starci app check` and `starci app world` (world runs for every app, on request), the `starci app upgrade` lite refusal, the scaffolders of `add connection` and `add inbox` (both refuse, as before for every live app), the edition gate of `starci app add` (scaffold/scaffold-gate.mts keeps the slot gate). `starci app scaffold` writes no `edition`.
- Changed (templates): the lite template groups take the plain names: app/ci-workflows, app/package-scripts, app/tool-config, app/gitignore, app/starciwork.gitignore, app/codegen, app/skeleton (the overlay merged over its base), be/auth, fe/auth. be/skeleton-lite and fe/skeleton-lite stay the overlay over the shared base skeleton, which also holds the sources the add verbs copy later.
- Changed (Sonar): knowledge/sonar-gate.yaml is one gate, named `starci-quality-lite` (the server gate object the live apps select); its `lite` block is gone. knowledge/hfs/ci-standard.yaml `jobs` is one list (`required`, `pipeline`, `optional`).
- Added (stacks): the optional `environments.<env>.monitoring` block of application-stacks.yaml (schema, the HFS_STACKS_SHAPE rule stacks-monitoring.mts, stack/monitoring.mts). It declares metrics endpoints, logs (on by default), blackbox probes (by default the ingress hosts), exporters of backing services (postgres and redis sidecars, minio, qdrant, kafka-connect and ingress scraped natively), traces and profiles. `stack render` adds scrape and probe labels, exporter sidecars, the OTLP and Pyroscope endpoints and the external monitoring network. An environment without the block renders byte for byte as before.
- Changed (templates): the app skeleton application-stacks.yaml carries the block as a commented opt-in example, and its production README says how to opt in.

R187 names both.

## 6.13.57 - 2026-11-20

- Added (R277, `rules/secret-custody-only.mts`, `HFS_PACKAGE_SECRET_NOT_SEALED`, `HFS_PACKAGE_SECRET_RECIPIENT`): a business package may commit its provider keys as `packages/<pkg>/secrets/<name>.sops.yaml`, only as a valid sops document (every leaf `ENC[...]`, a `sops` block with age recipients) sealed to the recipients its own `packages/<pkg>/.sops.yaml` names for that path. Plaintext is `HFS_PACKAGE_SECRET_NOT_SEALED`, a missing or different recipient set `HFS_PACKAGE_SECRET_RECIPIENT`; `.env`, `*.secrets.env` and other plaintext stay refused. ## 6.13.56 - 2026-11-19

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the Grammar family 0.49.0 (range ~0.49.0), eslint-canon-fe 9.21.0, @starci/gallery 0.1.8 and the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.56.
- Added (R448, `hfs.json` `sides.be.apps[].boot.timeoutMs`): the time the composition may take (1000 to 300000 ms, default 15000). The boot worker loads the app source by transpiling each file alone, with no program and no type check, so a large composition loads in seconds, and a boot factory may return an application context it composed itself (a testing module with the infrastructure doubled), which the check closes.

## 6.13.55 - 2026-11-19

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.55.
- Added (R448, `runtime/scripts/hfs/app-boot.mts`, `cache/boot-entry-check.mts`): the boot check. A back-end app declares the root a command of it boots in hfs.json `sides.be.apps[].boot` (`{ module?, export?, factory?, env? }`); `starci app check --gate hfs` (its `checks` step, so a project with the wiring gate off still has it judged) composes it with `NestFactory.createApplicationContext(root, { abortOnError: false })` in a child with no listener, no lifecycle hook, no network and only `NODE_ENV=test` plus the declared stub env, then closes it. A Nest wiring exception is `BE_BOOT_ENTRY_FAILED` with Nest's own message (redacted); a failure outside the wiring is the warning `BE_BOOT_ENTRY_UNJUDGED`. The verdict is the `boot-entry` record of `check/build/`, keyed on the be side (tracked and untracked program inputs), `packages/`, `hfs.json`, the declared entries, the lockfile, the Node major and the harness. An app that declares none runs nothing. Why: a provider injecting a token its module did not import passed the doubles of the R272 proof and every gate, and took the dev stack down at the stack seed.
- Added (`runtime/scripts/lib/error-tail.mts`, PROJECT-STACKS-16): a failed seed command, seed door or post-deploy task prints the error-only, redacted tail of its output under its failure line (`STACK_SEED_COMMAND`, `STACK_SEED_DOOR_REFUSED`, the post-deploy FAIL line): Error and exception lines, stack frames and Nest's "can't resolve dependencies" block, every opened secret value masked (raw, percent-encoded, JSON-escaped) and credential URLs redacted; never the raw output or an env dump.

## 6.13.53 - 2026-11-19

- Added (ONE SOURCE, owner 2026-11-19): three checks that keep the marketing site, the console and the canvas on one source. R445 `rules/brand-one.mts`: `FE_BRAND_NOT_ONE` (the web apps of a front end hold exactly one `modules/brand/brand.css`), `FE_BRAND_CANVAS_LITERAL` (a canvas generator under `.starcistacks/canvas/gen/` writes a colour literal; `gen/kit/` aside) and `FE_BRAND_CANVAS_TOKENS_STALE` (`.starcistacks/canvas/tokens.css` is not what the brand file generates; `scripts/hfs/canvas-tokens.mts` writes it, `--check` judges it). R446 `rules/globals-one.mts`: `FE_GLOBALS_NOT_ONE` (a second global stylesheet, or a file importing a stylesheet other than `globals.css`) and `FE_GLOBALS_IMPORTS_MISSING` (the one globals lacks the Grammar or the brand import). R447 `rules/state-pages.mts`: `FE_STATE_PAGE_NOT_SHARED` (every not-found, error plus global-error, and offline boundary renders one shared page component per kind) and `FE_STATE_PAGE_GROUP_MISSING` (every route group, at any depth, carries its own `error.tsx` and `not-found.tsx`). R443 `error-pages.mts` now judges a boundary at any depth of the route tree, not only at `[locale]` and one group below it.

- Added (UI Audit gate): `screen.yaml` per gallery screen replaces `explain.md` and `audit.json`; the `uiaudit` gate family (`cache/uiaudit-family.mts`, failure codes of the audit), the `uiaudit/` runtime shipped in the package, the `audit run|status` and `gallery migrate-explain` verbs, and the skeleton's screens as `screen.yaml`. `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.52 and the gallery pin 0.1.5.

## 6.13.51 - 2026-11-19

- Fixed (`stack/render.mts`, shared-edge ingress): a rendered router and service are named `<stack>-<service>` (`<stack>-<service>-<port>` for several ports), never the bare service name, because the edge-traefik is shared with other stacks and a duplicate router name makes Traefik drop both; the edge network is named by `traefik.swarm.network` (swarm provider) and `traefik.docker.network` (docker provider).
- Added (`ingress.routes[]`, shared edge): a route may carry `paths` (exact), `pathPrefixes` and an explicit `priority`, so one host can split by path between services (a front end that serves the host and an api that takes `/api/`, `/graphql`); routes sharing a port and the same constraint form one router (`<stack>-<service>`, `<stack>-<service>-paths`, `-paths-2`, ...) whose rule is `Host(...)` and, with paths, `&& (Path(...) || PathPrefix(...))`; one Traefik service per service and port holds the port.

## 6.13.50 - 2026-11-19

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the Grammar family 0.47.4 (range ~0.47.4: loading buttons in an inert hidden wrapper), @starci/gallery 0.1.4 and the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.50.
- Fixed (`rules/env-parity.mts`): `ENV_SEALED_UNREAD` no longer flags an SSH_GROUP key (`VPS_PORT`, `VPS_HOST`, `VPS_USER`, `privateKey`, `knownHosts`) in a transport member (the `deploy.remote.sealed` member and `vps-ci`): the deploy transport reads it, no EnvSource does; the same classification `ENV_DUPLICATE` uses. A third member holding one stays unread.
- Fixed (`cache/nivoos-package-check.mts`): the nivoos package check installs the dependencies of each `packages/<name>/workbench/<app>/` that lacks them (`npm ci --ignore-scripts` through the heavy governor) before it judges the package, so a fresh worktree is no false red; a failed install is its own finding, the package is not judged and no verdict is recorded.
- Added (`ingress.kind: shared-edge`, PROJECT-STACKS-4): an environment declares its public hosts once, in `ingress.routes` (`host`, `service`, `port`) with the edge `network` and `certResolver`; `stack/render.mts` renders each routed service's Traefik labels (rule, entrypoint, tls certresolver, service port, docker network) and joins the edge network. A hostname written in the stack file of a shared-edge environment, or a rendered host `routes` does not hold, is `STACKS_HOST_UNROUTED`. The own ingress form (`ingress.component` + `ingress.hosts`) is unchanged.

- Fixed (`sync`): `images.yml` of a lite app builds the storage image when its `deploy.images` names it (`app: storage`), so `statics/` ships through the normal release.

## 6.13.46 - 2026-11-18

- Added (R402, PROJECT-LINT-3; `cache/check-verdicts.mts`, `cache/project-checks.mts`, `runtime/scripts/lib/project-ext.mts`): a project check may declare what it reads. `ext/manifest.json` `checks[].reads` (tracked files or folders) and `refs` (git revisions `<ref>:<path>`) scope and cache its verdict as a record of `check/build` (step `project-check`), keyed on the content of those paths and of its own file, the objects the refs name, the Node major, the harness version and the lockfile; an unchanged key reuses the verdict (green or red), a ref git cannot name runs the check and stores nothing. A check with no `reads` runs every time as before.
- Changed (R436, `cache/nivoos-package-check.mts`): the nivoos package check is scoped and cached per package (step `nivoos-package`): keyed on the tracked files of the package and of the workspace packages it and the CLI depend on (the SDK), their built output, the registry versions of the CLI and SDK, and the seed `origin/dev:packages/<name>/<out>` holds. The workspace CLI and SDK are built first through the stamped package build the types step uses, so a stale `dist/` can no longer let the version guard (a cell, graph or agent whose content changed under the same version) pass. `check/build` keeps the verdict steps the CI-parity family does not own.
- Documented (PROJECT-LINT-4): a gate the app declares off takes its checks with it; `checks` off runs no project check and no nivoos package check, `types` off no type-check.
- Added (R395 `HFS_APP_GALLERY_DESIGN_CRITIQUE_MISSING`, `rules/gallery-design.mts`, `gallery-explain.mts`; PROJECT-GALLERY-7 item 3): a `matches` page needs the section `## Phương án đã cân nhắc` in its explain.md. `design.json` pages may carry `matchedAt` (ISO, `matches` only); an error when `matchedAt` is on or after 2026-11-18T00:00:00+07:00, info (not final yet) for older `matches` pages or ones without it. The explain readers (`gallery-explain.mts`, `@starci/gallery`) skip that section instead of reading it as a region.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the Grammar family 0.47.1 (range ~0.47.1: the workbench gaps, `Grid size="tile"`, tile `groundTone`, `TagGroup` `onClearAll`, `Select` `icon`, `SearchField` `measure="toolbar"`, six DNA laws) `@starci/gallery` 0.1.1 and the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.46.

## 6.13.45 - 2026-11-18

- Fixed (`sync/nested-lock.mts`, `sync/index.mts`, `sync/managed.mts`): `starci app sync` rendered `gallery/package.json` (it added `@starci/gallery`) and left `gallery/package-lock.json` as it was, so `npm ci` in `gallery/` failed and the dev stack's gallery never started. For every managed sub-package that already has its own `package-lock.json`, `sync --execute` now resolves the lockfile again with `npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts` after it writes the package.json (exit 1, with the command printed, when npm cannot). A dry run prints `HFS_SYNC_DRIFT <dir>/package-lock.json` and `app check` reports `HFS_MANAGED_FILE_DRIFT` for the lockfile, both naming the dependencies that differ and the fix command. A sub-package with no lockfile is not judged.
- Fixed (`stack/local-config.mts`, `local-prepare.mts`, `local-up.mts`): a gallery that fails inside `stack up` (its `gallery-install` setup, or its `gallery-live` readiness row) no longer aborts the whole `up`. Each failing gallery row is printed as `FAIL`, the api, the web and the services keep running, the tunnel routes and origins are still printed, and `up` ends with a line naming the failed rows and exits non-zero (`data.failed` in `--json`). A failing api, web or service row still stops `up` with its own code.
- Checked (lite starter): `templates/app/skeleton-lite` already carries `statics/.gitkeep`, the three seed levels (`.starcistacks/seeds`, `dev/seeds`, `production/seeds`, each with `seeds.yaml` and `sql|data|files`) and the managed storage image; a spec holds the lite starter to the full one. An app scaffolded before the statics slot has to add those folders by hand (the starter is written once).
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.45.

## 6.13.44 - 2026-11-18

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.44.
- Fixed (R436 `HFS_NIVOOS_UNDECLARED`, `rules/nivoos.mts`, `slots.mts`): a package folder listed in hfs.json `publishes` (the platform's own published SDK, CLI and catalogue packages) is no business package and needs no `sides.nivoos.packages` entry; the SDK package itself (its name is an SDK name) is exempt too. A sibling of the SDK such as `packages/nivoos-cli` no longer blocks adopting 6.13.43.

## 6.13.43 - 2026-11-18

- Changed (R53 `FE_ERROR_BOUNDARY_MISSING`, R59 `FE_I18N_PLACEMENT`; `scripts/hfs/slot-required.mts`, `rules/frontend-tree.mts`): the locale `loading.tsx` and `error.tsx` that `fe.app.next` requires are also satisfied by a route group directly under `[locale]` (`src/app/[locale]/(pages)/loading.tsx`). A `loading.tsx` at `[locale]` wraps the `[...rest]` catch-all, so `notFound()` fired after streaming began and every unknown address answered HTTP 200 with the default title (a soft 404). `FE_I18N_PLACEMENT` is unchanged (every route file still sits under `[locale]`; a group is transparent).
- Once a group holds a boundary, the app must also hold `src/app/[locale]/[...rest]/page.tsx` calling `notFound()` and a `src/app/[locale]/not-found.tsx` exporting `generateMetadata` or `metadata`, both outside the group (`FE_ERROR_BOUNDARY_MISSING`). A `loading.tsx` still at `[locale]` stays accepted and is reported as an info `FE_I18N_PLACEMENT` finding that names the move.
- `FE_ERROR_PAGE_NOT_EMPTY_STATE` judges the boundary files of the group too.
- Templates (`templates/fe/skeleton`, `skeleton-lite`): pages, `loading.tsx` and `error.tsx` live in `[locale]/(pages)/`; new `[locale]/[...rest]/page.tsx`; `[locale]/not-found.tsx` exports the localized title (`appNotFoundMetadata`, `landingNotFoundMetadata`). Patterns FE-FOLDER-3 and FE-FOLDER-4 say so.
- Fixed (`stack/local-*.mts`): `stack up` verifies each recorded PID (its command and its port) before trusting it, so a reused PID is no longer taken for the stack; status, doctor, down and verify read the same check.
- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): the Grammar family 0.46.2 (range ~0.46.2) and @starci/eslint-canon-be 3.27.3 (range ~3.27.3) and @starci/auth 0.7.2 (the lite baseline key rules; see the lite migration note below). The `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.43.
- Added (`sides.desktop`, `runtime/scripts/hfs/desktop-side.mts`, `contexts/project/desktop.md` PROJECT-DESKTOP-1..4, R444): the desktop app of a project, a Tauri v2 remote-origin shell that loads the deployed web app (macOS, Windows, Linux). `hfs.json` declares it beside `fe`: `{ app, name, identifier, origin, devOrigin?, allowedOrigins?, schemes?, targets?, updater?: { pubkey, endpoint } }`. It is a declaration side with a folder of its own (`desktop/`), like `nivoos`: it is not a member of `PROJECT_SIDES`, so a project that declares none has no new file, slot, variable line or check.
- Added (sync, `sync/desktop.mts`, `templates/app/desktop-config`, `templates/app/desktop-workflow`): for a project that declares it `starci app sync` renders `desktop/src-tauri/{tauri.conf.json,Cargo.toml,build.rs,src/main.rs,src/lib.rs}`, `desktop/frontend/index.html` and `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml` from the declaration (slots `app.desktop-config`, `app.ci-desktop-release`, `managedWhenSides: [desktop]`); the managed `.prettierignore` and `.gitignore` gain `desktop/` and `desktop/src-tauri/{target,gen}/`. The version is `"../../package.json"`, so the installer, the updater manifest and the web app share one number. The Rust shell creates its one window, refuses navigation outside the declared origins (the rest opens in the system browser), exposes no command to the page, handles the declared deep link schemes through the single-instance plugin and checks the updater once at start.
- Added (`desktop-release.yml`): a `v<version>` tag builds the installers per declared system on GitHub-hosted runners (Rust stable, `tauri-cli` 2.12.5, `tauri-apps/tauri-action` pinned by commit), uploads them and the updater manifest `latest.json` to a draft GitHub Release and opens it when every system built; the signing secrets (`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`, `APPLE_*`) are read by name and only the set ones reach the build.
- Added (R444, `rules/desktop.mts`): `HFS_DESKTOP_UNDECLARED`, `HFS_DESKTOP_FILE_MISSING` (icons; `Cargo.lock` a warning), `HFS_DESKTOP_CONFIG`, `HFS_DESKTOP_SECURITY` (global Tauri API, `dangerous*` switches, unsafe CSP, an updater over http, a capability that opens the IPC to a wildcard or plain-http remote origin) and `HFS_DESKTOP_SIGNING_KEY` (a certificate, key store or private key tracked). Registered in `failure-codes.yaml`, both `rules.yaml` and both `slots.yaml` mirrors, the A15 finder list and the README table. The checks never run cargo.
- Added (`starci app desktop init|doctor|build|dev|keygen`, `desktop/cli.mts`): `init` declares `sides.desktop`, adds the optional CI secret names to `ci.secrets` and runs sync; `doctor` reports the Rust toolchain; `build [--target <os>] [--debug] [--bundles <list>]` runs `cargo tauri build` for this machine's own system and refuses without Rust (`DESKTOP_TOOLCHAIN_MISSING`, exit 2, naming CI as the builder); `dev` opens the shell on the dev origin; `keygen --out <file>` writes the updater key pair, refusing a path inside the repository. Refusals: `DESKTOP_USAGE`, `DESKTOP_ALREADY_DECLARED`, `DESKTOP_DECLARATION_INVALID`, `DESKTOP_TARGET_UNKNOWN|UNDECLARED|HOST`, `DESKTOP_NOT_RENDERED`, `DESKTOP_KEY_INSIDE_REPO|EXISTS`. `package.json` `files` gains `desktop/**`.
- Not wired, owner decisions: Windows code signing (an OV/EV certificate or Azure Trusted Signing), the Apple Developer account and notarization credentials, the updater key custody, mobile targets.
- Fixed (`cache/nivoos-package-check.mts`): `HFS_NIVOOS_PACKAGE_CHECK` finds the `nivoos` bin in `@starci/nivoos-cli` (then a package named `nivoos`), under the package's `node_modules` and the app root's; the finding says to add `@starci/nivoos-cli`. `HFS_NIVOOS_UNDECLARED` also counts `@starci/nivoos-sdk` and `@starci/nivoos-cli` as the SDK.
- Fixed (`rules/unused-symbol*.mts`): `HFS_UNUSED_SYMBOL` reads a JSON catalog object handed on whole (`words={messages.app.errors.global}`: a JSX attribute, a call argument, a spread, a property value, a return) as a read of every key under it; an alias initializer and a member read are judged as before. The lite scaffold's global error page passes unchanged.
- Fixed (gallery coverage, `gallery-routes.mts`): the `[locale]/[...rest]/page.tsx` that only calls `notFound()` is no product route; the not-found page is its own screen.
- Fixed (spec): the mail link check reads the auth pages under `[locale]/(pages)/`.
- Fixed (lite skeleton): the managed identity module passes its own key rules. `identity_refresh_tokens.session_id` is `identity_session_id` (migration and `IdentityRefreshTokenEntity`); `identity_attempts` and `http_security_rate_windows` are keyed `id uuid primary key` beside a unique `key`; the stack declares the `storage` component as derived (its entrypoint reloads `statics/`). A lite app rendered from an earlier skeleton keeps its landed baseline: renaming the column there is a forward migration, then `starci app sync --execute` renders the entity.
- Fixed (`lib/secret-patterns.mts`): the unquoted `assigned-secret` pattern skips a `--` comment line of a SQL file (`-- drop-identity-password-tokens: forward-only;` is a sentence); a quoted literal in a comment and every other file still match.


## 6.13.42 - 2026-11-18

- Changed (`knowledge/hfs/canon-pins.yaml`): @starci/jest-preset 2.11.1: a root package with its own `packages/<name>/tsconfig.json` is compiled by jest with that file's options in its own unit project (a package on standard decorators, such as an SDK, no longer meets the back end's `experimentalDecorators`); a package without one keeps the back end options. The rendered `be/jest.config.js` is unchanged. The `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.42.

## 6.13.41 - 2026-11-18

- Fixed (`rules/failure-class.mts`, R432 `BE_FAILURE_CLASS_BY_TEXT`): a regular expression literal is read in linear time. Its body let a backslash match either the escape or the plain-character alternative, so a literal full of `\u` escapes (a Vietnamese character class) backtracked catastrophically and hung `hfs structure`; the plain-character class now excludes the backslash.
- `starci app sync --execute` retires the three managed files an hfs before 6.13.33 wrote under `gallery/src` (`main.js`, `explain.js`, `style.css`) and removes the folder they leave empty; the dry run names each as `HFS_SYNC_DRIFT ... retired`. The thin gallery (`gallery/` = `vite.config.js`, `package.json`, `index.html`, `README.md` over `@starci/gallery`) is the one truth: sync never writes `gallery/src`, `app.gallery` and `HFS_APP_GALLERY_MISSING` never demand it.
- `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNWARMED` now names the one-round way: `npx --no-install starci app push`.
- `HFS_CLI_HFS_SKEW` (`core/app/skew.ts`): `starci app check|sync|audit|coverage|emit|codemod|release|ci` prints a coded line when the CLI is not the app's exact `starci-harness` pin, when the `@starci/hfs` it loaded is not the one it requires, or when the app's `node_modules/starci-harness` is not its pin (a stale install); `sync --execute` and `check --fix` are refused with exit 2 unless `STARCI_ALLOW_CLI_SKEW=1`.
- `starci app push [git push args]` (`core/app/push.ts`): runs `app check --env prod`, commits the cache records a real run writes as their own commit (never an amend, hooks run), repeats until a run writes nothing (3 runs at most), then `git push` once; refuses a tree with uncommitted work outside `.starcistacks/cache` (`HFS_PUSH_DIRTY_TREE`) and a check red for another reason.
- `project/gallery.md` PROJECT-GALLERY-1: the thin gallery has no `gallery/src`; an older CLI is the only thing that writes or demands it.
- `project/check-cache.md` operating procedure step 3: push with `app push`.
- `cli/README.md` "The starci CLI": which CLI judges an app, `HFS_CLI_HFS_SKEW`; `skills/starci/SKILL.md` one sentence.
- `hfs.json` accepts an optional top-level `publishes`: a non-empty unique list of workspace package folders relative to the app root (for example `"packages/nivoos-sdk"`), the packages `starci deps publish-local` puts on the local registry. A malformed list is a shape problem in the words of the declaration.
- `deps publish-local` also runs in an app whose hfs.json declares `publishes`: it publishes exactly those packages to the local registry with the harness rules (committed version, `starciSource` digest, `PUBLISH_LOCAL_VERSION_TAKEN`, the same `host up local-registry`), each built in dependency order after the app's own `npm ci`.
- New refusals: `PUBLISH_LOCAL_UNDECLARED` (a package of the app not named in `publishes`), `PUBLISH_LOCAL_DECLARATION_BAD` (a malformed list, a folder that is no package, a private package), `PUBLISH_LOCAL_NOT_INSTALLED` (no `node_modules` at the app root). `PUBLISH_LOCAL_NOT_HARNESS` now names the `publishes` key. The peer-line judgement is the harness's and is skipped for an app. npm publishing stays main-only.
- Consumers install them with `deps install-local <name>` as for any `@starci` package.
- The `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.41.

## 6.13.34 - 2026-11-18

- Fixed (`audit/judge.mts`, PROJECT-LINT-5): an audit exception's `via` names packages a path passes THROUGH, not only the roots it starts at: a finding is exempt when every lockfile path to the package passes through a `via` package before it (a second walk stops at every `via` package; a node it still reaches fails `AUDIT_HIGH`, naming the dependencies the bypassing paths come from). A fix is npm's `fixAvailable: true`, a non-major update, or a major update of the vulnerable package itself; a semver-major change of another package (npm moving a parent, often backwards) is no fix: it no longer raises `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_FIX_AVAILABLE` and is printed on the `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_ACTIVE` line. ## 6.13.32 - 2026-11-18

- Added (`audit/judge.mts`, `audit/cli.mts`, PROJECT-LINT-5): time-boxed npm-audit exceptions. The production audit step of the CI template and of the pre-push parity step is `npx --no-install starci app audit --omit-dev --level=<level>`: it runs `npm audit --omit=dev --json` and judges the report against `.starcistacks/security/audit-exceptions.yaml` (entries: exact GHSA `advisory`, `package`, `via` dependency roots, `reason`, `approvedBy`, `approvedAt`, `expires`). A finding at or above the level is exempt only when an active exception matches its advisory and package and every lockfile path to the package starts at a `via` root; anything else is `AUDIT_HIGH`. Codes: `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_EXPIRED`, `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_TOO_LONG` (over 14 days), `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_FIX_AVAILABLE`, `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_UNUSED`, `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_INVALID`, `AUDIT_REPORT_UNREADABLE` (errors) and `AUDIT_EXCEPTION_ACTIVE` (printed on every run). An exception needs the owner's approval; an agent never adds one. R269 (`HFS_AUDIT_STEP_MISSING`) accepts the new step and still refuses a swallowed one. The managed .gitignore tracks the exceptions file. The `@starci/hfs` self-pin is 6.13.32.

## 6.13.31 - 2026-11-18

- Fixed (`cache/built-packages.mts`, `cache/app-families.mts`): the types step of `starci app check` builds the workspace packages its programs resolve through a build output (an `exports`/`main`/`module`/`types` target no tracked file holds, such as `dist`) before the first `tsc`, dependencies first, each by its own `build` script, skipped while its stamp (tracked sources, the built packages it needs, the lockfile) is unchanged and its outputs exist. A program that reads such a package is keyed by that package's sources, so a stale record can no longer hide diagnostics. A failed build is `TYPES_PACKAGE_BUILD_FAILED` (one finding per package; its readers are not run).

A failure is `TYPES_CODEGEN_FAILED`. A fresh StarCi Next tree type-checks with no hand-run build or codegen (470 findings before, 0 after).
