## 6.5.2 - 2026-11-09

- Fixed: the delivery id of a resumed parked job is `<jobKey>:r<n>`, no longer `<jobKey>-r<n>`. The outbox check `job_outbox_dedupe_key_check` needs a `:` in every dedupe key, so the old form dead-lettered every `parked_jobs_sweep` resume. `resumedDeliveryId` and `jobKeyOf` (`platform/jobs/jobs.contracts.ts`) follow: `jobKeyOf` strips the one last `:r<digits>`, so a job key holding `:` is claimed intact; a job key must not itself end in `:r<digits>`. The job runner comment, the lite `jobs` and `jobs-failed-awaiting-fix` migration comments, BE-JOBS-7 and BE-RESILIENCE-2 carry the new form.
- Changed: `starci app codemod self-recovery` also rewrites a managed jobs file rendered by hfs 6.5.0 or 6.5.1 in the old `-r<n>` form (and left untouched since) to the current template. Run it, then `starci app sync`. A row in flight under an old `-r<n>` delivery id was never valid against the constraint, so no data migration is needed: the sweep resumes it again under `:r<n>`.
- Specs: `src/resume-delivery-id.spec.mts` (the form, the constraint pattern, the parse back to the job key including a key with `:`); `src/self-recovery-codemod.spec.mts` (the 6.5.x form is rewritten).

## 6.5.1 - 2026-11-09

- Added: `starci app check --env local` (with the `hfs` gate) reads the live dev stack after the tree checks, never cached and not asked of `--env prod` (PROJECT-STACKS-10, `stack/local-live-checks.mts`). `pending-migrations` (R393, `STACK_PENDING_MIGRATIONS`, error): every migration file the repository holds that the running dev database has not applied (the plan `starci app db migrate` would run, read from `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` by `docker exec` in the db container with the credentials it holds) is named, with `starci app stack up --env <env> --execute`; a database that cannot be read is a warning of the same code. `dead-letter-repeat` (R394, `STACK_DEAD_LETTER_REPEAT`, error): a queue or job with more than one `queue.delivery.dead-lettered` or `event-bus.delivery.dead` line in the last 24 hours of `logs/api.log` of the environment is named with its last error message. A stack that is not up, and a missing log, are not judged and say so in one line.
- Docs: R393 and R394 in the rule book (both copies), the two failure codes with their Vietnamese meaning, `contexts/project/stacks.md` PROJECT-STACKS-10 and `check-cache.md`; BE-SAGA-5, BE-SAGA-6 and BE-SAGA-7 (side steps do not block, an honest holding line for a customer, the resume ladder of an interactive run) with the owed enforcers R390 to R392.
- Specs: `src/stack-live-checks.spec.mts`.

## 6.5.0 - 2026-11-09

- Added: `starci app add connection <name> --owner <api|worker app> [--isolation database|schema] [--env-prefix <PREFIX>]`: one more database connection of a full app, so a second back-end app can own its own database (R174 refuses it composing another app's). It declares the connection in `hfs.json` `sides.be.connections` with every other byte of the file kept, writes `<name>.connection.ts`, `<name>.config.ts` and `<name>.decorators.ts` in `platform/database` (the files R84 asks for), exports them and gives the connection's EntityManager token a place in `database.module.ts`, adds `<PREFIX>_URL` (and `<PREFIX>_SCHEMA` for a schema connection) to every environment roster, declares the dev database (a native stack: the `databases` entry, its password key in custody and secrets, and the URL key in the `files` of the owner process; a compose stack: the compose secret and `<PREFIX>_URL_FILE` of the owner service) and the production secret `<prefix>-url` (declared with its versioned runtime name, granted to the owner service, mounted as `<PREFIX>_URL_FILE`, listed external in the Swarm model and sealed as a placeholder). It is a noun of its own: `add app` generates a front-end Next workspace and a connection is no app. Everything is planned before anything is written, the YAML edits keep the owner's comments, a duplicate name or an overlapping env prefix is refused (`HFS_ADD_EXISTS`, `HFS_ADD_CONNECTION_PREFIX`), a lite app refuses it, and what only the owner can do (creating the dev password, composing the connection in the owner app, its cli migrate list and test world) is printed as `owner step:` lines.
- Added: `local.databases` in the `local` block of `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml`: one entry per further connection, `{connection, urlEnv, password}` (the service, port and administrator are the `database` block's, the role and database default to `<project>_<env>_<connection>`). `starci app stack up` provisions each role and database beside the first, and a run receives a connection's URL as `<urlEnv>_FILE` only when it lists the key in `files`.
- Added: `starci app add webhook <name>` in a full app also lays down `platform/inbox` and `platform/idempotency` (the idempotency tree, with its TypeORM migrations), registers their entities, migrations, error kinds and messages in each app's `compose/platform.ts` and composes `InboxModule` over the primary connection in the api app's `webhooks` group, so a webhook intake can claim its delivery key on the `Inbox` port (R263 `webhook-inbox-key`). The plan is made whole before any write, and nothing is overwritten or composed twice. New `starci app add inbox` (no operand) does the same for a full app that already has webhooks and is a no-op when the capability is there; a lite app is unchanged and refuses it.
- Changed: the infra owner table (`ruleParams.be.infraOwners`) accepts the owner entry `slot:<be slot id>`, which makes every file of that slot an owner, and owns `@nestjs/websockets` by `platform/realtime` and `slot:be.feature.realtime.websocket`: the `<channel>.gateway.ts` door R212 requires and its spec import the socket decorators, every other file reports `BE_INFRA_OWNER`.
- Changed: `socket.io-client` is pinned for both sides (the realtime specs of a back end drive a gateway through it).
One auth package for both editions: `@starci/auth` 0.6.0 holds the lite sign-in, the full OIDC client and every provider and issuer, and `@starci/auth-oidc` is retired (0.3.0, a deprecated re-export).

- Added: `starci app codemod auth-merge` (`codemod/auth-merge.mts`): a dry run by default, `--execute` writes. Every quoted specifier of `@starci/auth-oidc` in an app's source (imports, re-exports, `import()`, `require`, `jest.mock`) becomes the entry of `@starci/auth` that holds it (the package and `/server` and `/contracts` -> `@starci/auth/oidc`, `/client` -> `@starci/auth/client/oidc`, `/testing` -> `@starci/auth/testing`), and every package.json that required `@starci/auth-oidc` requires `@starci/auth` at its canon pin instead (an older exact `@starci/auth` is raised to it; a range or a newer pin stays). A subpath with no new home (`AUTH_MERGE_SUBPATH`) and two imports of one module after the rewrite (`AUTH_MERGE_DUPLICATE_IMPORT`) are left for a person (exit 1). Installed packages and build output are not entered; a second run finds nothing. Report schema `starci/auth-merge-report@1`.
- Changed: R328 (`BE_AUTH_PASSPORT_OWNER`, both rule books): the identity libraries (`passport-oauth2` added) are the dependencies of `@starci/auth`, the one sign-in package of both editions, imported by no app file; `@starci/auth` and `@starci/auth/server` only by `integrations/passport` (lite), `@starci/auth/oidc` only by `integrations/keycloak` (full, which also composes the issuer presets, the GitHub Actions CI attestation among them: there is no separate integration), `@starci/auth/contracts` and `@starci/auth/testing` by those two glues, and the retired `@starci/auth-oidc` by no app file. `ruleParams.be.infraOwners` follows (slot manifest 3.26.0): `@starci/auth/server`, `@starci/auth/oidc`, `@starci/auth/contracts` and `@starci/auth/testing` rows, `passport-oauth2: []`, `@starci/auth-oidc: []` in place of its two keycloak rows; slot `be.integrations.keycloak` also admits `keycloak.attestation.ts`, the CI attestation port over `githubActionsIssuer`. The rule's full witness is `packages/eslint-canon-be/src/auth-full-owner.spec.ts` and its full example `packages/auth/src/oidc/index.ts`.
- Changed: the front-end stack's `auth` concern installs `@starci/auth` alone and forbids `@starci/auth-oidc` (`FE_STACK_FORBIDDEN`), so a full starter's front-end app depends on `@starci/auth` (its `./client/oidc` entry) instead of the retired package.
- Changed: the canon pins name `@starci/auth` 0.6.0 and no longer `@starci/auth-oidc`, add `passport-oauth2` 1.8.0 and `@types/passport-oauth2` 1.8.0, and name `@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.20.3 and `@starci/hfs` 6.5.0. `removed-vocabulary.yaml` lists `@starci/auth-oidc` (not scanned: the docs name it to say it is retired).
- Docs: `contexts/code/be/api-auth.md` BE-AUTH-2, `infra.md`, `contexts/code/fe/imports.md`, `transport.md`, `contexts/project/full.md`, `lite.md`, `packages/README.md` and the package READMEs.
- Specs: `src/auth-merge-codemod.spec.mts` (the entries; a dry run writes nothing; `--execute` rewrites imports, mocks and manifests and leaves `node_modules` and look-alikes; a second run finds nothing; the findings; the verb), `src/auth-package.spec.mts` (each entry's owners, the retired package owned nowhere, the front-end stack and the pins).


## 6.4.0 - 2026-11-09

- Added: `local.basePort` in the `local` block of `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml` (a whole port from 1024 to 65436: the project owns `basePort` .. `basePort + 99`), normalised by `normalizeLocal` as `LocalBlock.basePort` (null when absent). The authority for the role table is `runtime/scripts/hfs/stack-base-port.mts`.
- Added: R389, the role table of the native process ports of a dev environment that declares a base: the primary front end (the first process named `web`, `app`, `console`, `fe` or `frontend`) listens on base, the api (`api`, `core`, `be`, `backend`) on base+1, the AI gateway (`gateway`, `ai-gateway`, `ai-router`; base+2, the api port + 1 of the ai-router convention) on base+2, and every further native process (mcp, landing, further next apps, workers with a port) from base+3, or from base+2 when no gateway runs, up to base+99. Compose service ports are exempt. A departure is `STACKS_BASE_PORT_ROLE` (error); an environment with native processes on ports and no `basePort` is `STACKS_BASE_PORT_UNDECLARED`, a warning, so existing apps stay green.
- Added: `starci app stack doctor --env <env>` prints a `base port` row (the role table) and a `port range` row that fails when another app registered on the machine declares an overlapping base..base+99 for the same environment name. The apps come from the machine registry (`machine.sqlite` `ledgers`, retired ones excluded): each registered checkout's `application-stacks.yaml` and `hfs.json` name its base and project; this app and the lane worktrees of it are skipped, and a checkout that cannot be read is skipped (`stack/local-peers.mts`, the `peers` seam of `LocalDeps`).
- Added: the range 3900..3999 is reserved for the shared UI-lane `next dev` preview: the doctor prints a failing `port range reserved` row when `basePort`..`basePort + 99` overlaps it, and the scaffold never picks a base in it.
- Changed: the lite skeleton declares `basePort` in its dev `local` block (web = base, api = base+1); the scaffold fills it from the project name (`{{basePort}}`, `{{apiPort}}`: 3000, 3100 ... 4900).
- Docs: `contexts/project/stacks.md` PROJECT-STACKS-10 "One base port per app", R389 in the rule book (`contexts/hfs/rules.yaml` and the runtime copy `runtime/knowledge/hfs/rules.yaml`), the two failure codes with their Vietnamese meaning in `failure-codes.yaml`.
- Specs: `src/stack-base-port.spec.mts` (the parse; the role table for the nivo layout web 3100, api 3101, gateway 3102, mcp 3103, landing 3104 and the starci-next layout app 3000, core 3001, landing 3002; the gateway slot only when a gateway exists; base and base+1 reserved; violations; container ports exempt; the missing-base warning; the doctor rows; the machine registry lookup) and `src/stack-local.spec.mts` (the doctor verb end to end).
- Fixed (finding): `starci app add queue|job` in a full app declared `backing: ["redis"]` and added `bullmq` and `ioredis` but rendered no Redis, so the generated queue read `QUEUE_REDIS_HOST` (a required read) with nothing in the stack to answer it. It now declares the one persisted Redis, as lite does, without lite's password (full's `queue.config.ts` reads the address alone, so no sealed member and no grants): the stateful `redis` component of `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml` with its backup entry, restore record (`recovery/redis.yaml`), `deploy.volumes` entry and per-environment component; the `redis` service of every docker-swarm stack (`redis-server --appendonly yes --appendfsync everysec --maxmemory-policy noeviction`, a named volume at `/data`, the internal `backing` network, which a stack that already declares it for the database keeps) with `QUEUE_REDIS_HOST` and `QUEUE_REDIS_PORT` on every api and worker service; the images lock row; the same two keys in each `KEYS.md`; and, in a docker-compose dev environment, the loopback `redis` service on its own port with the volume `redis-data`, the queue address on the native `local.processes` that run the queue (or on the compose service that runs it, with the Redis first in the `local.services` order and on its network). Every edit keeps the owner's comments, is idempotent (a stack that declares a `redis` component is the owner's and is left alone) and is planned before anything is written (a stack with no api or worker service refuses `HFS_ADD_STACK_SHAPE`). `starci app sync` renders none of it: the stack declaration is the owner's file and only the `add` verb edits it.
- Fixed (finding): `starci app add queue|job` in a full app patched `platform/logging/index.ts` to export `./correlation.policy`, `./correlation.port` and `CORRELATION_RUNNER` / `InjectCorrelationRunner`, and wrote a queue tree importing `@modules/platform/ids`, in an app that had none of them, which broke `build:be` and the integration setups. It now checks, before it writes or patches anything, every `@modules/platform/<capability>` the tree it generates imports plus the logging correlation files and symbols, and refuses `HFS_ADD_PLATFORM_MISSING` naming each missing piece ("Nothing was written"); it lays none down itself because each also needs its module registered in the app's composition. Specs: a full app lacking ids and the correlation refuses with every file byte-identical; the trees the verb writes resolve every `@modules` import (TypeScript module resolution over the scaffolded app).
- Fixed: the dev compose file gains the lite and full `redis` service at the end of its `services` block through `YamlBlockEdit`, not before the first top-level `volumes:` line, which put it under `configs:` when a compose file lists `configs:` before `volumes:`; a lite stack with that shape now parses to what it was plus the service.
- Added: `YamlBlockEdit.prepend`; an `environments.<env>.backup` block that is missing is opened instead of failing; a `backing` network or a service that already joins it is not declared twice.
- Specs: `src/hfs-add-queue-full.spec.mts` mirrors `hfs-add-queue-lite.spec.mts` for full (the Redis in the production stack, the dev compose file, the manifest, the keys and the images lock; the same persistence judgement R276 applies to lite's Redis passes on it; `add job` and a second `add` change nothing more; an api and a worker; a native dev process; a stack with no queue service refuses before writing; an owner-declared `redis` component is left alone; an app without a stack; `prepend`).
- Not changed: R276 (`STACKS_LITE_REDIS_PERSISTENCE`) stays lite-only (`editions: [lite]`): a full app may run a cache Redis that evicts or use a managed Redis, so judging a full Redis service needs its own rule and failure code. `starci app add` still writes at once: no document says it is dry until `--execute`, its README says it writes, and the generators `add`, `remove` and `scaffold` write their trees directly while the dry-run standard of cli/README.md names `sync`, `codemod` and `upgrade`; a dry run for `add` is a decision for the CLI standard, not a change inside this package.
- Changed: x `@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.20.2); the docs `api-auth.md` BE-AUTH-2, `infra.md`, `full.md` and the package READMEs name both owners.


## 6.3.1 - 2026-11-08

- Changed: R350 states the card inset: 16 is the card's own inset alone, the value of `--grammar-inset-card` (Grammar 0.27.0), never a gap; the canon pin of `@starci/stylelint-canon` is 2.8.0, which accepts it there and nowhere else.
- Changed: `STACKS_PORT_PUBLISHED` accepts `mode: host` for the declared ingress service (the component named by `ingress.component`) on published ports 80 and 443, besides `mode: ingress`. Ingress mode goes through the Swarm routing mesh, which rewrites the client source IP, so every client reaches the app as one address and a per-IP rate limit becomes one site-wide bucket; host mode keeps the real client IP behind the single proxy hop. Every other service keeps the old rule: it publishes nothing. A host-mode ingress listens on every node it runs on, so the same code is raised unless the ingress is `deploy.mode: global` or carries a `deploy.placement.constraints` entry.
- Specs: `src/hfs-stacks-swarm.spec.mts` (the ingress in host mode on 80/443 passes with a constraint or in global mode, fails replicated and unpinned; the ingress-mode case still passes; a non-ingress service in host mode fails). Docs: `contexts/project/stacks.md` "PROJECT-STACKS-4", R266 in the rule book.
- Fixed: `starci app codemod common-base` deletes the unit spec of every file it turns into a pure re-export (`outcome.mapper.spec.ts`, `record.policy.spec.ts`, `sequence.policy.spec.ts`, and a spec beside any other file it rewrites): the logic they specified lives in `@starci/common`, and a spec of a re-export failed `BE_SPEC_FORM_ALL_ROLES` (`SUBJECT` given an object). A dry run lists the deletions; a file the app still owns (no longer declaring what `@starci/common` owns) keeps its spec.
- Fixed: the same codemod re-exports only the names the app imports. A name no file of the app imports (`DomainErrorInit`, `OutcomeErrorClass`, an `Outcome*` member, `isDomainError`) is left out of the re-export and removed from the `platform/errors` and `platform/primitives` barrels that passed it on, so `HFS_UNUSED_EXPORT` no longer names it; a file none of whose names is imported keeps its template form. The skeleton follows: `errors.contracts.ts` re-exports `ErrorKind` and `ErrorParams`, `outcome.contracts.ts` re-exports `Outcome`, and the skeleton no longer carries the three specs of re-exports (the lite queue starter no longer copies `sequence.policy.spec.ts`).
- Fixed: `HFS_EMPTY_DIR` and `HFS_GHOST_TREE` skip a directory git ignores (`git check-ignore`), such as the Sonar scanner's own `.scannerwork/.sonartmp/<n>`; a leftover git does not ignore is still reported.
- Changed: the canon pin of `@starci/common` is 1.0.1 (CommonJS and ESM over one class identity, `params` and `cause` accept `undefined`, `ErrorParams` is a named interface).
- Specs: `src/common-base-codemod.spec.mts` (the spec of a re-export is deleted and a dry run reports it; a custom file keeps its spec; only imported names are re-exported and the barrels are pruned; `pruneBarrel`) and `src/hfs-check-tree-slots.spec.mts` (a git-ignored empty directory is no finding, a real leftover still is).

## 6.3.0 - 2026-11-08

- Changed: the managed `cache-verify.yml` (slot `app.ci-cache-verify`) is sharded like `ci.yml`: a matrix with one job per family the cache holds records of (`hfs`, `secrets`, `format`, `db`, `wiring`, `types`, `test`, `build`) and the `lint` family once per side, each `starci app check --env prod --fresh --gate <family>`, then asserts the cache folder is unchanged; a `verdict` job is green only when every shard is. Each job's timeout is `timeoutVerify` (the largest of the lint, test and build timeouts), so a nivo-sized app no longer runs one whole fresh check against 180 minutes. The workflow no longer skips a dispatch off the default branch: `workflow_dispatch` runs for the head of any branch, and the concurrency is per ref.
- Changed: the release gate's verify lock reads the verify runs of the default branch and those of the release's own sha on any branch (`gh run list --commit`); the newest finished one decides. A green verify of the release sha (dispatched on `dev` or a candidate) clears `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_VERIFY_LOCKED` for that sha; a newer red verify of the default branch, or a red one of the sha, locks it. `readLock` of `releaseGate` takes the sha. The gate's `app-check` row stays diff-only against the trusted cache, `npm audit` stays a live row, and Sonar on the main project and the L4 record stay real.
- Specs: `src/cache-verify-workflow.spec.mts` (the shards; `app sync` renders the sharded workflow for lite, full and root-package apps; no default-branch guard; per-ref concurrency), `src/release-cache-verify-lock.spec.mts` (a green verify of the same sha clears a red main verify; a newer red main verify locks again; the gate asks for the sha's runs).
- Added: `@starci/common` (1.0.0) joins the canon pins (side both) and the starter dependencies of the full and lite back ends. The skeleton re-exports it: `platform/errors` (`domain.error.ts`, `errors.contracts.ts`) re-exports `DomainError`, `isDomainError`, `DomainErrorInit`, `ErrorKind` and `ErrorParams`, and `platform/primitives` re-exports `Outcome`, `ok`, `refused`, `unwrapOutcome`, `isRecord` and `eachInOrder`, so `@modules/platform/errors` and `@modules/platform/primitives` imports stay as they are.
- Added: `starci app codemod common-base` rewrites an app's own `DomainError`, `ErrorKind`, `ErrorParams`, `DomainErrorInit`, `Outcome`, `isRecord` and `eachInOrder` files of `platform/errors` and `platform/primitives` into re-exports of `@starci/common`, adds the dependency, and lists a root package that still declares `starci.errorBase`. Spec: `src/common-base-codemod.spec.mts`.
- Changed: a root package's `starci.errorBase` key is no declaration any more: `readPackageContract` stops the lint on it, and the package's errors extend `DomainError` of `@starci/common`.
- Changed: the pins follow the packages that moved to the shared error base (`@starci/auth` 0.5.0, `auth-oidc` 0.2.0, `ai-router` 0.6.0, `mail` 0.2.0, `object-storage` 0.2.0, `event-stream` 0.2.0, `test-world` 1.4.0, `nest-kit` 0.2.2, `jest-preset` 2.9.0) and the canons (`eslint-canon-be` 3.20.0, `eslint-canon-fe` 9.16.8, the Grammar family 0.25.3).
- Changed: `starci app release gate` judges the work once. The rows `app check` already judges (`audit`, `format-check`, `typecheck`, `build-be`, `build-fe`, `lint`, `db-lint`, `test` with its coverage threshold, `db-drift`, each under its default command) run no command when the `app-check` row of the same run is green: they print `REUSED <row> (reused from app-check ...)` and their L4 record entry carries `reused: true` and `reusedFrom` (the gate, the head, the `app-check` command, its log and sha256). The `db-drift` row runs when the `app-check` log carries `CI_PARITY_DB_SKIPPED`; a red `app-check` row stops the gate; `--full` still runs that row as `--fresh` and the reuse is the same. The printed plan states for each row whether it reuses or runs, and how (`reuses` in `--json`). Before, the test row re-ran every spec after the governed `app-check` row, as a raw child under node's default heap (exit 134, `JavaScript heap out of memory`).
- Changed: a row that still runs a heavy command (the world layers, an app's own rows, the Sonar scanner on the main project key; also the fast checks of `release push --urgent`) runs through the governor the CLI hands in (`stackIo.heavy`, request kind `test` or `build`: a heavy slot, the heap cap of its kind, and for a test row the workers and worker heap its room holds), never as a raw child. `starci` verbs and `npm audit` run plain. Without a governor (the package on its own) every row is a plain child. `runGate` is asynchronous. `HeavyRequest.kind` gains `test`.
- Specs: `src/release-gate-reuse.spec.mts` (a green `app-check` is the verdict of the covered rows and no child runs for them; the record names the run; the plan; `--full`; a red `app-check` stops the gate; no database leaves the drift row to run; an app's own command under a covered name runs, governed; the Sonar row on the main key and the tag still run, the scanner governed).
- Fixed: an integration spec now counts as a consumer of its own integration in a per-provider slot (`be.integrations.<provider>`, such as keycloak or mail), not only in `be.integrations` with a `<provider>` binding. R112 (the spec references the error enum) and `HFS_UNUSED_EXPORT` no longer contradict each other. Specs: `src/hfs-unused-symbol.spec.mts`.
- Changed: the `undici` pin is 6.29.1 (6.29.0 carries GHSA-2m5v-pch6-74hx and four denial-of-service advisories that fail `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`), and the canon pin of `@starci/auth-oidc` is 0.2.0.
- Changed: the canon pin of `@starci/test-world` is 1.4.0 (the stack mail sink, `stacks: { mail: {} }`), still paired with `@starci/jest-preset` 2.8.1.
- Added: `src/managed-templates-canon.spec.mts` renders the whole managed set `app sync` writes (`renderRepo`, a lite and a full app), lints every back-end and front-end TypeScript target with the real built `@starci/eslint-canon-be` / `-fe` with `fix: true`, and asserts the autofix changes no render and nothing remains to report. Run against the templates before the canon migration (`03d1d0e0^`) it names exactly the 17 files an app saw drift on (the five `domain/identity` services and the twelve `features/api/auth/application` handlers); the templates and generators of 6.2.6 / 6.2.7 already render the canon's output, so no template changed.
- Fixed (finding): an app whose `starci app sync` reports drift on files `app check --gate lint --fix` just merged was synced by a CLI whose installed `@starci/hfs` (`cli/node_modules`, 6.2.3) predates the canon migration of the templates, not by 6.2.6 or 6.2.7 (the npm tarballs of both carry the migrated templates byte for byte). Reinstall the CLI's dependencies (`npm ci` in `cli/`) so the sync renders the pinned hfs.
- Fixed: the committed records of the architecture machine, the unused-symbol analysis and the boot proof named the host's absolute paths (the folder judged, the resolved TypeScript, every file the proof loaded), so two checkouts of one commit wrote different bytes and each other checkout and CI found the cache unwarmed. Their data keeps each path under the app root as `<app>/...` and reads it back as this checkout's (`portableData`, `hostData` of `cache/records.mts`). Spec: `check-cache-portable-records.spec.mts`.
- Fixed: a unit spec's record is reused in every checkout of its commit. The digest of the Jest project configuration in its closure key held the app root's path (the preset, transform and setup files resolve into the app's install), so a clone in another folder, a worktree or a CI runner ran every spec again; the app root is now a placeholder like the Jest root (`projectConfigHash`). Spec: `check-cache-test-config-hash.spec.mts`.
- Fixed: a repository whose origin is a local Windows path (`C:\work\repos\app`) is named by its last segment (`repositoryName`), so the README title (`HFS_README_TITLE_INVALID`) and the Sonar credential's repository binding (`CI_SECRET_SEALED`) of such a clone hold. Spec: `repo-identity.spec.mts`.
- Fixed: a fresh lite scaffold passes its own `starci app check`: its templates are Prettier-clean, the web catalogs hold no key the pages stopped reading, the mail barrel exports only what the app imports, `mail.config.ts` refuses a bad `MAIL_SECURITY` or `MAIL_DEFAULT_LANGUAGE` with the config error of the key (`EnvSource.enum`), the passport configuration keeps no unit spec (a configuration is no logic role), the returned-rows policy spec reads its errors without an `any`, the README cites only paths a fresh app holds, and no `db/seed.sql` is written (R332).
- Fixed: a declared sealed member holds the keys its environment's KEYS.md roster maps to its path again (the roster row's pattern had lost its escapes), so the scaffold seals the db service's `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` instead of a slug-named `DB_PASSWORD` no reader uses (`ENV_SEALED_UNREAD`). Spec: `sealed-member-keys.spec.mts`.

## 6.2.10 - 2026-11-08

- Fixed: a pure component that hands a ReactNode prop on as it is (`(props: P): ReactNode => (props.active ? props.candidate : null)`) is a render target for `FE_WORLD_OWNER_RENDER_BOUNDARY` and `FE_CONNECTED_BLOCK_RENDER_PAIR`. The detector required the pure half to return JSX, which contradicted the canon's `starci-fe/no-useless-fragment` (the fragment that wrapped the prop is now forbidden). A function counts when every return is JSX, null/undefined/false or a read of its own first parameter (a prop, a destructured prop, or a conditional/logical of these), at least one is such a read, and it still uses no world and captures no outer function; a module constant, a hook value or a call result still fails.
- Specs: `src/fe-render-target-passthrough.spec.mts` (lite and full).

## 6.2.9 - 2026-11-08

- Changed: `starci app check` runs its steps one after another, one heavy step at a time, in the priority order of `CHECK_GRAPH` (the unit run, lint, the structure pass, tsc, wiring, then the rest as their needs allow), and each step sizes its children on the RAM and cores free when it starts. The dependency graph run in parallel (admission in priority order, CPU lanes held per step, the launch wait of `LAUNCH_WAIT_MS`) is gone from `cache/schedule.mts`: a unit run that started beside lint, hfs, tsc and wiring held one lane and ran `--maxWorkers=1` on 1932 specs with 38 GB free, and it now takes `clamp(floor((room - parent) / worker), 1, min(cores - 1, testWorkers))` workers from the room at its own start and holds no lane. The journal and the cache are unchanged (a fully reused step costs about no time).
- Changed: the types step runs its tsc programs together as many as the room at its start holds of the tsc heap cap (`STARCI_TSC_HEAP_MB`, handed over by the CLI) and the free lanes allow (`concurrency` of `runTypesFamily`), where it started every changed program at once and let the governor queue them; the lint step sizes its ESLint chunks from the room at its start the same way.
- Specs: `src/check-schedule.spec.mts` (steps run in priority order and never overlap; a step starts after the one before ended; the unit run on a 38 GB, 12-core room takes more than one worker after other steps and holds no lane; a named worker cap is kept; a wholly reused step costs no time) and `src/check-cache-types-family.spec.mts` (tsc programs run together up to the concurrency asked once).

## 6.2.7 - 2026-11-08

- Fixed: the canon pins move to `@starci/eslint-canon-fe` 9.16.7 and `@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.19.7, whose test-scope Sonar way block no longer uses a negated `ignores` selector that `canonScope` refuses (every app lint ended in `unsupported canon ignores selectors`).
- Added: `src/lint-canon-scope.spec.mts` loads the built flat config of both canons, as an app's `be`, `fe` and `packages` configs compose it, and runs `canonScope` over it: it must not throw, and a source file and a spec file must both be in scope.

## 6.2.6 - 2026-11-08

- Changed: `HFS_CANON_PIN_DRIFT` is report-only in `app check --env local` for a pin whose exact version the app's lockfile resolved from a registry on this machine (`starci deps install-local`), not for a spec that spells `-local.<n>`: `deps publish-local` publishes the version committed in git, so a local build has no distinguishing version. A range pin, or the same version resolved from npm, is a real drift.
- Changed: the verb catalog (`runtime/packages/cli/src/catalog.generated.mjs`) carries the texts of `deps publish-local`, `deps install-local` and `deps check` of CLI 1.1.0-alpha.93.

## 6.2.5 - 2026-11-08

- Fixed: a whole `starci app check --env prod` crashed with `HFS_MANIFEST_INVALID: internal error: finding code CI_PARITY_DB_SKIPPED has no failure-code catalog entry loaded` on a host with no Docker and no `--db-url-env` database: the warning of the skipped database migration checks had no entry in `runtime/modules/kernel/failure-codes.yaml`. The entry is added beside `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SKIPPED`, and the spec `src/check-report-why.spec.mts` now fails when any `CI_PARITY_*` code the parity family exports or emits has no catalog entry.
- Changed: `FE_CONNECTED_BLOCK_RENDER_PAIR` and `FE_WORLD_OWNER_RENDER_BOUNDARY` accept the forwarded children nested between the tags of a resolved pure component (`<XBase props={...}>{props.children}</XBase>`, or the destructured `{children}`) as a render path, beside the attribute form `children={props.children}`. The one child must be `children` of a parameter of the connected component; any other element or expression between the tags still fails. FE-FUNCTION-2 writes the nested form, which Sonar S6748 and `react/no-children-prop` accept. Specs: `src/fe-forwarded-children-render-pair.spec.mts`.
- Changed: the canon pins move to `@starci/eslint-canon-fe` 9.16.6 and `@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.19.6 (the Sonar way mirror: one import per module, the server's nested-conditional reading, the unicorn and core rules the Sonar way profile fires, a test-scope block over specs, and `throw-domain-error` judging a rejection like a throw with a closed list of framework control-flow signals) and to `@starci/jest-preset` 2.8.1 (the lcov names sources from the repository root with forward slashes, so Sonar resolves root package files). Every template under `templates/` is the canon autofix output of the new rules (one import per module, error-typed catch names, arrow callbacks); the frozen `templates/snapshots/` are unchanged so the self-recovery codemod still recognises untouched files. Spec: `src/check-cache-test-family.spec.mts` expects the root-relative lcov source.

## 6.2.3 - 2026-11-08

- Fixed: the Sonar scan of `starci app check` on a server without branch support (Community) no longer reads the main project first. A derived project `<key>-local` the token can analyse (`api/qualitygates/project_status` answers 200, which a project analysis token may read) is scanned as it is, with no read of the main project and no gate binding; only a missing derived project is created, which needs the token to read the main project and to create projects and select a gate, and without that the scan is skipped naming the remedy (create `<key>-local` and bind it to the gate, or give the token Create Projects). The gate digest of the scan key falls back to the gate's name when the token cannot read its conditions, and to no reuse when it reads neither: the scan still runs. The verdict of the derived project's gate is read with the scan's own token. Specs: `src/check-cache-sonar-scan.spec.mts`.

## 6.2.2 - 2026-11-08

- Added: `starci app check --env local|prod` (default `local`). Both environments judge the same code checks (hfs, format, db, wiring, lint, types, tests, coverage, build, audit stay identical and blocking; a lint, type or test error is red in both). They differ only in the findings about whether the run is fit to commit, push or release: the codes marked `localOnly: true` in `modules/kernel/failure-codes.yaml` (`HFS_CANON_PIN_DRIFT` of a pin naming a `<version>-local.<n>` build, `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNPORTABLE` of a local-registry install without a source digest) are listed `LOCAL, REPORT ONLY` and never red in `local`, and the run ends `gate: green for local (not pushable)` when only they remain; `prod` blocks them. The one list is `runtime/scripts/hfs/check-env.mts`; a producer marks the instance a local-registry build alone causes (`local: true`). The managed `.husky/pre-push`, `ci.yml` and `cache-verify.yml` templates and the release gate's `app-check` row run `--env prod` (run `app sync --execute`). Specs: `src/check-env-source-key.spec.mts`, `src/check-gates-parser.spec.mts`, `src/release-cache-verify-lock.spec.mts`.
- Changed: the check cache keys a harness or tool package on its source. A package packed by `release publish` or `deps publish-local` carries `starciSource` (the digest of its tracked source files) in its manifest; the `hfs` and `pkg:@starci/*` fingerprint parts, the install digests of a record's reads and the lint import graph use it in place of the version and integrity when present, so the `-local.<n>` build and the npm build of one commit yield identical keys and the records of a run on the local build stay valid after `deps install-local --restore`. A local-registry install whose package carries the digest is portable; one without it, and a linked or copied folder, is not.
- Changed: `HFS_DEV_BRANCH_MISSING` (a whole `app check` of an app repository with no `dev` branch, locally or on origin) is an error in both `--env local` and `--env prod`, and PROJECT-BRANCH-1 says an app must keep `main` and `dev`. The managed pre-push hook keeps one bootstrap path: the first push into an empty remote must push `main` and `dev` together at the same commit; `main` alone is refused `BRANCH_MAIN_NOT_ON_DEV`. Specs: `src/dev-branch.spec.mts`, `src/pre-push-branches.spec.mts`.
- Added: the export `@starci/hfs/tool-cache` (`toolCacheDir`), the untracked tool cache of an app, which the CLI's `deps install-local` keeps its record in.

## 6.2.1 - 2026-11-08

- Changed: `app check` starts its critical path first. Each step of `CHECK_GRAPH` declares a static `priority` (the unit run, then lint, the structure pass, tsc, wiring, the rest); ready steps start highest first, and a child asks the governor, and takes its lanes, only once every ready or running step of a higher priority has had a child admitted, has ended or `LAUNCH_WAIT_MS` passed. The jest run is sized on the whole room, not on what lint and tsc children left, and an inline step waits only for higher-priority child steps. A step wholly reused from the cache asks no slot. Specs: `src/check-schedule.spec.mts`.

## 6.2.0 - 2026-11-08

- Fixed: a whole `starci app check` crashed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'title')` after a Sonar scan that was skipped (no token, server or permission): the catalog explanations were read only for `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SCAN_FAILED` and `CI_PARITY_SONAR_GATE_FAILED`, so the warning `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SKIPPED` reached `withWhy` with none, and the run ended with no run record and no cache flush. The check reads the explanation of every code the scan returns, and `withWhy` throws an internal error naming a code that has no catalog entry loaded. Spec: `src/check-report-why.spec.mts`. A crash in the report writes no cache and no run record; the journal keeps the judged families (`contexts/project/check-cache.md` "The journal").
- Changed: the memory plan the CLI hands an app check holds the check's parent heap and the lint chunk, no per-side heaps or workers (the CLI sizes a test run at admission from the live RAM), so the out-of-memory clause names the parent heap, and the died-runner finding names the governor's `testRun` caps.
- Changed: `app check` runs its families as the steps of one dependency graph (`cache/schedule.mts` `CHECK_GRAPH`): coverage and the baselines after test, the lint ratchet after lint, build after types, lint, test and wiring (its codegen and build:modules rewrite what they read), the Sonar scan after every other step but the ratchet; everything else runs at once. Each step starts the moment the steps it needs ended, whatever their verdict, so a red family stops no other. A step that throws ends the run with its error once the others ended.
- Changed: each heavy child of a step goes to the governor on its own and takes CPU lanes from the run's one pool of cores - 1; a test run asks for the free lanes as its most workers (`HeavyNodeRequest.workers`) and gives back what its room did not size (`onAdmit`). A step whose work is in-process starts once the running child steps have their children admitted.
- Changed: a side's ESLint judged files make ceil(files / `plan.lintChunk`) chunks and run as many children at once as the room holds of the eslint cap and the free lanes allow, at most the chunks (`eslintParts`); each child streams to a journal file of its own (`journal(folder, kind, context, part)`). The tsc programs and the lint sides run at once.
- Added: the steps table of the report and `steps` of `--json`: per step its wall time, the measured RAM peak of its children (`HeavyNodeResult.peakMb`), its parallelism, its family's reused and checked counts and where its results live; the run log records each family's seconds. Every progress line and child label names its family (`app check <family> ...`); a terminal rewrites a running line in place only while one step is open.
- Removed: `runAppFamilies`, the sequential app pass; the structure, format, db and wiring families are steps of the graph (`cache/run.mts`).
- Changed: the check cache keys every reused result on what its tool read. A unit spec's record carries the installed packages its run loaded (recorded by `@starci/jest-preset` 2.8's runtime) and the app files outside its closure, so a package bump re-runs only the specs that loaded it and an hfs or CLI bump re-runs none; `lock:test` is the test runner's toolchain (Jest, its transform, TypeScript) and `node` (the Node major) is a part of the unit specs. A `tsc` program's record carries the installs and files `tsc --listFiles` reported, in place of `lock:types`. The boot proof keys on the back-end side and the packages its workers loaded. The hfs, secrets, wiring and db families read the hfs that runs, `hfs.json` and the manifests' shape; no family reads the CLI version, a manifest's dependency versions, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes` or `turbo.json`, and `sonar-project.properties` and `codecov.yml` are sonar's alone. Cache format 2: the first run after the upgrade is full.
- Added: the CI-parity build steps keep their verdict (green, or red with its finding) in `.starcistacks/cache/check/build/`, keyed on the step's command and environment, the Node major, the files it builds and the lockfile installs its manifests resolve; the database migration checks on the migration chain, the scratch image, the entity list's import closure and its packages, the engine manifest, the db rules' baseline and the hfs version. A killed, timed-out or database-less step stores no verdict.
- Changed: the unit run turns each spec's coverage file into its record while Jest runs and removes it, so the record is the one copy. Specs: `check-cache-test-family.spec.mts`, `check-cache-types-family.spec.mts`, `cache-fingerprint-families.spec.mts`, `check-cache-precise-keys.spec.mts`.

## 6.1.1 - 2026-11-07

- Fixed: the bundled canon pins name the canons and the Grammar family at the versions whose `@starci/hfs` peer is `~6.1.0` (`eslint-canon-be` 3.19.4, `eslint-canon-fe` 9.16.4, the Grammar family 0.25.1), so an app on hfs 6.1 installs with `npm ci`; `contexts/stack/pins.yaml` agrees.

## 6.1.0 - 2026-11-07

- Added (R388): an app keeps exactly two long-lived branches, `main` and `dev` (`contexts/project/branches.md` PROJECT-BRANCH-1). The managed `.husky/pre-push` hook (`slots.yaml` 3.25.0: run `app sync --execute` to render it) now hands the bundled push gate the remote it pushes to and refuses a push of `main` whose commit is not reachable from `dev`, the local one and the remote-tracking one or the dev pushed with it (`BRANCH_MAIN_NOT_ON_DEV`; a missing dev refuses too, except the first push of `main` into an empty remote), a push of `main` that is not the checked-out `HEAD` (`BRANCH_MAIN_NOT_CHECKED_OUT`: the hook's check judges the checked-out tree), and a push of `dev` that deletes it or does not contain `main`, local, remote-tracking or pushed with it (`BRANCH_DEV_NOT_ON_MAIN`). A push of `main` or a `v*` tag runs the plain `app check` with `STARCI_JUDGED_BRANCH=main`, judged as `main` (the full gate, `judgedBranch` of `branch-profile.mts`) whatever branch is checked out, so the branch profile of `dev` never applies to it. The managed `ci.yml` plan step refuses a push of `main` whose commit is not on `origin/dev`.
- Added: a whole `starci app check` warns `HFS_DEV_BRANCH_MISSING` for an app repository with no `dev` branch, local or `origin/dev` (`runtime/scripts/hfs/dev-branch.mts`); the harness repository (`kind: runtime`) is never asked.
- Added (R387): a branch profile loosens old debt only, never new code. Where a profile reports lint, an ESLint error on a line changed since the merge-base with `main` (the diff the Sonar new-code prediction reads, `newCodeSince` of `cache/app-families.mts`) is the blocking `LINT_NEW_CODE_FINDING` while a finding on an untouched line stays report-only; where it reports sonar, the predicted new-code coverage of the changed lines below the minimum `newCode.coverage.minPercent` of the Sonar gate `main` enforces is the blocking `COVERAGE_NEW_CODE_BELOW`. The Sonar server's gate and the Codecov upload stay report-only, the lint ratchet stays, and `main` runs the full gate unchanged (`cache/new-code.mts`). Both codes are plain errors, never marked report-only.
- Fixed: the local Sonar scan of a whole `app check` on a profile branch was skipped whenever the run held a report-only finding (old lint debt); a finding of a family the profile reports no longer makes the run red for that decision, so the scan runs as a report.
- Added: failure codes `BRANCH_MAIN_NOT_ON_DEV`, `BRANCH_DEV_NOT_ON_MAIN`, `BRANCH_MAIN_NOT_CHECKED_OUT`, `HFS_DEV_BRANCH_MISSING`, `LINT_NEW_CODE_FINDING` and `COVERAGE_NEW_CODE_BELOW`; rules R387 and R388. An app migrates by creating `dev` from `main`, pushing it and running `app sync --execute`.
- Changed: `starci app check` (and `--gate lint`) is green only with zero error findings. The threshold is gone: a run with fewer than 20 error findings no longer exits 0, and no code is a "hard blocker" any more, since every error blocks. The verdict is one rule over the error findings that the branch profile does not report (`gateVerdict` in `runtime/scripts/hfs/gate-verdict.mts`, formerly `gate-threshold.mts`): `gate: red, N error finding(s)` or green with no line. `GATE_ERROR_LIMIT`, `gateErrorLimit`, `STARCI_GATE_ERROR_LIMIT`, `GATE_THRESHOLD_RULING`, `HARD_BLOCKERS` and the `hard` field of the report's `gate` are removed. Specs: `src/gate-verdict.spec.mts`, `src/check-eslint.spec.mts`.
- Fixed: a run that does not run the Sonar scan prints one `sonar scan:` line saying why (the run selects families, the branch profile turns sonar off, another family is red, no token or server, no properties or Sonar service). The check's `predictions` lines, which carried the scan's lines, were never printed in the text report; `printCheckOverview` prints them. A scan that runs prints its start, the project key it scanned, its duration and the gate status. Specs: `src/check-eslint.spec.mts`, `src/check-cache-sonar-scan.spec.mts`.

## 6.0.5 - 2026-11-07

- A patch: the check cache treats a `@starci` package whose lockfile `resolved` names a registry on this machine (a `starci deps install-local` install) as having no portable fingerprint, so a run under a local install writes no cache and the push gate's `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNPORTABLE` names `starci deps install-local --restore` as the remedy (`localReasonOf` in `cache/fingerprint.mts`, specs `cache-fingerprint-families.spec.mts`, `check-cache-freshness.spec.mts`). The generated verb catalog follows the CLI's registry (`deps source` gone, `deps install-local` added).

## 6.0.4 - 2026-11-07

- Changed: a deploy of a `database.target: stack` database takes the first start whenever the database is empty, not only when no release was ever accepted: with an accepted release it reaches the running database first and, when it holds no migration history and no table in `public`, runs the hold deploy, the emptiness proof, the migrations and the real deploy (`firstStartReason` in `stack/db-phases.mts`; the deploy prints why it chose it). A database that cannot be reached still takes the expand path, which refuses it.
- Added: `STACK_DEPLOY_DB_FOREIGN_CHAIN`. A migration preview that meets recorded versions the app's chain does not explain (another chain, such as the one a declared `db.baselineReset` replaced) is refused before any backup, migration or deploy, naming the first versions; emptying the database is the app's own operation, so the refusal and `STACK_FIRST_START_NOT_EMPTY` say the app empties it itself and the next deploy takes the first start (no hand `docker volume rm`). `db migrate`'s red preview carries `unexpected` in its data.
- Added: `database.appPassword: { member, key }` in application-stacks.yaml. After the migrations, the first start gives the app role (hfs.json `db.appRole`, default `app_be`) that sealed value: opened in memory, checked as the generated password kind (`STACK_FIRST_START_APP_ROLE` otherwise), sent to psql on stdin inside the database container as the cluster's own role, and redacted from every message; it is never in argv, a file or a printed line.
- Added: failure codes `STACK_DEPLOY_DB_FOREIGN_CHAIN` and `STACK_FIRST_START_APP_ROLE`.

## 6.0.3 - 2026-11-07

- A patch: `app check` keeps what it has judged while it runs. Every family's records are journaled the moment the family finishes, and inside a family the types family journals each program, the test family each spec's coverage as the reporter writes it, and the lint family each batch of 250 files the ESLint list runner judged (the child appends them to the journal file the family opens for it). The journal is host state in the untracked tool cache (`journal/` beside the run log), one file per run, kind and content key, so parallel writers never share a file. A run killed or crashed at any moment therefore keeps the finished families and the judged part of the running one: the next plain run serves the entries like records when their fingerprint digest (and, for lint results, the digest of the side key, the cross-file inputs, the installed packages and the base) still matches, drops a line the kill cut short, and runs a family the fingerprint would run in full against them when they were journaled under today's parts. A run that ends compacts each replaced folder into the committed files and removes its journal; the committed files are written by a temp file and a rename. CI writes and reads no journal, the cache verdict (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FORGED`, stale, missing, unwarmed) judges what the flush wrote as before, and `--fresh` ignores the journal. Specs: `src/cache-journal.spec.mts`.
- A patch: a raised pin no longer lints the whole back end again. The back-end canon's `third-party-import-owner` declares the app root's `package.json` as a content input of every source file, so a bump of `@starci/hfs`, `starci-harness` or any library in that manifest changed the cross-file input of all files and ESLint judged every one of them (15 minutes over 9549 files) although the rule reads dependency names, workspaces and local references only. The app root's and a side's own manifest now stand in a content input by its identity (`manifestIdentity` of `cache/lint-family.mts`): each dependency's version range reduced to a workspace, file or link reference, an alias or a plain registry range. A new, removed or renamed dependency, a `workspaces` entry or a reference still dirties every file that reads it. Specs: `src/check-cache-lint-manifest.spec.mts`.

## 6.0.2 - 2026-11-07

- A patch: the types and test families leave the tools of the other families out of their lockfile parts. `lock:types` and `lock:test` hashed every resolved install, so a bump of `@starci/hfs`, `starci-harness`, an ESLint or stylelint package or Prettier re-ran the type check, the unit specs, their coverage and the Sonar prediction although no program or spec loads those tools. Both parts now cover every install a program or a spec can load: the ESLint and stylelint packages with their `@starci` canons, Prettier and its configuration, `@starci/hfs` and `starci-harness` are left out unless a workspace manifest declares them or the resolved dependencies of a declared package name them in `package-lock.json`; `typescript`, the libraries and every other install stay in. A manifest that cannot be read leaves nothing out. A bump of `@starci/hfs` or the CLI therefore re-runs the hfs, secrets, wiring and db families alone, a lint package re-runs lint and sonar, Prettier re-runs format, and a library re-runs types and test. Specs: `src/cache-fingerprint-families.spec.mts` bumps each tool and a library in turn and names the families each part reaches.

## 6.0.1 - 2026-11-07

- Added (R386): a whole `starci app check` runs the app's CI sonar job against the app's own Sonar server. The scanner (`@sonar/scan`, as `app release gate` runs it) reads the app's `sonar-project.properties`; the server and the token's custody are those of its stack declaration (the token is opened from the sealed secrets and reaches the scanner through its environment alone); the quality gate is the one the server holds for the project, read after the analysis. The scan runs after every other family and the coverage step are green, from the run's own linter results (`reports/lint.sonar.json`). It never overwrites the project's own analysis: a server with branch support receives it as the branch `local/<user>`, a server without (Community) as the derived project `<projectKey>-local`, created with the main project's visibility and bound to the main project's quality gate when the token may. A scanner failure is `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SCAN_FAILED` (it quotes the scanner's last lines, such as `Line 15 is out of range ... File has 14 lines`), a red gate or a failed compute-engine task `CI_PARITY_SONAR_GATE_FAILED`, both errors. A missing token, an unreachable server, a token without the permission the derived project needs or an unreadable verdict is the warning `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SKIPPED`, never a green; an app with no `sonar-project.properties` or no Sonar service gets an info line. A green verdict is kept in the untracked tool cache (`sonar-scan.json`) by the scanned content, the target, the scanner version and the digest of the gate's conditions, so an unchanged tree is not scanned again; `--fresh` scans again.
- Added: failure codes `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SCAN_FAILED`, `CI_PARITY_SONAR_GATE_FAILED` and `CI_PARITY_SONAR_SKIPPED`; rule R386; `sonarGate` of the release gate is exported for the check to share one Sonar connection.
- Changed: R383 states the database migration checks as part of the build parity family (reported as `CI_PARITY_DB_SKIPPED` where the host has no Docker or database).
- Added: R235 lists the lint rule `no-line-separator-literal` of both canons (a raw U+2028, U+2029 or U+0085 in a file, which the Sonar scanner counts as a line break and TypeScript does not, so the analysis aborts with "Line n is out of range"; the finding names the escape to write). The canon pins move to eslint-canon-be 3.19.3 and eslint-canon-fe 9.16.3.

## 6.0.0 - 2026-11-07

- A major: one rule book. The rule catalog (`knowledge/hfs/rules.yaml`) has no `cases` and no `amendedBy`: each rule's one-line law states the rule as it holds now, and `rule-catalog.mts` refuses either key by name. The design truths cite the rule each entry rests on (`cite: {rule: <rule id>}`; any other cite is a malformed manifest), and the digests of the manifest, the loosening fixtures and the vendored YAML live in `knowledge/hfs/design-truths.pins.yaml` (`readTruthsPins`, held to the files by `src/loosening-state.spec.mts`). R274 states the one-rule-book law; `modules/kernel/command-policy.yaml`, which no code read, is deleted. `modules/kernel/removed-vocabulary.yaml` gains kind `grammar`, the Grammar names the harness contexts no longer use. Comments, knowledge, failure-code texts and printed messages cite rules by their ids and carry no ruling history; stale `.mjs` citations name their `.mts` sources. App migration: an app's own `policy/loosening.yaml` exception or branch profile that cited a ruling (`kind: ruling`, `decision: <id>`) cites a rule instead (`kind: rule`, `cites: <rule id>`); the harness CLI refuses the old form.

## 5.13.10 - 2026-11-07

- Changed (breaking for CLI callers, no alias): plain `starci app check`, with no flag, is the one run everywhere: every family diff-only against the cache, the CI-parity families (`build`, `audit`), CI's coverage step and the cache verdict (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNWARMED`, `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNPORTABLE`, `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`), which stays a hard error. A run with a selector (`--gate`, `--side`, `--coverage-from`, `--fix`) judges those gates alone. The managed pre-push hook runs plain `app check`; the managed CI and nightly templates follow.
- Changed: `--fresh` is the one escape hatch for a broken or suspect cache: the selected families (all when none is selected) ignore their records, are recomputed from scratch and written back. It replaces `--no-cache`.
- Removed: `--no-cache`, and the environment variable `STARCI_APP_CHECK_CACHE` (`full`, `verify`) with the in-process comparison it ran. The nightly `cache-verify.yml` runs `app check --fresh` and fails when the committed records are not the ones the cold run produced; the `ci.yml` dispatch input `full` is gone; `app release gate --full` runs the `app-check` row as `app check --fresh` (the other rows reuse the records it wrote). `slots.yaml` is 3.24.0 (the managed hook and CI templates change; `app sync --execute` renders them).
- Changed: the `build` parity family also runs the build job's `database migration checks` step as CI runs it (`npm run db:lint && npx starci app db drift`), so `BE_ENTITY_SCHEMA_DRIFT` (a manifest that lacks the CHECK constraints a migration creates) is found by a local run. A host with no Docker and no `--db-url-env` database gets `DB_DRIFT_NEEDS_DATABASE` from the verb, reported as the warning `CI_PARITY_DB_SKIPPED`; any other failure is `CI_PARITY_STEP_FAILED`.
- Fixed: the `build` parity family exports `SWC_NATIVE_BINDING_CACHE` on Windows at a short folder per app under the user profile (`<profile>\sw\<8 hex of the repo path>`), unless the caller set one. The SWC native addon's lock under the tool cache was longer than MAX_PATH and failed the front-end parity builds with `ERR_SWC_NATIVE_CACHE` (os error 3), and a drive-root folder fails the addon's DACL check.
- Changed: a unit spec whose closure changed on disk during the run (a merge or a codegen mid-run) is still failed, and its `JEST_SPEC_FAILED` message says "closure changed during the run (merge or codegen mid-run); run the check again".

## 5.13.9 - 2026-11-07

- A patch: `app db drift` no longer reports the CHECK constraints a list partition inherits from its partitioned parent. The catalog query for CHECKs filtered on `conparentid = 0`, which does not exclude an inherited CHECK, so `records_data_check`, `records_entity_check` and `records_version_check` of the parent `records` were reported as `BE_ENTITY_SCHEMA_DRIFT` on every `records_<entity>` partition (`check-undeclared`). The query now reads only CHECKs local to their table (`pg_constraint.conislocal`), as the index query already skips inherited indexes; a CHECK added on the partition itself and missing from the manifest is still reported. Spec: `src/db-drift-docker.spec.mts` migrates a partitioned parent with two CHECKs and a partition with one local CHECK in a real postgres.

## 5.13.5 - 2026-11-06

- A patch: a SQL migration has its own read budget. The tree checks refused any file over 1 MiB (`MAX_READ_BYTES`), so a baseline reset, which is one file by design, was reported "not a readable SQL file" and every database rule saw nothing in it: the baseline header, the pgmq queue creates, the scope role and the destructive-ruling proof were reported missing. `readText` now gives `db/migrations/*.sql`, `db/migrations/archive/*.sql` and `db/bootstrap.sql` the explicit budget `MAX_MIGRATION_READ_BYTES` (16 MiB, documented in `rules/read.mts`); every other file keeps 1 MiB. A migration over 16 MiB is still unreadable. Spec: `src/hfs-read-migration-budget.spec.mts`.
- `DB_APP_ROLE` reads a role created inside an idempotent DO block. A baseline creates `app_be` and `app_engine` as `DO $$ BEGIN IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'x') THEN CREATE ROLE x ...; END IF; END $$`; the rule read only a top-level CREATE ROLE, so the scope role and app role of such a baseline looked never created. That exact guarded shape (the guard names the created role, no else branch) is now read with the same attribute checks as a top-level statement (LOGIN/NOLOGIN, NOINHERIT, BYPASSRLS and the administrative attributes refused). A DO block that creates or alters a role through a dynamic EXECUTE is refused as unreadable. Specs: a guarded block passes, a guarded block with a forbidden attribute is refused, a dynamic EXECUTE block is refused.

## 5.13.2 - 2026-11-05

- Added (real database isolation; R256 `DB_POLICY_TAUTOLOGY`, BE-PERSISTENCE-19): hfs.json `db.scopeSettings` lists the transaction settings an app sets per request to enter a scoped database role (for example an engine role entered by a SECURITY INVOKER function that sets `nivo.workspace_id`). A policy for any role but the application role now passes when it reads a declared setting with `current_setting`, directly or through a SQL function whose body reads it. An undeclared setting, a literal true, `OR true`, `IS NOT NULL` and a self comparison are still refused. A policy that relies on a declared scope principal must compare it with the owning column (`db.scopeColumns`, default `workspace_id`), `column = principal` on either side; `id = principal`, another column, `<>` and `IS NOT NULL` tests are refused. A declared setting counts only in a policy whose roles are all in `db.scopeRoles` (a list of `{ role, reason }`, required with `scopeSettings`): the same predicate `TO authenticated`, `anon`, an undeclared role or a mixed-role list is refused, and each scope role must be created NOLOGIN NOINHERIT NOBYPASSRLS by a migration and never altered to gain LOGIN or administrative attributes (`DB_APP_ROLE`). The declaration shape accepts only dotted lower-case names and never `request.jwt.*`.
- Added (HR-COORD-2, the engine-owned records model): hfs.json `sides.be.engineOwned` = `{ capability, tables, partitionsOf, views, manifest }` declares the database objects one engine capability owns: base tables, the partitions of one of them and its views (a prefix pattern such as `v_*`), built by generated migrations. R86 `BE_SQL_TABLE_OWNER`: only that capability reads or writes them (any other capability's SQL naming one is refused; without the declaration the engine's own writes to `records` were refused as another capability's table). R35 `BE_SCHEMA_OWNER`: an `@Entity` of another capability over an engine-owned table, partition or view is refused; no entity per partition or view is required. R333 `BE_ENTITY_SCHEMA_DRIFT` (`starci app db drift`): TypeORM `EntitySchema` instances in the entity list are compared like classes, and the partitions, views, CHECK constraints and indexes of the engine-owned objects are compared both ways with the entity manifest the declaration names; a missing or malformed manifest is no verdict (`DB_DRIFT_NO_MANIFEST`). The key sits on the be side beside `dbFunctions` and `sqlPassThrough` because the architecture checks read the be side view.
- Added (R255 `DB_APP_ROLE`): a declared scope role (`db.scopeRoles`) may be granted only to the application role; a `GRANT <scope role> TO` any other role, `authenticated` or `PUBLIC` is refused.
- Added (HR-CORE-BE-3, R34): hfs.json `sides.be.ddlRenderers` = `[{ path, reason }]` declares the root-package files (`packages/<name>/...`, app-root relative, a `*` only in the file name) that render DDL text for `cli entities plan --write`; the runtime merges it into `ruleParams.ddlRenderers` of the be view, which eslint-canon-be 3.19.1 reads in `no-runtime-schema`.
- Added (Nivo migration history reset; R213, R257): hfs.json `db.baselineReset` = `{ baseline: "db/migrations/<ts14>_<name>.sql", ruling, date, reason }` declares a one-time reset. While the baseline is not yet on the base branch, `DB_MIGRATION_SHAPE` (removed-migration immutability and ordering against the old chain) and `DB_ARCHIVE_MUTATED` (archive immutability) accept the removal of every older active file and of the archive; the baseline must carry `-- starci:baseline-reset <ruling>`, exactly one file may carry that marker, no older active file may stay and later migrations must sort after the baseline (`DB_BASELINE_SHAPE`, `DB_MIGRATION_SHAPE`). Once the baseline is on the base the declaration is spent; without the declaration nothing changes. The landed-migration downgrade only reports, so it needed no change.
- A patch: the runtime the canons read changed compatibly, so the canons and the Grammar family are not released.

A failed step is `CI_PARITY_STEP_FAILED`. `--gate build` and `--gate audit` run them alone. `db:lint` and `app db drift` stay CI-only (they need a database).
