## 5.10.1 - 2026-11-05

- Fixed (release 7c.5.1): the managed CI builds an app's root workspace packages (the agent SDK, the module packages and every other `packages/*` workspace) before every step that reads their types or compiles code importing them, with the app's managed `build:modules` script (`npm run build:modules --silent`): the fast job before its boot proof (`app check --gate wiring` ran ahead of the typecheck that alone built them: `BE_APP_BOOT_PROOF_MISSING` and `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FORGED`), every side of the lint matrix in "build the lint inputs" (an unbuilt package read as `any`: `no-unsafe-*` and `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FORGED`), each build of a multi-front-end app, the Sonar job before its scan, the nightly `cache-verify.yml` and the full edition's `e2e.yml`. An app without root packages renders exactly as before.
- Added: R301 `CI_STANDARD` item `packages-built-first` (knowledge/hfs/ci-standard.yaml `rootPackages`): in an app with root packages (its lint matrix names `packages`, or its package.json declares `build:modules`) a step that reads them follows a step of its job that builds them. Specs: `ci-standard.spec.mts`, `ci-commands.spec.mts`.
- Fixed: a cache commit no longer rewrites `.starcistacks/cache/check/hfs/root.jsonl`. The cross-file `rule:` records were keyed on the digest of the whole tracked path list, the cache folder's own record files included, so every commit that added or removed a record file staled them and the next run (the pre-push check after `app check --fresh` and its cache commit) rewrote them: a record that depended on its own commit. The digest now leaves out the cache folder's paths, and a cross-file rule that read a cache file is judged live. Spec: `check-cache-structure-keyed.spec.mts`.
- Added: a branch profile may cover the feature branches that merge into a profile branch by a pattern (`branches: { "ui/*": { gates: { lint: report, sonar: report } } }`). A pattern is a literal first segment, then `/` and segments in which `*` stands for any characters but `/` (GitHub's push-filter meaning); it never starts with `release/`, and R378 `HFS_BRANCH_PROFILE_INVALID` refuses one that matches the default branch (main, master or `ci.github.defaultBranch`) as it refuses naming it. A branch takes its own entry, else every pattern that matches it, merged gate by gate to the stricter mode; the default branch never takes a profile. `app check --profile` names the pattern (`branch profile ui/* (a pattern covering ui/3)`), the lint ratchet compares with the branch's own base and still blocks on the touched files, hfs and the safety gates block as always, and the rendered CI runs the reduced plan on a push to a covered branch. The policy fallback outside the CLI is `[dev, ui/*]`.
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.10.0 - 2026-11-04

- Added: branch profiles. An app declares `branches: { <branch>: { gates: { lint|sonar|codecov: blocking|report|off } } }` in `.starcistacks/application-stacks.yaml`; hfs and the safety gates (types, wiring, db, secrets, format, test) stay blocking on every branch, and the default branch can never be declared (R378 `HFS_BRANCH_PROFILE_INVALID`, a hard blocker; a refused profile lowers nothing). The profile follows the branch (`GITHUB_BASE_REF`, so a PR into main runs the full gate, then `GITHUB_HEAD_REF`, `GITHUB_REF`, else git). A `report` gate runs, writes its records and prints its findings, which never reach the verdict; `off` does not run; each lowered gate prints one notice, and `app check --profile` prints the profile.
- Added: the lint ratchet (R379 `LINT_RATCHET_RAISED`, a hard blocker): where lint reports, a touched file may not gain findings over its base (the merge-base with `STARCI_BASE_REF` or `origin/<branch>`, else `HEAD^`; `app check --ratchet` against HEAD for the pre-commit hook) and a new file must be clean; both versions are linted as texts in one ESLint child per scope.
- Changed: the managed pre-commit hook runs the staged linters where lint blocks and `app check --ratchet` where it reports; the rendered CI runs on pushes to profile branches, where a reported Sonar analysis or Codecov upload runs but only a full run judges its quality gate; R251 accepts exactly the declared profile branches.
- Fixed: a committed cache file stays well under what a git host takes: an area whose records pass 32 MB is split one folder deeper, and a single folder that still does into hash buckets (`<area>/~<n>.jsonl`); Nivo's test-coverage records were one 366 MB `be/src.jsonl`, which GitHub refused (GH001). Readers read every `.jsonl` of a folder, so the old file is still read and is replaced on the next write.
- Added: the pre-push `app check --fresh` refuses a cache file over `STARCI_CACHE_FILE_MAX_MB` (90) as `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`, a hard blocker, before anything uploads.
- Changed: the rendered CI's unit run takes one more shard per `suitesPerShard` (150) spec files over the standard's 6, so a 1,676-suite back end runs in 12 shards inside the test job's timeout.
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.9.1 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: the rendered CI's lint job outlasts the ESLint child of its largest side: its `timeout-minutes` is the CLI's liveness budget of that child (max(eslint minutes, minutes per 1000 files x the side's tracked script files), handed to sync as `STARCI_ESLINT_TIMEOUT_MINUTES` and `STARCI_ESLINT_MINUTES_PER_1000_FILES` by `starci-harness` 1.1.0-alpha.67) plus `lintOverheadMinutes` (20) of the CI standard, never below the default 30; a declared `ci.jobs.lint.timeoutMinutes` still wins. An 11,346-file back end renders 77 minutes. The warm-cache path is unchanged.
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.9.0 - 2026-11-04

- Added (release 7b+8, self-recovering flows, BE-RESILIENCE-2): `platform/queue` hands a parked job back to its queue (`QueueRedelivery`); `platform/jobs` parks a job a retry cannot fix as `failed_awaiting_fix` with one alert (`FlowAlerts`), resumes it at a new build, on the `parked-jobs:sweep` tick and on demand (`ParkedJobResumerService`, `ParkedJobResumeRequestsService`, lite `cli queues resume [--job <id>]`), and keys a remote effect by `JobClaims.effectKey` (`<jobId>:<step>`) claimed in the inbox, so a pivot never repeats after a resume. R377 `BE_FLOW_FAILURE_SWALLOWED` refuses a flow file that catches a failure without rethrowing or parking it, and any `removeOnFail`.
- Changed: a failure is classified by `classifyFailure(cause, DomainError)` of `@starci/nest-kit` 0.2.0, the one rule of the job runner, the lite pgmq consumer and the full event runner; an event consumer's failure a retry cannot fix goes to its dead letter at once under its class. `platform/queue` no longer renders `queue.failure.ts`.
- Added: `starci app codemod self-recovery`, a three-way move of an app's jobs, queue and event-bus platform onto the self-recovering flows against the versioned template snapshot this package ships (`templates/snapshots/5.8.3`): a file equal to the snapshot's rendering is rewritten, the templates added since are added, a lite app gains the `jobs-failed-awaiting-fix` migration once, and a hand-edited file is left for a person.
- Fixed: a full app's `add queue` and `add job` compile: the queue's clients (bullmq, ioredis) at their pins, the correlation exports, and a full `QueueTransport.persist` that resolves once each job is held by its queue (`QUEUE_JOB_LOST` otherwise).
- Changed: the auth-lite `modules/auth` index no longer re-exports `EmailDoorName`, which no app reads through the owner; the skeleton hook names its door from `sendEmailDoor`.
- Changed: slots 3.23.0 (the park, resume and awaiting-fix database functions of `platform/jobs`), and the canon pins name this release and `@starci/nest-kit` 0.2.0.

## 5.8.3 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: the test family reports a runner that dies (`runnerDeath`: a V8 heap line, or an abort exit, while specs had not reported) as one `JEST_RUNNER_DIED` finding with that line and the count of specs it never reached; those specs are no longer `JEST_SPEC_FAILED`, a spec that reported its failure still is, and the threshold is not judged on the short total. A coverage run's parent heap is the CLI's `heavy.memoryMb.test` (8192 from `starci-harness` 1.1.0-alpha.65).
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.8.2 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: the rendered lite sign-in passes the canon it ships with: `identity-email.service.ts` (`ReadonlyArray<MailLanguage>`, an optional chain), its spec (`mock<MailOptions>({ ... })`, no assertion) and `identity-mail.messages.ts` (`ReadonlyArray<string>`). The template canon checks (`scaffold-template-canon-be`, `-fe`) lint with the canon's whole config as the rendered `eslint.config.mjs` builds it, its borrowed typescript-eslint, stylistic and Sonar way rules included, so a managed file and the canon can no longer contradict each other.
- Fixed: `HFS_UNUSED_SYMBOL` (R280) reads a TS catalog (`*.messages.ts`) through the function of the same file that draws its copy; only the catalog's object declarations are metadata, so a key read as `copy.subject` is no longer reported.
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.8.1 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: a root workspace package's client (`src/client/**`) and its type modules (`src/**/*.types.ts`, `src/**/*-types.ts`) are outside the back end's unit run, beside its workbench, public face and barrels: the rendered `be/jest.config.js`, `sonar-project.properties` and `codecov.yml` exclude them (BE-TEST-13). R273 (`HFS_COVERAGE_BELOW`) parses each such type module and refuses one that emits code (a value, a function, a value import), naming the first statement.
- Changed: the canon pins name this release.

## 5.8.0 - 2026-11-04

- Added: a migration already on the base branch, byte-identical at the merge-base (origin/main, else main, or the base `app check` is given), is landed and immutable (R213): its `DB_MIGRATION_DESTRUCTIVE` and `DB_DYNAMIC_DDL` findings become known items (level info, `landed` names the base) while it stays unchanged; a changed landed file still blocks and a new file is judged in full (`rules/database-landed.mts`).
- Added: `hfs.json` `db.destructiveRulings` (`{ migration, objects, reason, date }`): a destructive ruling accepts exactly those objects of one migration outside a contract migration while the contract proof (no be/src reference now, none in the previous release) holds, else `DB_DESTRUCTIVE_RULING_UNPROVEN`, a hard blocker; the db family keys on `hfs.json`.
- Fixed: `HFS_DEPLOY_SCHEDULER` accepts the managed `cache-verify.yml`, the one workflow R251 lets schedule.
- Fixed: `app.starcistacks` places `.starcistacks/images/<name>/**` in both editions, the image folder of a customised service (PROJECT-STACKS-11), and the stacks gate reserves `images/` as no environment (`HFS_STACKS_SHAPE`, `STACKS_UNDECLARED_ENVIRONMENT`).
- Fixed: `HFS_SERVICE_PLACEMENT` (R163) reads a root workspace package's manifest `packages/<name>/package.json` as no service root; a Dockerfile or a nested manifest under `packages/` still is one.
- Fixed: `STACKS_IMAGE_ENV_DIVERGES` (R374) reads the project image the deploy renders (`<registry>/<owner>/<project>-<name>@sha256:...`, its placeholder digest included) as the folder's image.
- Changed: slot `be.integrations.passport` requires the capability module (`passport.module.ts` over `passport.module-definition.ts`, the app logger handed to `@starci/auth`) in place of `passport.registration.ts`, and the lite template renders it; `starci app codemod passport-module` moves an app (slots 3.22.0).
- Changed: the runtime law R304 says a root workspace package declares its zod contracts in any file (`@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.16.0); the canon pins name this release.

## 5.7.1 - 2026-11-04

- Added: a seed entry declares how it learns of drift. `mode: always` runs an idempotent, cheap command on every stack up; an `upsert` entry's optional `probe` (an app command as an argv list, given the entry's input) exits non-zero on drift and runs the entry even when its hash is unchanged; the hash skip stays the default (manifest `starci/seeds@1`, additive). The receipt keeps each run's outcome (`unchanged` or `restored` from the command's last output line, otherwise `applied`) and never any other output; a probe that cannot run is `STACK_SEED_PROBE`, and a probe on a once, always or files entry, a probe that is no argv list, or `always` on a files entry is `HFS_SEED_MANIFEST` (PROJECT-STACKS-12).

## 5.7.0 - 2026-11-04

- Changed: the check cache fingerprint is judged per family: a part makes only the families that read it run full, and the diagnosis names the family and the part. `package-lock.json` counts per family by its toolchain's resolved installs, `package.json` by shape outside the hfs-run families; a bump of `@starci/hfs` never re-runs ESLint, and a library bump re-lints only the files that import it, through the package digests of the import graph. The types and test families still run full on a change of their toolchain's resolved installs.
- Added: `starci app check --fresh`, run by the managed pre-push hook, refuses a push whose committed cache a real run on its tree changes (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNWARMED`) or a host that cannot write a portable cache (`HFS_CHECK_CACHE_UNPORTABLE`, naming `deps source --npm`).
- Changed: the release gate runs every row diff-only against the warm cache (`app release gate --full` is the owner's opt-in), and a red or unreadable nightly verify refuses it with `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_VERIFY_LOCKED`; CI trusts a release candidate or tag at its merge-base with main. `HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FORGED` stays a hard blocker at any count.
- Added: the managed `.github/workflows/cache-verify.yml` (slot `app.ci-cache-verify`, slots 3.20.0), the nightly `STARCI_APP_CHECK_CACHE=verify` on the default branch; `ci.yml` reads the trusted cache on every event and goes full only on a dispatch with its `full` input, never passing `--no-cache`; R251 and `CI_TRIGGERS_RELEASE_ONLY` admit that one schedule.
- Added: R376 `HFS_BOOT_LOGGER_SILENCED`: an app root's `main.ts` never boots Nest with `logger: false`, so a DI or bootstrap failure prints on stderr and exits non-zero instead of ending every command silently (BE-CLI-1 rewritten).
- Changed: the lite app CI's fast job runs the R272 boot proof (`starci app check --gate wiring`) before typecheck, and ci-standard item `boot-proof` requires it, so a provider a domain module needs and an app root does not compose fails CI, not every boot.
- Upgrade: `npx starci app sync --execute` renders `cache-verify.yml`, the pre-push hook and `ci.yml`; replace `logger: false` in `be/apps/cli/src/main.ts` with the template's logger and error handler; then run `npx starci app check` once and commit `.starcistacks/cache/` (the new lock parts make lint, types and test run full once).

## 5.6.1 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: the OpenClaw runtime layer recipe (`stack/images/openclaw-runtime`) starts as root only to give the gateway's state folder `~/.openclaw` to `STARCI_RUNTIME_USER` (named once, in its Dockerfile) when that user does not own it, then drops to the user with `setpriv`; a bind folder the host created as root no longer crash-loops the gateway with EACCES.
- Fixed: moving an installed copy's legacy `runtime/.runtime` out keeps each entry's owner and mode: it renames the entry, or on another volume copies it with mode, uid and gid restored.
- Changed: the R348 law names the written `PaneGrid` child of `Page chrome="panes"`.

## 5.6.0 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: an installed or locally sourced copy keeps its host state outside itself. A stack's bind-mounted data, logs and receipts, `machine.sqlite` and the blob store live in the per-user base `installedStateBase` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%`, else `$XDG_STATE_HOME`, else `~/.local/state`, then `starci-harness/state`; a stack's state keyed by project and environment below it), so `npm ci` and a package copy no longer delete a running stack's data; a checkout keeps `<root>/.runtime`, and `STARCI_LOCAL_ROOT` overrides both.
- Added: the first `starci app` verb moves the state a copy kept in `runtime/.runtime` to that base (resumable, an entry the base already holds kept aside as `legacy-<stamp>/`), and refuses `HFS_STATE_LEGACY_HELD`, naming `starci app stack down --env <env>`, while a running stack holds it.
- Changed: the canon pins name `@starci/auth` 0.4.0 and `@starci/auth-oidc` 0.1.3.

## 5.5.1 - 2026-11-04

- Fixed: a seed `data` or `test-account` command run by `app stack up` gets the environment the app's api process gets in that environment (`runEnvironment`: its declared env, the sealed `_FILE` pointers and the database URL of the app role), beside the seed-secret values on stdin; it ran with an empty environment, so an app's seed CLI could not start.
- Fixed: `ENV_SEALED_UNREAD` counts a `{"secret": "seeds-<name>"}` reference in seed data as a read of that member (a shared-level reference reads it in every environment).
- Fixed: `HFS_STACKS_SHAPE` accepts the shared seed level `.starcistacks/seeds/`: the reserved folder names of `.starcistacks/` are one list, `RESERVED_STACK_FOLDERS` (`cache`, `seeds`), which the stacks gate reads.
- Fixed: R187 (`HFS_DOCKER_BUILD_CONTEXT`) refuses a Next image's `COPY be/contracts` only when the back end holds no contracts (the COPY would fail the build); when it holds them, the COPY is the app's choice for each image, neither required nor refused.

## 5.5.0 - 2026-11-03

- Changed: the back end's unit run holds the root packages (`packages/<name>` with a `package.json` and a `src/`, never the registry): their specs run in `npm test`, `app check --gate test` and the CI shards, and their logic is measured at 100 per file (the workbench, the public face and the barrels aside) inside the Sonar and Codecov scope. `test:modules`, the fast job's module-packages step and the per-package `jest.config.js` are removed; `starci app sync` renders the change (PROJECT-3, BE-TEST-13).
- Fixed: each lint scope runs on its own: a side whose ESLint child does not complete is a tool error of that side (exit 2), and the next side and the root packages are still judged; a host hold still ends the run. The ESLint liveness budget of a diff run scales with the files of the side, whose typed program the child builds, not with the length of the diff.
- Added: `app check --gate lint --side packages`; an app with root packages lints them in a job of the CI lint matrix (`[be, fe, packages]`) with their own Sonar report, and the release gate's lint report does the same.
- Added: a root workspace package declares its own error base and result union in its `package.json` (`starci.errorBase`, `starci.outcome`), read by the root-packages view (`readPackageContract`).
- Fixed: `app sync` renders the root `eslint.config.mjs` and the module registry (`tsconfig*.json`, `src/template`, `src/manifest`) as the canon Prettier config prints them, so an app's prettier hook passes them and formatting them is no longer `HFS_SYNC_DRIFT`; the managed lite sign-in templates are Prettier output.
- Added: hfs.json `"modules": "data"` declares that an app keeps its agent modules as data (`.starcistacks/seeds/data/modules`): `app sync` renders no registry package `packages/modules` (one the app keeps is its own code), and `app add|remove module` refuse `HFS_MODULES_ARE_DATA`. An app without the key keeps the module-packages render.
- Added: seed levels `.starcistacks/seeds/` (every environment, first) and `.starcistacks/<env>/seeds/`, each a `seeds.yaml` (`starci/seeds@1`) with `sql/`, `data/` and `files/` and entry kinds `sql`, `data`, `test-account` and `files`, judged by R375 (`HFS_SEEDS_SHAPE`, `HFS_SEED_MANIFEST`, `HFS_SEED_DDL`, `HFS_SEED_DATA_SCHEMA`, `HFS_SEED_TEST_DATA`, `HFS_SEED_SECRET_REF`); `HFS_SEED_PII` also reads JSON and YAML seed data; a production level holds test data only in its one declared `test-account` entry. New apps are scaffolded with the three levels.
- Added: the seed step (`stack/seeds.mts`): receipts in `public.starci_seed_receipts` (scope, id, content hash, secret member names with a digest), `once` and `upsert` entries, secret references (`{ "secret": "seeds-<name>" }`) opened in memory with the env's age identity and passed to the app's seed command on stdin, never to disk, argv, logs or receipts; `files` entries are checked only. `app stack up` runs it after the migrations, and the deploy after convergence, its dry run listing the entries (PROJECT-STACKS-6, PROJECT-STACKS-12).
- Changed: `db baseline --squash` writes `.starcistacks/seeds/sql/reference.sql` and declares the entry `reference`; the scaffold's `cli seed run` reads the shared then the env `sql/` folder (the lite one takes `--env`); slot `app.db.seed` is removed and R332 refuses `db/seed.sql`.
- Fixed: `app codemod sops-rules` keeps the comment header `.sops.yaml` opens with (the managed header of `secret init`).
- Fixed: a fresh `app scaffold --edition full` passes its own check: the dev environment declares the `local` block `app stack up --env dev` runs, and its runbook names the `starci app stack` verbs instead of docker commands.
- Fixed: the Next image of a front end that reads `be/contracts/` copies it only when the back end holds it; R187 (`HFS_DOCKER_BUILD_CONTEXT`) refuses a COPY of a missing `be/contracts` and a missing COPY once contracts exist.
- Changed: the lite sign-up door takes an optional full name and keeps it as `full_name` and `name` in the account's profile data, and the scaffolded sign-up form asks for it; the lite account directory and the Google sign-in read it back into the session (`AccountStanding.name`, `@starci/auth` 0.3.0).
- Changed: the canon pins carry no release-age wording (a version, security fixes included, may be adopted as soon as it is published), and name `@starci/auth` 0.3.0, `@starci/auth-oidc` 0.1.2, the Grammar family 0.21.17, `@starci/eslint-canon-be` 3.15.0, `@starci/eslint-canon-fe` 9.13.0 and `@starci/jest-preset` 2.7.0.
- Upgrade: (1) `npx starci app codemod seeds-layout`, read the report, then `--execute`: it moves `db/seed.sql` and loose `.starcistacks/<env>/seeds/*.sql` into the seed levels and writes their manifests; move any secret out of seed data into a `seeds-<name>` member and declare production's one test account as a `test-account` entry. (2) An app that keeps its modules as data adds `"modules": "data"` to hfs.json. (3) An app whose back end emits no contract deletes `COPY be/contracts be/contracts` from its `fe/apps/*/Dockerfile`, which the app owns. (4) `npx starci app sync --execute` renders the jest packages scope, the CI lint matrix and the Prettier-clean managed files.
- Changed: slot `app.packages` places any root workspace package `packages/<name>/` (the agent SDK, a block library such as `packages/blocks-core`, a module package, later a reviewed plugin package), not only `agent-sdk` and `module-<name>`, so each is linted by the root config (`--side packages`) and read as a first-party package by the import graph; slot manifest 3.19.0.

## 5.4.0 - 2026-11-03

- Fixed: `starci app sync --execute` never leaves an app uncompilable. Before it writes a managed TypeScript file or deletes a retired one, it compiles each front-end and back-end project with the change applied (the app's own TypeScript; the changed files and every file importing them, against what is on disk). A managed group whose render adds a type error, in its own files or in an app file that imports one, is held whole: `HFS_SYNC_GROUP_HELD`, exit 1, naming the group, its files and the first errors (what the app must add or still imports); the rest is written, and a side without TypeScript installed is named in a notice (`sync/compile-guard.mts`).
- Fixed: `starci app codemod module-definition` drops every imported name the rewrite made unused (`ConfigurableModuleCls`, `ConfigurableModuleHost`, `ConfigurableModuleBuilder`), keeps the names the file still uses and `import type`, keeps the builder's JSDoc, and writes each file as the app's Prettier prints it, so no TS6133 and no prettier-hook refusal follow it (`codemod/app-prettier.mts`).
- Changed: `starci app codemod account-doors --contract` says why it writes nothing: the contract migration is already in the chain, or no migration grants the app role on schema `auth`.
- Changed: the lite sign-in renders `@starci/auth` 0.2.1: `identity_refresh_tokens.raced_count` in the baseline (the package's `identity-sessions.sql`), the refresh-token entity's `racedCount` and the session store's `markTokenRaced`. An app whose baseline holds the 0.2.0 tables adds `@starci/auth/migrations/identity-refresh-raced-successors.sql` as a migration.
- Changed: the canon pins name `@starci/auth` 0.2.1, `@starci/auth-oidc` 0.1.1 and the Grammar family 0.21.16.
- Upgrade, for an app on the CLI 1.1.0-alpha.49 / hfs 5.0.8 render moving to this version: (1) `npx starci app codemod module-definition --execute`, then `npm install`; (2) `npx starci app sync --execute`, which writes everything that compiles and holds the auth-lite sign-in glue while the app's own identity code differs, naming each missing or still-imported symbol; (3) adopt `@starci/mail` and make the identity domain provide what the held errors name; (4) `npx starci app sync --execute` again, which writes the glue once it compiles; (5) `npx starci app codemod account-doors --execute`, and `--contract` when the app still grants on schema `auth`; (6) add the `raced_count` migration above.

## 5.3.0 - 2026-11-03

- Fixed: the secrets family of the committed check cache stores codes, paths, positions and digests only (`cache/secret-records.mts`, `runtime/scripts/lib/secret-records.mts`), and the one secret judgement of R06 judges a file of `.starcistacks/cache/check/secrets/` by that contract, so `starci app check` can no longer write a cache the staged guard refuses (`HFS_PLAINTEXT_SECRET` for the `secrets/` folder name). A cache written before reads back as digests. Codemod: `starci app codemod secrets-cache`.
- Added: `@starci/nest-kit` in the starter dependencies and in every module-definition template: a module definition is one call (`defineConfigurableModule<XOptions>()`, `defineCapabilityModule<XOptions>()` for a capability's representative module). The lint on stated export types (R368) accepts a destructured export of those two calls and no other; the digest of the file carries the call, so a change of the options type reaches the importers; `capability-module-shape` (R45) accepts the helper and `module-per-transport` confines it to the definition file. Codemod: `starci app codemod module-definition` rewrites the destructured and the typed builder forms and adds the dependency.
- Fixed: `HFS_MONO_WORKSPACES` asks for `packages/*` after the fe workspaces once the app tracks a root package (slot `app.packages`, the module packages), so `starci app sync` and `starci app check` keep the entry `app add module` writes; the managed `test` and `build:be` scripts build the module packages first and the CI fast job runs `npm run test:modules` (PROJECT-3, BE-TEST-13).
- Added: `starci app stack up` builds the harness runtime layer an app image stands on (`openclaw-runtime`) from the recipe this package ships (`stack/images/openclaw-runtime`, kept byte-equal to the harness `images/openclaw-runtime` by `npm run bundles`) when the host neither holds nor can pull it, as a governed heavy build.
- Fixed: a secret member no creation rule of `.sops.yaml` reaches is refused `SECRET_SOPS_RULE_MISSING` before sops runs (a rule written for the legacy `.enc` spelling does not reach a `.enc.yaml` member), naming the path and `starci app secret init --env <env>`. Codemod: `starci app codemod sops-rules` migrates the legacy rule to the managed one (public recipients only).
- Fixed: `starci app secret set` on a tooling member (`sonar.enc.yaml`, any member a service credential's custody path names) no longer declares it in `environments.<env>.secrets` (which made CI custody refuse the analysis token); it binds the member to its service (`credentials` and `ci.secrets`, the shape the template renders) or prints the exact binding. Codemod: `starci app codemod quality-binding` binds an app scaffolded before and names a runtime entry an earlier set declared.
- Added: the lite starter depends on `@starci/mail` and renders the five email doors (BE `IdentityEmailService` and `identity_email_tokens`; FE `/auth/signup`, `/auth/resend`, `/auth/forgot`, `/auth/confirm`, `/auth/reset`, `EmailDoorForm`, the managed `modules/auth/email-doors.ts`), a Mailpit dev sink pinned by digest (`mail-sink.spec.mts`), and the stack links `@starci/mail` in the scaffold type-check.
- Fixed: the `theme-tokens` codemod drops an alias whose rename would name itself (`--starci-core-accent: var(--accent)` became `--accent: var(--accent)`, which is invalid at computed-value time and blanked every brand colour) and a rule block that held only such aliases, with its comment; R61 now lists the stylelint rule `no-self-reference` (`@starci/stylelint-canon` 2.6.0) among its enforcers.
- Changed: the scaffold type-check runs the back-end and front-end chains and both editions concurrently (the same checks, async spawns).
- Fixed: an app's root workspace packages (`packages/agent-sdk`, `packages/module-<name>`, the managed `packages/modules`) are first-party packages of the import graph. A side imports one by its package name through the `exports` of its manifest: that is no import that leaves the side (`ARCH_INTERNAL_IMPORT_OUTSIDE` stopped the front-end lint run on `@scope/agent-sdk/workbench`) and adds no edge; an import into the package's source past its entry points stays refused, with a message that lists the entry points. The packages are read from the app root's `workspaces` (patterns that start with no side folder) and from the slots `app.packages` and `app.module-registry` (`architecture/root-packages.mts`, `rootPackages` of the architecture config). Spec: `architecture-root-packages.spec.mts`.
- Added: the slots `app.packages`, `app.module-registry` and `app.packages-lint` in the runtime slot manifest (3.17.0; the first two were only in `contexts/hfs/slots.yaml`, so `packages/` classified as `HFS_SLOT_UNDECLARED`). `eslint.config.mjs` at the app root is the managed config of the root packages, no longer a forbidden tool config.
- Added: `starci app sync` renders `eslint.config.mjs` at the app root whenever the app has a root package: the back-end canon over the back-end subpaths of `packages/<pkg>/src/**`, the front-end canon and the Grammar family's `<family>GrammarPackagesConfig` over `src/workbench/**` and `src/public/**`. `starci app check --gate lint` runs it as a third scope (`packages`, from the app root, uncached) beside `be` and `fe`. The package view of the slot resolver (`openPackagesHfsView`, `declaredPackagesHfsView` in `view.mts`) is the profile of a side over the paths of the app root; a package file is of the tier `package` and has no owner. Spec: `lint-packages.spec.mts`.
- Changed: the lite sign-in and the email doors reach accounts through the account doors. The baseline migration includes `templates/app/auth-doors.sql.partial`, which is now the source of truth of the doors (it is no longer a byte copy of an app's own migration): eight SECURITY DEFINER functions `public.be_auth_user_by_email`, `_by_id`, `_by_google`, `be_auth_create_user`, `be_auth_link_google`, `be_auth_touch_user`, `be_auth_confirm_user(user, now)` (stamps the confirmation only, no sign-in record) and `be_auth_set_password(user, hash, confirm, now)` (sets the hash and, when `confirm` is true, confirms the mailbox), each with a pinned `search_path` and EXECUTE revoked from public, anon and authenticated and granted to `app_be` alone, plus a `lower(email)` index on `auth.users` the by-address door reads. `be_auth_set_password` is a four-argument function: an app that already has a three-argument one of its own keeps both. The three grants of `app_be` on schema `auth`, `auth.users` and `auth.identities` are gone from the baseline, so one injectable query no longer reaches every account's hash, role and metadata. The eight doors are in `ruleParams.be.dbFunctions`; an app lists none of them in hfs.json.
- Changed: the managed identity glue (slot `be.identity.sign-in`): `AuthUserEntity` and `AuthIdentityEntity` are deleted; the account directory, the authenticator and `IdentityEmailService` (signup insert, mailbox confirm, password reset) call the doors through the new managed `persistence/account-doors.port.ts`, which validates every answer (`returnedRows`, `rowShape`); the database decides `disabled` at its own now() (a banned account no longer receives a reset mail). The rate counter over `identity_attempts` and the fail-closed refusal mapping are unchanged.
- Added: `IdentitySignInService.endOthers(principal)` signs the caller out of every other device, and `Principal.sessionId` carries the device session of the access token. The authenticator logs an account created by Google sign-in as `identity.sign_in.account_created` (new managed `identity-sign-in.log-events.ts`) with the account id only. The lite starter carries `platform/database/database.client.ts` and exports `callDbFunction`.
- Changed: R255 (`DB_APP_ROLE`) also refuses any grant to the declared back-end role on schema `auth`, on a table or sequence in it, or by default privilege for it, with no transition tolerance. The migrations are judged in order, so a later migration that revokes a grant clears it. BE-AUTH-2, BE-PERSISTENCE-4 and BE-PERSISTENCE-17 follow.
- Added: `starci app codemod account-doors [--contract] [--execute]`, the upgrade of a lite app already on the template: it writes the expand migration `db/migrations/<ts>_account-doors.sql` with the doors the app lacks (a door is known by name and argument types, so an app that has the six earlier doors gets only the two new ones) and, with `--contract`, the migration `<ts>_contract_revoke-auth-grants.sql` that revokes the three grants from the declared role.
- Changed: `starci app sync --execute` deletes the retired `auth-user.entity.ts` and `auth-identity.entity.ts` of an app on the old glue.
- Upgrade, for a lite app scaffolded at 3.16.0 or later (the old glue grants `app_be` on `auth.users`): the new glue calls functions the app's database does not have until the expand migration is applied. Ship these three steps in one release, in this order; R255 reports the old grants until step 3 is in the chain, so they cannot ship apart.
  1. `npx starci app codemod account-doors --execute` (expand): writes the migration that creates the doors the app lacks and the index.
  2. `npx starci app sync --execute`: moves the glue onto the doors and deletes the two entity files; `npx starci app check` is green except R255 until step 3.
  3. `npx starci app codemod account-doors --contract --execute` (contract): writes the migration that revokes the old grants. It refuses while an account door is undefined or a back-end source still maps `schema: "auth"`.
  4. The deploy applies the expand migration before the services and the contract migration after the accept.
  5. Rollback: the release before this one still reads the auth tables, so a rollback to it after the accept must restore the grants by hand, as the database owner:
     ```sql
     grant usage on schema auth to app_be;
     grant select, insert, update on table auth.users to app_be;
     grant select, insert on table auth.identities to app_be;
     ```
     (use the role `db.appRole` of hfs.json when it is not app_be). Revoke them again with the contract migration when the rollback is undone.
  6. An app that already defines the six earlier doors (Nivo) runs step 1 too and gets only `be_auth_confirm_user` and the four-argument `be_auth_set_password`; its hfs.json `sides.be.dbFunctions` may drop the doors, which the canon now lists.

## 5.2.0 - 2026-11-03

- Added: a scaffolded app of each edition type-checks and builds on every side (`scaffold-type-check.spec.mts`: one install per edition, `tsc` of the back end and its specs, the back-end build, the workspace packages, `tsc` and `next build` of each front-end app). The templates follow the Grammar contract: `Link` and `Page` for `SiteShell` and `NotFoundNotice`, a callable `toast`, the session reducers as a type, explicit next-intl navigation generics, optional access to the test world's apps and connections, no unused import in the migrations fixture. The lite dev api process no longer runs `--transpile-only`.
- Added: the custody kind `ed25519-pem` of `local.custody` (the dev sign-in key is EdDSA); the lite dev environment names `AUTH_WEB_ORIGIN`.
- Added: `starci app add module <name>` and `starci app remove module <name>`: a module package `packages/module-<name>` of the product's agent SDK (`./template`, `./manifest`, `./workbench`, `./public`), registered in the root `workspaces` and the back-end and front-end dependencies, listed in the managed registry package `packages/modules` that `starci app sync` renders and drift-checks. Slots `app.packages` and `app.module-registry` (manifest 3.17.0). The module packages enter the Sonar sources, the Codecov paths and a `build:modules` / `test:modules` script pair that the typecheck, the lint and the build run first.
- Added: rule R374 (`STACKS_CODE_BIND_MOUNT`, `STACKS_IMAGE_BASE_UNPINNED`, `STACKS_IMAGE_BASE_NOT_FORK`, `STACKS_IMAGE_ENV_DIVERGES`): a customised service is an image built from `.starcistacks/images/<name>/` on the harness's pinned base, no bind-mounted code, the same image in every environment (PROJECT-STACKS-11).
- Changed: FE-PACKAGES-2 (a shared package, a module's workbench included, draws only with Grammar) and BE-TEST-13 (a module package's logic is inside the 100 per file scope).
- Added: the lite starter composes `@starci/auth`: it depends on the package and on none of the identity libraries, `integrations/passport` is the glue the slot lists (`passport.config.ts`, `passport.options.ts`, `passport.decorators.ts`, `passport.kit.ts`, `errors/`), the web app keeps one `@starci/auth/client` instance in `modules/auth/client.ts`, and the baseline migration holds the package's session tables (`identity_sessions`, `identity_refresh_tokens`) byte for byte.
- Changed: `infraOwners` own `@nestjs/passport`, `passport*`, `@nestjs/jwt`, `jose` and `bcryptjs` by nobody and `@starci/auth` by `integrations/passport` alone (R328); the `own-jwks-principal` machine refuses a `jwtVerify` call anywhere in the app and requires the `@starci/auth/server` import in the passport integration (R329); `transport-owner` no longer admits an auth door in `modules/auth`; the `auth` stack concern names `@starci/auth` and forbids `jose`, `jwt-decode`, `next-auth`, `@auth/core`, `keycloak-js` and `oidc-client-ts`.
- Changed: the lite stack templates name `AUTH_WEB_ORIGIN` and the Ed25519 signing key; `AUTH_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` is an EdDSA key and the public key is derived from it.
- Added: the lite starter signs in with no hand-written auth code. The managed slots `be.identity.sign-in`, `be.feature.auth` and `fe.modules.auth.client` render, from `templates/be/auth-lite` and `templates/fe/auth-lite`, the `/auth` doors (login, refresh, session, logout, Google start and callback, jwks), the entities of the session, attempt, user and identity tables, the session, account and rate stores, the authenticator, `IdentitySignInService`, their specs and the web app's `createAuthClient` client; an edit is `HFS_MANAGED_FILE_DRIFT`. The identity error family gains the sign-in codes, the passport family keeps its `PASSPORT_` codes.
- Added: the slot field `managedOnly` (a slot that only lists files sync renders; the slot owning the path judges them). Manifest 3.16.0.
- Changed: the sync and the scaffold count the back-end source files their own targets render, so the CI shard count and the coverage scope are the same before and after the targets are written (one sync leaves the app in sync).
- Added: the canon pin of `@starci/auth` 0.1.0.
- Added: `starci app stack prepare|doctor|up|status|logs|down|verify --env <env>` for a docker-compose (dev) environment, driven by the new `environments.<env>.local` block of application-stacks.yaml: ports, compose services and their health, the environment's own database role (the migrations run as it and the app role gets its own password), dev-only custody generators, least grants (`STACK_GRANT_<NAME>`), data directories (`STACK_DATA_DIR`, `${data}`), setup and seed commands (a seed runs after the migration or after readiness), native app processes, readiness and verification probes. `up --execute` is the one command (host table, custody, setup, services, database, migrate with a receipt, seed once, processes, readiness, origins); `prepare`, `up` and `down` are dry runs until `--execute`, `down` never removes a volume, `doctor` prints the owner-run install step per OS for each missing prerequisite and installs nothing. A swarm environment keeps the swarm verbs and refuses the dev-only ones.
- Added: `starci app secret init --env <dev env> [--execute]` creates the dev-only values `local.custody` declares (hex, base64, password, uuid, rsa-pem, ec-pem), once: a sealed value is kept, a half-present pair is refused, a vps or production environment is refused.
- Added: `db migrate` runs its client tools inside the database container named by `STARCI_DB_CONTAINER`, so a host without PostgreSQL client tools migrates a dev database.
- Added: rules R372 (`STACKS_LOCAL_CONFIG_INVALID`, `STACKS_DEV_UNDECLARED`: a coherent local block, and a lite app declares its dev environment) and R373 (`STACKS_RUNBOOK_DOCKER_COMMAND`: the dev runbook names `starci app stack up --env <env>` and carries no docker command).
- Changed: the lite skeleton's dev environment is `docker-compose` with a local block, a compose file and a runbook of the new verbs (it was a Swarm stack with illustrative digests); slot `app.starcistacks` admits `<env>/infra/compose/**` in lite; `add queue` in lite adds the Redis to the dev compose file and the queue settings to the dev processes.

## 5.1.0

- Added: `starci app codemod explicit-surface` writes the inferred type of every export the classifier counts as open (R368): return types, variable, parameter and property types, from the app's own TypeScript program. It prefers a named type the program exports, writes `ReactElement` for components and Next's `Metadata` for `generateMetadata`, adds merged type-only imports after a directive prologue, compiles the result beside the original and writes a file only when it gains no diagnostic, and reports (with a suggested fix) destructured exports and anonymous, computed, too long, `any`, unnameable and predicate types. Prettier of the app formats what is written.
- Added: `starci app codemod directive-first` moves a `"use client"` or `"use server"` below another statement, or in the form `("use client")`, to the first statement of its module (R369).
- Changed: the test-world declaration (R47, R89) is read in its typed form, `const world: TestWorldApi = defineTestWorld({ ... })` with `useTestWorld` and `useSandbox` exported typed from it, as well as in the destructured form; the template writes the typed form, and a config with neither is still refused.
- Added: `template-surface.spec.mts` holds every TypeScript template to R368 and R369 (the front-end units are judged through their generators).
- Changed: the scaffold templates write the type of every export (R368): Nest module definitions export each `ConfigurableModuleHost` member with its type, hooks name their result interface, async pages and layouts return `Promise<ReactElement>`, redux, zustand, next-intl and Apollo exports are typed, SQL texts are `SqlText`, entity and migration lists are `DatabaseConnectionOptions` members. A new app scaffolds clean under `explicit-export-surface`.
- Added: `runtime/scripts/lib/declaration-surface.mjs`, the one classifier of a TypeScript file's exported type surface (the isolated declaration emit and the exports it cannot write without inference, with the JSX-only component exemption), and `runtime/scripts/hfs/explicit-surface-rule.mjs`, the factory of the `explicit-export-surface` rule both lint canons build (R368). The app-check ESLint cache reads the same classifier for `open`, so the rule reports exactly the files the cache counts as open.
- Changed: the ESLint child of the cached lint hands its project-graph snapshot out. The list runner (`lint/eslint-list.cjs`, now also the runner of a whole-side run, as the list `.`) asks each installed canon for `projectGraphSnapshot` after the lint and writes the findings beside its results; `lintSide` writes the `graph` records from them and builds the graph itself only for a canon the child did not answer for. A whole-side lint no longer builds the project graph a second time in the CLI process.

## 5.0.8

- Removed: the `starci app lint` verb. `starci app check` is the one verb for app quality and carries what the lint verb carried: `--side be|fe` limits the lint gate to one side (reading and writing that side's cache records), `--out <file>` writes the merged Sonar Generic Issue Import document of the run (the findings of the selected gates under `starci-hfs`, then ESLint and stylelint), and `--fix` runs ESLint and stylelint with `--fix` before the check (with `--gate lint`). There is no `--changed` and no `--workspace`: the cache makes a whole-app check of an unchanged tree take seconds. The rendered `lint` and `lint:fix` scripts, the managed CI lint step and the release gate's Sonar row call `app check --gate lint`, and rule R13 requires that form.
- Changed: usage is printed only for a usage error (`UsageError`), not for a failed check.
- Fixed: the `small-renames` codemod keeps `size="control"` on a bar control instead of rewriting it to `lg`.
- Changed: the structure pass keeps its work in the committed cache. A side key carries the hash it took of each input, so a stable untracked input (generated code) no longer discards the record of a scoped step, and `CheckSession.unchanged(paths, captured?)` compares an input with that hash. The per-file content rules (plaintext secrets, bypass markers, soft file size) are kept by the file's hash, the cross-file finders by the read set they recorded, and `--gate secrets` reuses the same per-file records, so a warm run judges nothing and a run after an edit judges that file. The whole-program records of a side with untracked program inputs are kept.
- Changed: The app-check ESLint cache dirties a file only when the type surface it reads can have changed. Each file records the digest of its isolated declaration emit and whether that surface is open (inferred from other files); a body-only edit lints that file alone, a changed signature its direct importers, an inferred type one more level at a time. A file that only names the types of others (a re-export, an imported type in a signature) and a component that returns only JSX carry a change of those types, never an edit of their bodies. Records without a surface are read as changed.
- Fixed: A file edited while the ESLint of a side ran loses only its own cache records instead of the records of the whole side.
- Changed: The files of a diff lint go to ONE ESLint child through a job file read by a small runner (`lint/eslint-list.cjs`) on the ESLint API of the side's install, instead of several chunked command lines that each built the TypeScript program again. The heavy-node request carries the number of files it judges (`files`).
- Changed: The import graph of the app-check lint cache remembers the files and folders it probes while resolving imports, and the launcher takes the side's scope from the cache instead of enumerating it again; the graph of a large side builds in a sixth of the time.

Preserve cache identities, closure, flush behavior and coverage thresholds. Record source-proven native Page body layouts with their contextual constraints.

- Fixed: Run the package export proof's normal pack lifecycle in a private source copy, so prepack never rewrites emit modules that concurrent worker proofs import.

## 5.0.1 - 2026-11-02

- Breaking: replaced individual family flags and `app check --full` with `--gate <name>` and `--no-cache`.

## 4.17.1 - 2026-11-02

- Fixed: unit provider contracts recognize the real Nest `Optional()` injection decorator through direct, aliased and namespace imports; TypeScript optional parameters and unrelated decorator names still require their providers.
- Fixed: cross-file `label-in-grid` judges the nearest peer-card layout (`Grid kind="cards"`), matching the existing one-file rule. Dated week regions keep their day headings; labelled peer tiles still fail, with generic regression coverage for both semantic week layouts and nested layout ownership. Bundled R356 knowledge names the same owner.

## 4.17.0 - 2026-11-02

- Fixed: the architecture machine run on an app root that has its own tsconfig threw "Cannot destructure property 'lines'" out of `checkClones`: the app resolver has no rule parameters, so the clone threshold (`ruleParams.common.duplicateBlock`, shared by every profile) is read from a side. Spec `architecture-clones-app-root`.
- Changed: the bundled playbooks, rule catalog, failure codes and draw script name the one card (`SurfaceCard kind="list"`, `kind="accordion"`) and Page's header slot; no SurfaceListCard, SurfaceAccordionCard or SectionHeader remains in `runtime`.
- Removed: `NavigationFeatureNav` from the surface names and the bundled Core playbook (the component is gone; `TopBar` takes `subnavigation`).
- Changed: the bundled rule catalog words the laws of R348, R351, R352 and R355 for the one card (no SurfaceListCard, SectionHeader or PageHeader).
- Changed: the status owners of the scaffold follow the flag-union `DataSlot` (`isLoading`, `isError`, `isForbidden`, `isEmpty`; `items` only on the ready member; `isValidating` beside data) and gain `slotOfReads` and `useSlotOfReads`; `isSkeleton` is `isLoading` in every template.
- Added: the codemod `loading-states` (`starci app codemod loading-states [--execute] [--json]`) for the grammar loading law: `isSkeleton` becomes `isLoading` on every Grammar element (and on an `isSkeleton` key inside a Grammar attribute, such as a breadcrumb item), `loadingRows` and a bubble skeleton's `rows` go, and an object literal in a `DataSlot` position loses its `items: undefined` (`SLOT_LITERAL_FLAGS`: `{ isLoading: true }`, `{ isError: true }`, `{ isForbidden: true }`, or `{ isEmpty: true }`); reported for a person: `LOADING_NAME_CONFLICT`, `IS_SKELETON_KEY`, `LOADING_CONTENT`, `SLOT_ITEMS_ACCESS`, `SLOT_LITERAL`, `HAND_SKELETON`, `SWR_DEFAULT_ARRAY`, `PLACEHOLDER_OBJECT`, `VALIDATING_AS_LOADING` and `FOREIGN_SLOT_STATUS`.
- Added: `tiers.grammar` in `knowledge/hfs/slots.yaml` (leaf, branch, composite, frame: a tier imports nothing above it) and the machine check `FE_GRAMMAR_TIER_DIRECTION` / `FE_GRAMMAR_TIER_STAMP` over a component library (`src/core/<tier>/<Name>/`): an import that climbs, a type-only one and one through a shared module included, and a `data-tier` stamp that is not the folder. `checkComponentLayers` runs the layer order over a component tree no hfs slot judges.
- Added: the codemod `tier-binding` (`starci app codemod tier-binding [--execute] [--json]`) for the grammar's tier binding: a child that became a prop (`Tooltip` `trigger`, `Disclosure`, `DragItem` and `MessageBubble` `body`, `MediaFrame` `media`, `Field` `control`, `Quote` `text`) moves into it, and `TAKES_NO_CHILDREN`, `LABEL_NOT_TEXT`, `CLASS_NAME_REMOVED`, `TOOLTIP_DISABLED_TRIGGER` and `CHILD_NOT_TEXT` are reported for a person.
- Changed: the scaffold follows the binding. `SlotView` takes a `render` function (not `children`), the `branch` unit takes a `body` slot, and `SiteShell`, which hosts the page, is a frame (`components/frames/SiteShell`, `packages/<project>-ui/src/frames/SiteShell`).
- Added: the render graph of the architecture machine (`render-graph.mts`: which component draws which, and where each places `children` and slot props) and two checks built on it: `surface-nesting` (R356, `FE_SURFACE_NESTING`: a card in a card, in `Pane surface` or in an overlay; a `DataTable` in a card; a `PaneGrid` outside `Page chrome="panes"`; a `Section` or `Heading` holding one card; a labelled card repeated in a `Grid`) and `render-prop-across-server` (R361, `FE_RENDER_PROP_ACROSS_SERVER`).
- Added: catalog entries R356 to R359, R361 and R367; every rule of `rules.yaml` names a doc.
- Changed: `starci app codemod` usage is derived from `CODEMODS`, so a new codemod cannot be missing from it. The bundle manifests of eslint-canon-be and eslint-grammar-core take `knowledge/hfs/rules.yaml` from hfs (it was a hand-kept copy that drifted), and the new architecture files are listed.
- Removed: failure code `FE_BUTTON_NO_VARIANT`.

- Added: the codemod `page-navigation` (`starci app codemod page-navigation [--execute] [--json]`) for the grammar's page and navigation cuts: `PrimaryRailLayout` to `Grid kind="main-rail"` and `FormGrid` to `Grid kind="form"` (a `className` or a spread blocks the rewrite), and every use of `PageHeader`, `PageContainer`, `Breadcrumbs`, `FillViewport`, `Rail`, `NavigationFeatureNav`, `Subnav` and `BottomNav` is reported with a code and the way to `Page`, `Grid` or `TopBar`.
- Changed: `starci app add layout` draws `Grid kind="main-rail"` and `starci app add overlay --form` a `Grid kind="form"`; R323 to R325 law text follows (the page parts are not exports).

- Added: the codemod `section-frame` (`starci app codemod section-frame [--execute] [--json]`) for the sections of the grammar: `Region` with a `SectionHeader` first in it, and a `SectionHeader` leading a plain wrapper, become one `Section`; `Region label` becomes `Section title` (reported, since the heading is new); `CollectionItem`, `ItemActions` and `ToolbarActions` become fragments; `Text weight` becomes `isStrong`. Reported for a person: `List`, `ListItem`, `LeadingNumber`, `Label`, a head with an eyebrow, a context-intro or rank 1, the removed `Text` values, and a strong block `Text` that heads a group (`TEXT_HEADING_FAKE`).

- hfs: codemod `surface-card-kinds` (`starci app codemod surface-card-kinds`).
- Added: the `frames` component tier for apps (grammar audit 2026-11): slot `fe.components` layers are `blocks, frames, composites, branches, leaves`, the architecture machine and the world check know it, and `starci app add frame <name>` writes one. The grammar package folders are `leaf`, `branch`, `composite` and `frame`.
- Added: the codemod `small-renames` (`starci app codemod small-renames [--execute] [--json]`) for the grammar's small cuts: `ChatBubble` to `MessageBubble`, `StatusDot` with text and `ConnectionStatus` to `Badge isDot`, `CloseButton` to `IconButton intent="dismiss"`, `TextAction href` to `Link`, `SurfaceCopyGroup` to `Stack`, the removed `variant`, `size="control"`, `isError` and `OtpInput` props, and `FlowGraph layout="orbit"` to `BubbleMap`; what a person decides is reported, not rewritten.

## 4.16.4 - 2026-11-01

- Fixed: the unused-symbol value resolver no longer overflows the call stack on a recursive function that spreads its own parameter (`{ ...term, op }` in a `reset(term)` that maps itself over `term.all`): the typed-property lookup carries the resolver's visited set instead of starting a fresh one, so the cycle ends at the first repeated node (`starci app check` crashed with "Maximum call stack size exceeded" in the hfs structure step).
- Changed: the bundled canon pins name @starci/grammar 0.20.1, eslint-grammar-core and eslint-grammar-offset-pop 0.20.1, eslint-canon-be 3.11.5, eslint-canon-fe 9.8.4 and hfs 4.16.4.

## 4.16.3 - 2026-11-01

- Changed: the bundled canon pins name hfs 4.16.3, @starci/grammar 0.20.0, eslint-canon-be 3.11.4, eslint-canon-fe 9.8.3 and eslint-grammar-core and eslint-grammar-offset-pop 0.20.0 (RELEASE_VERSION_NOT_BUMPED: 4.16.2 is on npm).

## 4.16.2 - 2026-10-31

- Fixed: R248 `ENV_DUPLICATE` judges app env only. The operator's SSH member (`deploy.remote.sealed`) and the CI member `vps-ci` are separate identities by design and never reach an app container, so the SSH group keys (`SSH_GROUP`, now exported from `secret-standard`) held by exactly those two members of one environment are no duplicate; an app key in two members, or an SSH group key in any third member, stays red.
- Changed: the CI transport lives in the sealed `vps-ci` alone and GitHub keeps only `SOPS_AGE_KEY`: `stack-key-restore.yml` (both editions) opens the sealed member with the same `stack connect` step as the deploy instead of `VPS_*` environment secrets, and the starters no longer declare `VPS_SSH_KEY`, `VPS_KNOWN_HOSTS`, `VPS_HOST` and `VPS_USER` under `ci:`.
- Added: the ssh gate operation `identity-restore <recipient>` (stdin: the identity; writes only the fixed server identity path, once, for the matching recipient), so the restore workflow is admitted when the CI key is forced through `starci-ssh-gate`; `CI_MEMBER` moves to `sealed-member`.
- Fixed: R259 `DB_MIGRATION_DESTRUCTIVE` leaves out `SET NOT NULL` and a type change on a column (or table) the same migration created earlier, which touches no existing data, and an entity references a table only by the name its `@Entity`/`@Table` decorator spells: a column property of the same word (`queue`) is no reference to a dropped table `queue`; an entity that spells no name keeps the identifier match.
- Fixed: `HFS_SCHEDULE_UNDECLARED` judges unparsable SQL only when it calls `cron.schedule`, `cron.unschedule`, `cron.schedule_in_database` or `cron.alter_job`; a statement of another dialect (MySQL session setup) schedules nothing.
- Changed: the bundled canon pins name hfs 4.16.2, @starci/grammar 0.20.0, eslint-canon-be 3.11.4, eslint-canon-fe 9.8.3 and eslint-grammar-core and eslint-grammar-offset-pop 0.20.0.

## 4.13.1 - 2026-10-31

The open items of the delivery pipeline (contexts/project/cicd.md, "As built").

- Added: `starci-ssh-gate`, the forced command of the CI SSH key. The release store's remote operations are named scripts with arguments of a closed alphabet (`stack/ssh-gate.mts`); the gate admits `docker system dial-stdio` and `starci-op <name> <args>` and refuses anything else with exit 126, saying nothing about the request. `starci app stack ci-key --env <env>` prints the owner-run install steps, `--print-gate` the gate text and `--public-key <file> --print-authorized-keys` the line (`restrict,command="starci-ssh-gate",no-pty,no-port-forwarding,no-agent-forwarding,no-X11-forwarding,no-user-rc <key>`). A sealed `vps-ci` member that holds `VPS_GATE: "1"` makes `stack connect` export `STARCI_SSH_GATE=1`, and the deploy, rollback, accept and status verbs then send the operations through the gate; without it they run the same bodies under `sh -c`. Not admitted yet: the post-deploy tasks and the manager backup and test decrypt of a stack database.
- Added: `deploy.notify` (`webhook: {member, key}`, optional `on`), off unless declared: one JSON webhook message (outcome, environment, stack, commit, tag, release, attempt, CI run URL, failure code) when an attempt ends; the URL is a key of a sealed member, https only, never printed; a failing webhook is a line of the result and never fails the deploy.
- Added: the journal document of an attempt carries `contract`, the contract migrations it applied, and the release store reads the journal (`journals()`). `stack rollback` refuses `STACK_ROLLBACK_CONTRACT` (dry run too) for a release older than the previous release of an attempt that applied a contract migration, or that the journal never saw once any is recorded, unless `--data-acknowledged`.
- Changed: the bundled canon pins name hfs 4.13.1 and the lint cohort eslint-canon-be 3.9.4, eslint-canon-fe 9.6.4, eslint-grammar-core and eslint-grammar-offset-pop 0.16.4 (their copies of the pins moved with them).
- Changed: the release store's lock, pointer, list, read, store, accept and journal commands are the named operations above; a releases base may not contain `..` anywhere.

## 4.13.0 - 2026-10-31

The delivery pipeline of an app (contexts/project/cicd.md; every green release tag deploys by itself, STARCI_DEPLOY_PAUSED is the brake, agents deploy, the CI deploy key is sealed, pull requests get CI, contract migrations run after the accept, Sonar judges every full run, images are cached in GitHub Actions and built for amd64 unless declared).

- Added: `starci app stack status --env <env>` (read-only) compares the live services of the stack with the accepted release and reports drift per service (`rolled back by swarm`, `updated outside a release`, `missing`, `not in the release`); `stack verify` fails on the same drift when the stack file pins its images by digest.
- Added: every executing `stack deploy`, `rollback` and `accept` is one recorded attempt: it takes `/srv/<stack>/deploy.lock` on the manager (shared with the urgent deploy, a bounded wait, then `STACK_DEPLOY_LOCKED`), writes `deploys/<attempt>.json` at every phase boundary plus the append-only `deploys.jsonl`, and snapshots the live services after converge, after a rollback and on accept (`release.json` gains `observed`).
- Added: `stack deploy --ci-run <id>` marks the attempt as a CI one and `--redeploy` deploys although the accepted release already runs the commit; without it a deploy of the commit the accepted release already runs, with matching services, ends green as `already-current`.
- Changed: preflight pulls the images of the rollback target too and refuses (`STACK_DEPLOY_ROLLBACK_PULL`) before the services are touched when one no longer pulls.

- Added: every deploy of an app that declares the `database:` block of application-stacks.yaml runs the database phases of `stack/db-phases.mts`. Before the stack deploy: `db-preview` (migrate preview of every pending migration; red refuses `STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_PREVIEW_RED`), `backup` only when something is pending (a fresh dump made on the manager from the database container, age-encrypted to the `.sops.yaml` recipients and the server recipient, kept in `<deploy.backup.dir | /var/backups/<stack>>/db`, receipt written beside it and judged against a second reading of the archive, test decrypt on the manager with its own identity; codes `STACK_DEPLOY_BACKUP_FAILED`, `_UNVERIFIED`, `_DECRYPT_FAILED`; nothing pending means no backup and `data.migrations.backup` is null) and `migrate` (the expand migrations: every migration but `*_contract_*`; `STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_FAILED`). Any refusal leaves the services untouched. After accept (in `stack deploy` and in `stack accept`): the `contract` pass applies the `*_contract_*` migrations; a red contract leaves the release accepted and the job red (`data.contractFailed`). A release that only carries a contract migration still gets its backup first. With no accepted release the deploy chooses the first start by itself (hold deploy, proof the database is empty, every migration inside the container, then the real deploy; `STACK_FIRST_START_NOT_EMPTY` refuses a database that holds data). `data.migrations` records pending files, backup receipt, applied versions and contract versions.
- Added: `database.target` (`stack`, default, or `external`) and `database.service` (default `db`) in the `database:` block. A `stack` database is reached inside its own container with the credentials that container holds (`ownContainerTarget`: role and database from the container, password read inside it from `POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE`), so a deploy holds no `DATABASE_URL` and no password; `stack deploy --db-url-env` is refused for it (`STACK_DEPLOY_DB_URL_UNEXPECTED`). An `external` database keeps `--db-url-env` (also on `stack accept`) and dumps where the deploy runs (`--backup-out`).
- Added: `db migrate` passes (`phase: all | expand | contract`) and `pushFiles` in its result data; `isContractMigration`.
- Added: `DB_MIGRATION_DESTRUCTIVE` (R259) also judges a contract migration against the previous release: a contract migration that is new since the previous release tag (the newest `v*` tag HEAD contains, not on HEAD itself) and destroys an object that the be/src sources of that tag still reference is a hard blocker, because the deploy applies it right after the accept while that release is the rollback target. The code change goes in one release and its contract migration in the next. The check is git-based: no release tag, or an unreadable history, means no previous release and no finding.
- Changed: the urgent floor shares these steps and their codes. `STACK_URGENT_BACKUP_FAILED`, `_BACKUP_UNVERIFIED`, `_DECRYPT_UNAVAILABLE`, `_DECRYPT_FAILED`, `_MIGRATE_PREVIEW_RED` and `_MIGRATE_FAILED` are now `STACK_DEPLOY_BACKUP_FAILED`, `STACK_DEPLOY_BACKUP_UNVERIFIED`, `STACK_DEPLOY_BACKUP_DECRYPT_UNAVAILABLE`, `STACK_DEPLOY_BACKUP_DECRYPT_FAILED`, `STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_PREVIEW_RED` and `STACK_DEPLOY_MIGRATE_FAILED`. The urgent path keeps its operator-held `--db-url-env` and its always-on backup. `stack deploy` accepts `--db-url-env` and `--backup-out` without `--urgent` (for `database.target: external`). An app without a `database:` block deploys exactly as before.
- Added: the urgent tag annotation carries the trailers `Starci-Urgent: <role>` and `Starci-Urgent-Reason: <text>` (`urgentTrailerBlock`, `parseUrgentTrailers` in release/records.mts; `release push --urgent` writes them), and the urgent record gains `trailers`. `stack deploy --ci-run <id>` is judged by the CLI (the jobs the CI standard requires, read from GitHub for the exact `--sha`); hfs takes the id only as the journal label of the attempt, after the CLI has accepted the run.
- Added: the managed workflows render the pipeline. `ci.yml` runs on `pull_request` to main as well (read-only, no environment, no secret), and holds `plan` (full, defer or reuse from the event, the ref and a green candidate run of the same commit), the checks, `images` (the reusable images.yml, built once beside the checks), `verdict` (needs every job, runs always, the single required check) and one `deploy-<env>` call of the reusable stack-deploy.yml per environment with `deploy.auto`, in declared order, after the verdict. Sonar and the Codecov upload run on every full run (a release tag or a candidate), not on tags only.
- Added: `images.yml` is a reusable workflow (`workflow_call`: `push`, `release`; output `refs`). It builds each declared app with buildx, the GitHub Actions cache (or `ci.imageCache: registry`) and an SBOM plus provenance (`mode=max`), pushes the deployed ones to GHCR under the immutable `sha-<sha>`, adds `v<version>` on a release run by retagging that digest (no rebuild), and collects the pushed digests as `{service: repository@sha256:...}`. An image whose public build values live in a sealed member is built by a second job bound to the release environment on a release tag run only; every other image builds with no environment and no secret. Platforms default to `linux/amd64` (`deploy.platforms`; `linux/arm64` adds QEMU).
- Added: `stack-deploy.yml` is reusable and still dispatchable (`deploy`, `redeploy`, `rollback`). The automatic deploy reads the variable `STARCI_DEPLOY_PAUSED` of its GitHub Environment first and stops without a code change when it is `true` (a manual run ignores it), resolves each image to a digest (the calling run's, else the registry's `sha-<sha>` tag), finds the green CI run of the commit and calls `stack deploy --sha <sha> --ci-run <run>`. Its only secret is the environment's `SOPS_AGE_KEY`.
- Added: the CI transport is the sealed member `vps-ci` of the environment (`VPS_SSH_KEY`, `VPS_KNOWN_HOSTS`, `VPS_HOST`, `VPS_USER`), opened with the server identity: `stack connect` prefers it over the job's `VPS_*` variables (which still work when the member is absent), pins the host keys with `StrictHostKeyChecking yes` and masks the host, the account and `deploy.remote.releasesBase` in the log of a public repository first.
- Added: stacks schema keys `environments.<env>.deploy.auto` (default false), `deploy.approval` (`none` | `reviewers`) and `deploy.platforms` (default `[linux/amd64]`), and `ci.imageCache` (`gha` | `registry`).
- Changed: R221 admits `workflow_call` and, in ci.yml only, `pull_request` to main (`branches: ['main']` and nothing else); `pull_request_target`, `workflow_run` and schedules stay refused. R251 judges a deployment caller (a job of ci.yml that calls the reusable stack-deploy.yml: permissions, concurrency and `with.environment`, no environment of its own), a workflow that runs on `pull_request` (read-only permissions, no job bound to an environment), recognises `starci app stack deploy|rollback|accept` as deployment commands, and judges the secrets allowlist and the dotenv ban on the deployment jobs rather than on the check jobs beside them (the Sonar token of ci.yml). R271 accepts the immutable `sha-${{ github.sha }}` tag from any ref and a release retag (`imagetools create`) only after a step under `if: ${{ inputs.release }}` that rejects a ref other than `refs/tags/v*`. R269 counts a job that the plan (`needs.plan.outputs.<x> == 'true'`) or a boolean input decides as enabled. R296 to R298 treat `vars.STARCI_DEPLOY_PAUSED` as a managed variable no app declares. The CI standard (R301) lists the jobs `plan`, `images` and `verdict` as required, the `deploy-<env>` calls after the verdict, and four new items (`pull-request-ci`, `images-once`, `deploy-brake`, the candidate trigger with pull requests).
- Changed: R259 (`DB_MIGRATION_DESTRUCTIVE`) states the previous-release rule of the contract migration; `HFS_DB_DEPLOY_UNGUARDED` (R271) still judges a full app's own `db-deploy.yml` and a lite app that has not synced yet, with the pointer to the stack deploy.
- Removed: the lite `db-deploy.yml` workflow. A lite app migrates in the migrate phase of its stack deploy (a verified backup, the expand migrations before the services, the contract migrations after the accept); `starci app sync --execute` deletes the file and the slot `app.ci-db` is forbidden in lite. The old tarball `images.yml` (never pushed) and the build-and-push step list of `stack-deploy.yml` are gone: the deployed digest is now the attested one the checks ran beside.

- Added: the committed app check cache (contexts/project/check-cache.md). `starci app check` runs every family diff-only against `.starcistacks/cache/` and writes what it judged back; `--full` runs the selected families in full; the selectors `--hfs --format --db --secrets --wiring --types --lint --test --sonar` run one family each and combine. Each family prints one diagnosis line (full or diff and why, checked, reused, findings new, known, fixed); progress goes to stderr with a percentage (whole lines off a terminal, `--quiet` silences it); timings go to the untracked tool cache. `STARCI_APP_CHECK_CACHE=verify` runs diff then full and fails with exit 2 on any difference; `=full` never reads the cache (the release gate sets it on every gate row). New `cache/` modules: types, hashing (CRLF to LF), JSON Lines, fingerprint, content index, trust (host: the working tree; CI: the cache at the merge-base or first parent), session, progress, run log, the hfs, secrets, db, wiring and format families, an in-memory fake session.
- Added: slot `app.starcistacks.cache` (slots 3.15.0, both editions); rule R343 with HFS_CHECK_CACHE_FORGED (hard blocker at any count), HFS_CHECK_CACHE_STALE and HFS_CHECK_CACHE_MISSING (warnings); the managed `.gitignore` re-includes the folder, `.gitattributes` marks it `merge=union linguist-generated`, the managed CI runs `app check --format` and sets the cache env to full on release candidates and tags, `db:lint` runs `app check --db`.
- Changed: Prettier (HFS_FORMAT) runs in batches of 64 files spread over worker threads that load the repository's prettier once, with configuration resolved once per directory (it was one lookup per file).
- Removed: `app check --fast`, `--base` and `--force`; `--wiring` is now the wiring family.

- Changed: the bundled canon pins name the 0.16.3 cohort (@starci/grammar, eslint-grammar-core and eslint-grammar-offset-pop 0.16.3, eslint-canon-fe 9.6.3, eslint-canon-be 3.9.3, stylelint-canon 2.3.0, jest-preset 2.6.0, hfs 4.13.0); ChatWorkspace and PaneLayout are removed with no alias; PaneGrid and Pane are the one generic pane grid: grammar names are generic. R341 FE_FREESTYLE_REASON_MISSING (eslint-grammar-core `freestyle-has-reason`) joins the bundled rule catalog.
- Fixed: two migrations written within one second (an app scaffold followed at once by `add table`, `add webhook`, `add queue`) took the same UTC timestamp and the second was refused with HFS_ADD_MIGRATION_ORDER, which failed the CI package job on a fast runner; the stamp now moves to the second after the latest migration when the latest is at most 5 s ahead of the clock (scaffold/migration-stamp), and a clock that is really behind is still refused. Specs: src/migration-stamp.spec.mts.
- Added: R342 `BE_ENTITY_EXTENDS_BASE` (eslint-canon-be `entity-extends-base`) joins the bundled rule catalog and the failure codes.
- Fixed: HFS_UNUSED_SYMBOL (R280) no longer reports a catalog key read through a translator helper. A hook that exposes its bound translators as members of a declared object type (`useMemo<OfficeWords>(() => ({ t: (key, values) => t(key, values) }))`) binds the app's own type alias of that member (`OfficeText`), or the member itself, to the translator's namespaces; a value of that type called with a key (`text("composerLabel")`, `props.text(...)`, `view.text(...)`, handed on through props, spreads and render callbacks) reads it in those namespaces. The binding is additive: a literal handed to a callback of another type, or a bare literal, still reads nothing.
- Fixed: the be skeleton's canon copy `platform/config/env-source.config.ts` is Prettier-formatted with @starci/prettier-config (the `optionalDomain` guard is wrapped), so an app that restores the canon bytes passes its own pre-commit format; HFS_CANON_COPY_DRIFT and HFS_FORMAT no longer contradict each other. A spec keeps every canon-copy template Prettier-stable.

## 4.12.2 - 2026-10-31

- Changed: the bundled canon pins name @starci/grammar 0.16.2 (the horizontal Stepper connector line; SurfaceListCard takes its depth from the surface context). R340 FE_RAISED_SURFACE_IN_SURFACE joins the bundled rule catalog.

## 4.12.1 - 2026-10-30

- Changed: the bundled canon pins name @starci/grammar 0.16.1 (accentCorner, Backdrop, the shell filling the viewport).

## 4.12.0 - 2026-10-30

- Changed: the package is strict TypeScript. Every source is a .mts compiled in place to .mjs and .d.mts at the path it always shipped at, so every import specifier and the exports map answer as before (src/package-exports.spec.mjs proves each one from the packed tarball). The specs, their fixtures and helpers stay .mjs and ran unchanged against the TypeScript build.
- Added: the platform database entity bases of the skeleton, AbstractEntity (created_at, updated_at, set by the database) and UuidAbstractEntity (the uuid id), written by the first `app add table`, whose entity extends UuidAbstractEntity; R86 sql-owner reads inherited columns.
- Fixed: a first start (`stack deploy --urgent --first-start`) deploys the stack with every service but the database at 0 replicas, waits for the database, proves it empty and migrates, then deploys the real stack: the app services no longer start against an empty schema and roll back.
- Fixed: the skeleton's database connection retries 10 times 3 s apart, so an app outlives its database's own start.
- Added: R337 HFS_AUTH_COOKIE_DOMAIN_MISSING: when a lite app's web and api are served on different hosts, the api's environment names AUTH_COOKIE_DOMAIN, a parent of both; the skeleton passport sets that Domain on its cookies when it is configured.
- Changed: the skeleton CLI apps log info to stderr (LoggingModule `info: "stderr"`), so a command's stdout carries only its output; the skeleton's stateless services roll back start-first.
- Fixed: the CLI audit's behaviour bugs: flags of another action are refused, a value flag cannot swallow the next flag, `--env` is required for stack effects, the generated USAGE lists every parser flag, `npm ci` is the scaffold hint, failure-code hints name live verbs.

- Upgrade note: right after upgrading, run `npx --no-install starci app sync --execute` in the app. The app's managed hooks and scripts still call `starci app hygiene` and `starci app sync --check`, which no longer exist, so the pre-commit hook fails until sync renders the new ones (and every managed file header now names `sync --execute`, so each managed file drifts once).
- Changed: the app verbs take the canonical spellings of contexts/harness/cli-canonical.md; the old spellings are unknown verbs and flags:
  - `app sync` is a dry run by default (exit 1 on drift) and writes with `--execute`; the Sonar scope block of `sonar-project.properties` is one of its managed files.
  - `app check --staged` is the pre-commit guard (staged `.starciwork`/`.starcistacks` paths, the secrets guard over the index, a new back-end logic file without its spec).
  - `app add service <dir> <name> [--inject ...]`, `app add spec <file>.service.ts` and `app add image` generate what the former `new` did; a flag a noun does not take is refused.
  - `app slot <path>` names the slot that owns a path; `app world up|down|status` runs the full edition's test world (`npm run test:world`); `app stack` is the Swarm deploy tooling alone.
  - `app scaffold <name>` creates `<--cwd>/<name>`; `app upgrade` is a dry run unless `--execute`.
  - `app lint --json` and `--out <sonar file>`; `app codemod --execute`, `--json` and `--out <file>`.
  - `app stack prepare` without `--execute` is the read-only host check; `--sha` names the commit on every stack action and on `release gate`, which adds the Sonar step whenever `services.sonar` is declared.
  - `app secret <action> --env <env>` on every action but `init`; `secret recipients --add/--remove`, `ci secret set` and `ci var set` are dry runs until `--execute`; `ci local` is a dry run unless `--execute`.
  - `app db migrate` without `--execute` prints the read-only plan with exit 0 (in step), 1 (drift) or 2 (unreadable); `--db-url-env <NAME>` on every db action; `db baseline` and `db restamp` act with `--execute`.
- Fixed: the first start of the urgent floor (`stack deploy --urgent --first-start --db-service <service>`) no longer starts the app services against an empty schema (their healthchecks failed and `failure_action: rollback` rolled every first start back at converge). The stack is first deployed with every service but the database at 0 replicas (a compose override merged by `docker stack deploy -c`, `stack.first-start-hold.yaml` beside the stack; a global-mode service is held off every node by an unsatisfiable placement constraint), then the `first-start` step waits for the database, proves it empty and migrates, then the real stack deploys and converges, verifies and rolls back as before. New step `deploy-hold`; a `--db-service` that is not a service of the stack is refused with `STACK_FIRST_START_USAGE`. A deploy without `--first-start` is unchanged.

## 4.11.22 - 2026-10-30

Every finding is still printed and kept in the report (`gate` carries the verdict); typecheck, build and the tests are not judged by it.
